HISTORY
ANCIENT INDIAN HISTORY


SOURCES OF ANCIENT INDIAN HISTORY

1.  Which of the following is NOT primarily considered a Literary Source for Ancient Indian History?
    a) Vedic Literature
    b) Puranic Literature
    c) Epigraphic Remains
    d) Foreign Accounts
    Answer: c) Epigraphic Remains

2.  The study of the development of scripts is known as:
    a) Epigraphy
    b) Numismatics
    c) Palaeography
    d) Archaeology
    Answer: c) Palaeography

3.  The study of inscriptions is called:
    a) Numismatics
    b) Epigraphy
    c) Palaeography
    d) Archaeology
    Answer: b) Epigraphy

4.  Which specialized study focuses on coins?
    a) Archaeology
    b) Epigraphy
    c) Palaeography
    d) Numismatics
    Answer: d) Numismatics

5.  The study of monuments and material remains is termed:
    a) Epigraphy
    b) Numismatics
    c) Palaeography
    d) Archaeology
    Answer: d) Archaeology

6.  How many main Puranas are mentioned in the text?
    a) 12
    b) 16
    c) 18
    d) 20
    Answer: c) 18

7.  Which two great epics are mentioned as sources for ancient Indian history, despite constant interpolations?
    a) Ramayana and Bhagavad Gita
    b) Mahabharata and Vedas
    c) Ramayana and Mahabharata
    d) Puranas and Upanishads
    Answer: c) Ramayana and Mahabharata

8.  According to the text, what can be reliably glimpsed from the Four Vedas?
    a) Detailed political history
    b) Accounts of royal coronations
    c) Culture and civilization of the Vedic period
    d) Precise measurements for sacrificial altars
    Answer: c) Culture and civilization of the Vedic period

9.  The "Sutra" form of expression is described as:
    a) Poetic and rhythmic
    b) Precise and exact prose
    c) Primarily narrative history
    d) Long philosophical discourses
    Answer: b) Precise and exact prose

10. Which part of Later Vedic Literature elaborates on Vedic rituals?
    a) Aranyakas
    b) Upanishads
    c) Brahmanas
    d) Sulvasutra
    Answer: c) Brahmanas

11. Discourses on different spiritual and philosophical problems are given in which Later Vedic Literature?
    a) Srautasutra
    b) Grihyasutra
    c) Brahmanas
    d) Aranyakas
    Answer: d) Aranyakas

12. Which text prescribes measurements for sacrificial altars and marks the beginning of the study of geometry and maths?
    a) Upanishads
    b) Sulvasutra
    c) Grihyasutra
    d) Srautasutra
    Answer: b) Sulvasutra

13. Accounts of royal coronation ceremonies are found in which Sutra literature?
    a) Grihyasutra
    b) Sulvasutra
    c) Srautasutra
    d) Dharmasutras (not mentioned but option to check comprehension)
    Answer: c) Srautasutra

14. Domestic rituals related to birth, naming, marriage, funeral, etc., are covered in which text?
    a) Sulvasutra
    b) Srautasutra
    c) Grihyasutra
    d) Aranyakas
    Answer: c) Grihyasutra

15. Jain literature was primarily written in which language?
    a) Pali
    b) Sanskrit
    c) Jain Prakrit
    d) Magadhi
    Answer: c) Jain Prakrit

16. Buddhist Pali was a form of Prakrit language prevalent in which region?
    a) Gandhara
    b) Malwa
    c) Magadha/South Bihar
    d) Kalinga
    Answer: c) Magadha/South Bihar

17. Ashokan edicts are primarily found in which language?
    a) Sanskrit
    b) Pali
    c) Greek
    d) Kharosthi
    Answer: b) Pali

18. Kautilya's Arthashastra is a book on:
    a) Ancient Indian medicine
    b) Statecraft
    c) Vedic rituals
    d) Astronomical observations
    Answer: b) Statecraft

19. In which period was Kautilya's Arthashastra written?
    a) Gupta period
    b) Pre-Mauryan period
    c) Maurya period
    d) Post-Maurya period
    Answer: c) Maurya period

20. How many chapters (books) is Kautilya's Arthashastra divided into?
    a) 10
    b) 12
    c) 15
    d) 20
    Answer: c) 15

21. The play Mudrarakshusha, which gives a glimpse of society and culture, was written by:
    a) Kalidasa
    b) Banabhatta
    c) Vishakhadatta
    d) Sudraka
    Answer: c) Vishakhadatta

22. Kalidasa's Malavikagnimitram is based on events from the reign of which dynasty?
    a) Mauryas
    b) Guptas
    c) Sungas (Pusyamitra Sunga)
    d) Chalukyas
    Answer: c) Sungas (Pusyamitra Sunga)

23. Which play by Kalidasa gives a glimpse of the Guptas?
    a) Malavikagnimitram
    b) Mudrarakshusha
    c) Abhgyanshakuntalam
    d) Mricchakatika (not mentioned but option to check comprehension)
    Answer: c) Abhgyanshakuntalam

24. Harshacharita, which throws light on many historical facts, was written by:
    a) Vakpati
    b) Bilhana
    c) Banabhatta
    d) Kalhana
    Answer: c) Banabhatta

25. Gaudauaho, based on the exploits of Yasovarman of Kanauj, was written by:
    a) Banabhatta
    b) Vakpati
    c) Bilhana
    d) Kalhana
    Answer: b) Vakpati

26. Bilhana's Vikramankadevachnrita describes the victories of which king?
    a) Harshavardhana
    b) Chandragupta Maurya
    c) Vikramaditya (later Chalukya king)
    d) Yasovarman
    Answer: c) Vikramaditya (later Chalukya king)

27. Kalhana's book Rajatarangini is a significant historical text. What is it primarily known for?
    a) Accounts of Vedic rituals
    b) A chronicle of kings (especially Kashmir)
    c) Statecraft principles
    d) Description of foreign trade routes
    Answer: b) A chronicle of kings (especially Kashmir)

28. Which Greek ambassador was in the court of Chandragupta Maurya?
    a) Deimachus
    b) Dionysios
    c) Megasthenese
    d) Arrian (not mentioned but option to check comprehension)
    Answer: c) Megasthenese

29. Megasthenese's book, Indika, is:
    a) Fully available to us
    b) Partially available in fragments
    c) A detailed account of Gupta administration
    d) Primarily a religious text
    Answer: b) Partially available in fragments

30. What kind of information do the fragments of Megasthenese's Indika provide?
    a) Vedic sacrificial procedures
    b) Maurya Administration, social classes and economic activities
    c) Gupta period trade routes
    d) Puranic genealogies
    Answer: b) Maurya Administration, social classes and economic activities

31. Chinese travelers visited India primarily as:
    a) Merchants
    b) Ambassadors
    c) Buddhist pilgrims
    d) Spies
    Answer: c) Buddhist pilgrims

32. Fa-Hien visited India in which century A.D.?
    a) 4th
    b) 5th
    c) 6th
    d) 7th
    Answer: b) 5th

33. Fa-Hien's accounts describe social-religious and economic conditions during the time of which dynasty?
    a) Mauryas
    b) Guptas
    c) Sungas
    d) Harshavardhana
    Answer: b) Guptas

34. Hiuen-Tsang visited India in which century?
    a) 5th Century
    b) 6th Century
    c) 7th Century
    d) 8th Century
    Answer: c) 7th Century

35. Hiuen-Tsang's accounts provide information about the age of which prominent ruler?
    a) Chandragupta Maurya
    b) Samudragupta
    c) Harshavardhana
    d) Vikramaditya
    Answer: c) Harshavardhana

36. I-tsing visited India in which century?
    a) 5th Century
    b) 6th Century
    c) 7th Century
    d) 8th Century
    Answer: c) 7th Century

37. Al-Beruni was a contemporary of which historical figure?
    a) Chandragupta Maurya
    b) Harshavardhana
    c) Mahmud of Ghazni
    d) Akbar (not mentioned but option to check comprehension)
    Answer: c) Mahmud of Ghazni

38. In which year was Al-Beruni born?
    a) A.D. 950
    b) A.D. 973
    c) A.D. 1000
    d) A.D. 1048
    Answer: b) A.D. 973

39. Where did Al-Beruni die?
    a) Central Asia
    b) Pataliputra
    c) Ghazni (present-day Afghanistan)
    d) India
    Answer: c) Ghazni (present-day Afghanistan)

40. What are "Megaliths" primarily associated with in South India?
    a) Temples
    b) Fortifications
    c) Graves encircled by large stones
    d) Residential complexes
    Answer: c) Graves encircled by large stones

41. The earliest system of writings found in Harappan seals is described as:
    a) Brahmi script
    b) Pictographic script
    c) Devanagari script
    d) Sanskrit script
    Answer: b) Pictographic script

42. What is the main limitation of the writing system found in Harappan seals as a historical source?
    a) It is not well-preserved.
    b) It has been fully deciphered.
    c) There has been no success in deciphering it.
    d) It only describes religious rituals.
    Answer: c) There has been no success in deciphering it.

43. Which inscriptions are considered to be the earliest deciphered writing system (3rd AD)?
    a) Harappan seals
    b) Megalithic carvings
    c) Ashokan inscriptions (in Brahmi script)
    d) Gupta period inscriptions
    Answer: c) Ashokan inscriptions (in Brahmi script)

44. Which groups of kings could not have been known about without numismatic sources?
    a) Mauryan and Gupta kings
    b) Indo-Greek, Saka-Parthian and Kushana kings
    c) Vedic and Later Vedic rulers
    d) Chola and Pandya kings (not mentioned but option to check comprehension)
    Answer: b) Indo-Greek, Saka-Parthian and Kushana kings

45. The fact that some coins were issued by merchants and guilds indicates what about commerce in later Ancient India?
    a) It was in decline.
    b) It was strictly controlled by rulers.
    c) It had become important.
    d) It was non-existent.
    Answer: c) It had become important.

46. In which period were the largest number of coins found, suggesting an increase in trade?
    a) Maurya period
    b) Post-Maurya period
    c) Gupta period
    d) Vedic period
    Answer: b) Post-Maurya period

47. What is a characteristic feature of the earliest coins of India, known as Punch-Marked Coins?
    a) They bear detailed portraits of kings.
    b) They are made only of gold.
    c) They bear only symbols, punched separately.
    d) They have inscriptions in Brahmi script.
    Answer: c) They bear only symbols, punched separately.

48. Indo-Greek coins were primarily made of:
    a) Gold only
    b) Silver and copper, rarely gold
    c) Bronze only
    d) Iron
    Answer: b) Silver and copper, rarely gold

49. Which dynasty issued mostly gold coins and numerous copper coins found in most parts of North India up to Bihar, with Indian influence seen on them from the beginning?
    a) Mauryas
    b) Guptas
    c) Kushanas
    d) Indo-Greeks
    Answer: c) Kushanas

50. On Kushana coins, besides Persian and Greek deities, what else can be found depicted?
    a) Roman emperors
    b) Indian gods and goddesses
    c) Chinese symbols
    d) Buddhist stupas only
    Answer: b) Indian gods and goddesses

51. Which dynasty issued the largest number of gold coins?
    a) Kushanas
    b) Indo-Greeks
    c) Mauryas
    d) Guptas
    Answer: d) Guptas

52. The Guptas appear to have succeeded which dynasty in the tradition of minting coins?
    a) Mauryas
    b) Indo-Greeks
    c) Kushanas
    d) Satavahanas (not mentioned but option to check comprehension)
    Answer: c) Kushanas

53. How did the Guptas modify their coinage?
    a) They simplified the designs.
    b) They completely Indianised their coinage.
    c) They removed all deities.
    d) They stopped minting gold coins.
    Answer: b) They completely Indianised their coinage

54. On Gupta coins, kings are depicted engaged in activities such as:
    a) Writing scriptures
    b) Building temples
    c) Hunting a lion or rhinoceros, holding a bow or battle-axes, playing musical instrument or performing Ashwamedh yajna.
    d) Negotiating trade deals
    Answer: c) Hunting a lion or rhinoceros, holding a bow or battle-axes, playing musical instrument or performing Ashwamedh yajna.

55. Temples and sculptures are found all over the country from which period onwards?
    a) Harappan period
    b) Vedic period
    c) Gupta period
    d) Maurya period
    Answer: c) Gupta period

56. In Western India, large caves excavated in the hills are mostly identified as:
    a) Royal palaces
    b) Granaries
    c) Chaitya and viharas
    d) Fortifications
    Answer: c) Chaitya and viharas


THE EARLY VEDIC PERIOD (1500-1000 B.C.)

1.  The Early Vedic Period is also known as:
    a) Later Vedic Age
    b) Rig Vedic Age
    c) Iron Age
    d) Painted Grey Ware Age
    Answer: b) Rig Vedic Age

2.  From where are the Aryans believed to have come to India?
    a) East Asia
    b) Central Asia (Steppe region)
    c) Europe
    d) North Africa
    Answer: b) Central Asia (Steppe region)

3.  The earliest Aryans lived in the land of ‘Sapta Sindhava’, which refers to the land of how many rivers?
    a) Five
    b) Six
    c) Seven
    d) Eight
    Answer: c) Seven

4.  The early Vedic society was primarily:
    a) Agricultural
    b) Urban
    c) Pastoral
    d) Hunter-gatherer
    Answer: c) Pastoral

5.  In the Rig Vedic period, what was the chief measure of wealth?
    a) Gold
    b) Land
    c) Cattle
    d) Grains
    Answer: c) Cattle

6.  A wealthy man in the Rig Vedic period was called:
    a) Gopati
    b) Gomat
    c) Raja
    d) Purohita
    Answer: b) Gomat

7.  The Raja or chief in the Rig Vedic period was called:
    a) Gomat
    b) Ganapati
    c) Gopati
    d) Senani
    Answer: c) Gopati

8.  In the Rigveda, 'Godhuli' is used as a term for a measure of:
    a) Wealth
    b) Land
    c) Time
    d) Cattle
    Answer: c) Time

9.  Which grain is mentioned in the Rigveda apart from Yava?
    a) Wheat
    b) Rice
    c) None
    d) Barley (Yava is Barley)
    Answer: c) None

10. Who was considered the greatest God of the Aryans in the Rig Vedic period?
    a) Agni
    b) Varuna
    c) Prajapati
    d) Indra
    Answer: d) Indra

11. Which deity occupied the second position among the Rig Vedic Gods?
    a) Varuna
    b) Agni
    c) Indra
    d) Pushan
    Answer: b) Agni

12. Which Rig Vedic deity personified water and occupied the third position?
    a) Indra
    b) Agni
    c) Varuna
    d) Soma
    Answer: c) Varuna

13. The Battle of Ten Kings ended with the victory of the Bharatas led by:
    a) Divodasa
    b) Sudas
    c) Vishwamitra
    d) Puru
    Answer: b) Sudas

14. Which social practices prevailed in the Rig Vedic society according to the text?
    a) Sati and child marriage
    b) Widow marriage and Niyoga
    c) Polygamy only
    d) Strict monogamy
    Answer: b) Widow marriage and Niyoga

15. Which of the following was NOT an important functionary in the Rig Vedic period?
    a) Purohita
    b) Senani
    c) Gramini
    d) Shudra
    Answer: d) Shudra

16. Which of the following was an important tribal assembly in the Rig Vedic period?
    a) Varna
    b) Gotra
    c) Sabha
    d) Ashrama
    Answer: c) Sabha

17. Which tribal assembly performed judicial and administrative functions?
    a) Samiti
    b) Vidath
    c) Gana
    d) Sabha
    Answer: d) Sabha

18. In the Rigveda, how many times is 'Samiti' mentioned?
    a) Seven
    b) Eight
    c) Nine
    d) Ten
    Answer: c) Nine

19. Which assembly was presided over by the king, and where the king was elected?
    a) Sabha
    b) Vidath
    c) Gana
    d) Samiti
    Answer: d) Samiti

20. The term ‘Gana’ is mentioned for how many places in the Rigveda?
    a) 36
    b) 42
    c) 46
    d) 50
    Answer: c) 46

21. The leader of 'Gana' was called:
    a) Gopati
    b) Rajan
    c) Ganapati
    d) Senapati
    Answer: c) Ganapati

22. Who were the most bitter enemies of the Rigvedic people?
    a) Panis
    b) Dasyus
    c) Rakshasas
    d) Asuras
    Answer: b) Dasyus

23. Were the Rigvedic people aware of Iron?
    a) Yes
    b) No
    c) Only partially
    d) The text does not specify
    Answer: b) No

24. Which deity was called 'Bhuvanchakshu'?
    a) Indra
    b) Varuna
    c) Agni
    d) Soma
    Answer: c) Agni

25. The Gayatri Mantra is mentioned in which Mandala of the Rigveda?
    a) IInd Mandala
    b) IIIrd Mandala
    c) VIIth Mandala
    d) Xth Mandala
    Answer: b) IIIrd Mandala

26. Who were the divine physicians in the Rig Vedic period?
    a) Yama and Varuna
    b) Asvins and Nasatyas
    c) Agni and Indra
    d) Pushan and Aditi
    Answer: b) Asvins and Nasatyas

27. Who was the Lord of the dead in the Rig Vedic period?
    a) Prajapati
    b) Vishnu
    c) Yama
    d) Varuna
    Answer: c) Yama

28. Who is mentioned as the mother of Surya?
    a) Aditi
    b) Ushas
    c) Prithvi
    d) Saraswati
    Answer: a) Aditi

29. Which Rig Vedic deity bears the title 'Asura'?
    a) Indra
    b) Agni
    c) Varuna
    d) Soma
    Answer: c) Varuna

30. Which river was the most important river in the Vedic period?
    a) Saraswati
    b) Ganga
    c) Yamuna
    d) Indus
    Answer: d) Indus

31. Which river was the most sacred river in the Vedic period?
    a) Indus
    b) Ganga
    c) Saraswati
    d) Yamuna
    Answer: c) Saraswati

THE LATER VEDIC PERIOD (1000-600 B.C.)

32. The period assigned to the Later Vedic phase is:
    a) 1500-1000 B.C.
    b) 1000-600 B.C.
    c) 600-300 B.C.
    d) 2500-1750 B.C.
    Answer: b) 1000-600 B.C.

33. Communities during the Later Vedic phase used a particular kind of pottery called:
    a) Black and Red Ware
    b) Painted Grey Ware (PGW)
    c) Northern Black Polished Ware
    d) Ochre Coloured Pottery
    Answer: b) Painted Grey Ware (PGW)

34. When was Iron metal introduced according to the text?
    a) Around 1500 B.C.
    b) Around 1000-800 B.C.
    c) Around 600 B.C.
    d) Around 500 B.C.
    Answer: b) Around 1000-800 B.C.

35. What was Iron mentioned as in the Later Vedic period?
    a) Loh
    b) Ayas
    c) Tamra
    d) Hiranya
    Answer: b) Ayas

36. How was society clearly divided in the Later Vedic period?
    a) Into tribes
    b) Into occupational groups only
    c) Into four Varnas
    d) Into regional kingdoms
    Answer: c) Into four Varnas

37. The upper three classes (Brahmin, Kshatriya, Vaishya) were known as:
    a) Shudras
    b) Dvij (twice born)
    c) Rishis
    d) Ganapatis
    Answer: b) Dvij (twice born)

38. The concept of 'Gotra' first appeared in which Veda, with the meaning of clan?
    a) Rigveda
    b) Yajurveda
    c) Samaveda
    d) Atharvaveda
    Answer: d) Atharvaveda

39. In the Later Vedic period, was 'sati' common?
    a) Yes, it was widely practiced.
    b) No, it was not common.
    c) The text does not mention it.
    d) It was mandatory for all widows.
    Answer: b) No, it was not common.

40. Which two Rig Vedic deities lost their importance in the Later Vedic period?
    a) Varuna and Soma
    b) Indra and Agni
    c) Pushan and Aditi
    d) Yama and Asvins
    Answer: b) Indra and Agni

41. Who became the supreme deity in the Later Vedic period?
    a) Indra
    b) Agni
    c) Prajapati (the creator)
    d) Vishnu
    Answer: c) Prajapati (the creator)

42. Who came to be conceived as the preserver and protector of the people in the Later Vedic period?
    a) Indra
    b) Prajapati
    c) Vishnu
    d) Pushan
    Answer: c) Vishnu

43. Which deity became the God of Shudras in the Later Vedic period?
    a) Indra
    b) Varuna
    c) Pushan
    d) Agni
    Answer: c) Pushan

44. How many deities were there during the Later Vedic period?
    a) 11
    b) 22
    c) 33
    d) 44
    Answer: c) 33

45. Which ritual conferred supreme power on the king in the Later Vedic period?
    a) Vajapeya
    b) Ashwamedha
    c) Rajasuya
    d) Soma sacrifice
    Answer: c) Rajasuya

46. What was the main sport in the Later Vedic period?
    a) Hunting
    b) Gambling
    c) Chariot racing
    d) Wrestling
    Answer: c) Chariot racing

47. 'Vajapeya' refers to:
    a) A type of sacrifice
    b) A drink of strength
    c) A royal procession
    d) A form of entertainment
    Answer: b) A drink of strength

48. Kings of the Eastern region were called:
    a) Svarat
    b) Virat
    c) Bhoja
    d) Samrat
    Answer: d) Samrat

49. Kings of the Western region were called:
    a) Samrat
    b) Svarat
    c) Virat
    d) Bhoja
    Answer: b) Svarat

50. Kings of the Northern region were called:
    a) Samrat
    b) Svarat
    c) Virat
    d) Bhoja
    Answer: c) Virat

51. Kings of the Southern region were called:
    a) Samrat
    b) Svarat
    c) Virat
    d) Bhoja
    Answer: d) Bhoja

52. Kings of the middle country were called:
    a) Samrat
    b) Raja
    c) Virat
    d) Bhoja
    Answer: b) Raja

53. The earliest evidence of medicine comes from which Veda?
    a) Rigveda
    b) Yajurveda
    c) Samaveda
    d) Atharvaveda
    Answer: d) Atharvaveda

54. In which Veda are Sabha and Samiti described as daughters of Prajapati?
    a) Rigveda
    b) Yajurveda
    c) Samaveda
    d) Atharvaveda
    Answer: d) Atharvaveda

55. A three-roomed mud house has been discovered at:
    a) Atranjikhera
    b) Suneri village
    c) Bhagwanpura
    d) Hastinapura (not mentioned but similar context)
    Answer: c) Bhagwanpura

56. The largest deposit of Iron weapons have been found at:
    a) Bhagwanpura
    b) Suneri village
    c) Atranjikhera
    d) Hastinapura
    Answer: c) Atranjikhera

57. Two furnaces for iron smelting have been found at which village in Jhunjhunu district?
    a) Bhagwanpura
    b) Atranjikhera
    c) Suneri village
    d) Noh (not mentioned but similar context)
    Answer: c) Suneri village

58. Eight forms of marriages are given in which text?
    a) Satapatha Brahmana
    b) Jabala Upanishad
    c) Ashvalahayan Grihyasutra
    d) Manu Smriti
    Answer: c) Ashvalahayan Grihyasutra

59. Which Brahmana text states that 'wife is half her husband'?
    a) Aitareya Brahmana
    b) Satapatha Brahmana
    c) Taittiriya Brahmana
    d) Gopatha Brahmana
    Answer: b) Satapatha Brahmana

60. Compared to the Early Vedic period, what was the status of women in the Later Vedic period?
    a) It improved significantly.
    b) It remained the same.
    c) It deteriorated.
    d) They gained political power.
    Answer: c) It deteriorated.

61. The earliest clear reference to the four ashrams is given in which Upanishad?
    a) Chandogya Upanishad
    b) Brihadaranyaka Upanishad
    c) Jabala Upanishad
    d) Katha Upanishad
    Answer: c) Jabala Upanishad

THE VEDIC LITERATURE

62. The word Veda is derived from the Sanskrit word 'Vidi' meaning:
    a) To sing
    b) To know or knowledge par excellence
    c) To perform rituals
    d) To meditate
    Answer: b) To know or knowledge par excellence

63. Vedic texts are divided into Sruti and Smriti. Sruti texts are based on:
    a) Memory
    b) Hearing
    c) Written records
    d) Interpretation
    Answer: b) Hearing

64. Which of the following is NOT part of the literature known as Sruti?
    a) Four Vedas and their Samhitas
    b) Brahmanas
    c) Mahakavyas (Epics)
    d) Upanishads
    Answer: c) Mahakavyas (Epics)

RIG VEDA

65. The Rig Veda is divided into how many Books or Mandalas?
    a) 8
    b) 9
    c) 10
    d) 12
    Answer: c) 10

66. Which Books of the Rig Veda are considered the oldest?
    a) Books I and X
    b) Books VIII and IX
    c) Books II to VII
    d) Book I and Book VII
    Answer: c) Books II to VII

67. The Rig Veda is a collection of how many hymns?
    a) 500
    b) 711
    c) 1000
    d) 1028
    Answer: d) 1028

68. The Rig Veda was written between 1700-1500 B.C. when Aryans were still in:
    a) Delhi
    b) Punjab
    c) Uttar Pradesh
    d) Bihar
    Answer: b) Punjab

69. Which Mandala of the Rig Veda is exclusively dedicated to Soma?
    a) VIII Mandala
    b) IX Mandala
    c) X Mandala
    d) III Mandala
    Answer: b) IX Mandala

70. The singer of the Rig Veda is called the:
    a) Udgata
    b) Ardhavayu
    c) Hotra
    d) Brahmana
    Answer: c) Hotra

71. The famous Purushsukta hymn, explaining the origin of four Varnas, is contained in which Mandala of the Rig Veda?
    a) III Mandala
    b) VII Mandala
    c) IX Mandala
    d) X Mandala
    Answer: d) X Mandala

72. Who wrote the Gayatri Mantra, mentioned in the 3rd Mandala of the Rig Veda?
    a) Vashistha
    b) Agastya
    c) Viswamitra
    d) Bharadvaja
    Answer: c) Viswamitra

YAJUR VEDA

73. The Yajur Veda is primarily a:
    a) Philosophical text
    b) Collection of melodies
    c) Ritualistic Veda
    d) Book of magical formulas
    Answer: c) Ritualistic Veda

74. The Yajur Veda is divided into which two main parts?
    a) Shukla Yajurveda and Krishna Yajurveda
    b) Purva Yajurveda and Uttara Yajurveda
    c) Brahmana Yajurveda and Aranyaka Yajurveda
    d) Rig Yajurveda and Sama Yajurveda
    Answer: a) Shukla Yajurveda and Krishna Yajurveda

75. The singer of the Yajur Veda is called:
    a) Hotra
    b) Udgata
    c) Ardhavayu
    d) Purohita
    Answer: c) Ardhavayu

SAMA VEDA

76. The word 'Saman' in Sama Veda means:
    a) Hymn
    b) Ritual
    c) Melody
    d) Knowledge
    Answer: c) Melody

77. How many hymns are collected in the Sama Veda?
    a) 711
    b) 1028
    c) 1603
    d) 2000
    Answer: c) 1603

78. The Sama Veda contains the:
    a) Purushsukta hymn
    b) Gayatri Mantra
    c) Dhrupada Raga
    d) Charms and spells
    Answer: c) Dhrupada Raga

79. The singer of the Sama Veda is called:
    a) Hotra
    b) Ardhavayu
    c) Udgata
    d) Brahmana
    Answer: c) Udgata

ATHARVA VEDA

80. The Atharva Veda is a collection of how many hymns?
    a) 500
    b) 711
    c) 1028
    d) 1603
    Answer: b) 711

81. Which is considered the latest Veda?
    a) Rig Veda
    b) Yajur Veda
    c) Sama Veda
    d) Atharva Veda
    Answer: d) Atharva Veda

82. The Atharva Veda is known for containing:
    a) Rules for sacrifices
    b) Philosophical discussions
    c) Magical formulas and medicinal treatises
    d) Genealogies of royal dynasties
    Answer: c) Magical formulas and medicinal treatises

83. In the Atharvaveda, Sabha and Samiti are described as:
    a) Uterine sisters – the two daughters of Prajapati
    b) Two branches of administration
    c) Assemblies for the twice-born
    d) Places for cattle grazing
    Answer: a) Uterine sisters – the two daughters of Prajapati

84. Which Veda is also known as Brahma Veda?
    a) Rig Veda
    b) Yajur Veda
    c) Sama Veda
    d) Atharva Veda
    Answer: d) Atharva Veda

Upanishads

85. The Upanishads are primarily what kind of texts?
    a) Ritualistic
    b) Historical
    c) Philosophical
    d) Grammatical
    Answer: c) Philosophical

86. Why are the Upanishads generally called Vedanta?
    a) Because they were written by Ved Vyasa.
    b) Because they are the oldest part of the Vedas.
    c) Because they came towards the end of the Veda.
    d) Because they explain the meaning of all Vedas.
    Answer: c) Because they came towards the end of the Veda.

87. How many Upanishads are there according to the text?
    a) 50
    b) 100
    c) 108
    d) 200
    Answer: c) 108

88. Which is the oldest Upanishad mentioned?
    a) Chandogya
    b) Katha
    c) Brihadaranyaka
    d) Kena
    Answer: c) Brihadaranyaka

Literature of Vedic Tradition (600 BC-600 AD)

89. Literature of Vedic Tradition (Smriti) comprises how many literary works?
    a) 4
    b) 5
    c) 6
    d) 7
    Answer: c) 6

90. Which of the following is NOT one of the six literary works of Smriti mentioned?
    a) Vedangas/Sutras
    b) Mahakavyas (Epics)
    c) Puranas
    d) Sruti
    Answer: d) Sruti

91. How many Vedangas are there?
    a) 4
    b) 5
    c) 6
    d) 7
    Answer: c) 6

92. 'Shiksha' (Phonetics) is one of the Vedangas. Which is the oldest text on phonetics?
    a) Ashtadyayi
    b) Nirukta
    c) Pratishakhya
    d) Chhandasutras
    Answer: c) Pratishakhya

93. Kalpa Sutras deal with:
    a) Phonetics
    b) Rituals
    c) Grammar
    d) Astronomy
    Answer: b) Rituals

94. Which part of Kalpa Sutras deals with family ceremonies?
    a) Shrauta Sutras
    b) Shulva Sutra
    c) Grihya Sutras
    d) Dharma Sutras
    Answer: c) Grihya Sutras

95. Ashtadyayi, the oldest grammar of the world, was written by:
    a) Yask
    b) Pingal
    c) Panini
    d) Kashyap
    Answer: c) Panini

96. Nirukta (Etymology) was written by:
    a) Panini
    b) Yask
    c) Kashyap
    d) Pingal
    Answer: b) Yask

97. Nighantu is described as the oldest:
    a) Grammar text
    b) Dictionary
    c) Word-collection of the world
    d) Phonetics text
    Answer: c) Word-collection of the world

98. Nirukta is described as the oldest:
    a) Word-collection
    b) Dictionary of the world
    c) Phonetics text
    d) Grammar text
    Answer: b) Dictionary of the world

99. Chhandasutras (Metrics) is a famous text written by:
    a) Lagadh Muni
    b) Pingal
    c) Panini
    d) Yask
    Answer: b) Pingal

100. Vedanga Jyotisha (Astronomy), the oldest Jyotisha text, was written by:
    a) Pingal
    b) Yask
    c) Lagadh Muni
    d) Panini
    Answer: c) Lagadh Muni

101. How many famous Smritis are there?
    a) 4
    b) 5
    c) 6
    d) 7
    Answer: c) 6

102. Which is the oldest Smriti text?
    a) Yajnavalkya Smriti
    b) Narad Smriti
    c) Manu Smriti
    d) Parashara Smriti
    Answer: c) Manu Smriti

103. Which of the following is NOT a commentator on Manu Smriti?
    a) Vishwarupa
    b) Meghatithi
    c) Vijayaneshwar
    d) Kulluk Bhatt
    Answer: c) Vijayaneshwar

104. Which Smriti had commentators like Vishwarupa, Vijayaneshwar, and Apararka?
    a) Manu Smriti
    b) Yajnavalkya Smriti
    c) Narad Smriti
    d) Brihaspati Smriti
    Answer: b) Yajnavalkya Smriti

105. Narad Smriti, Parashara Smriti, Brihaspati Smriti, and Katyayana Smriti belong to which period?
    a) Pre-Gupta Period
    b) Gupta Period
    c) Post-Gupta Period
    d) Mauryan Period
    Answer: b) Gupta Period

106. The Ramayana is known as:
    a) The longest epic
    b) Adi Kavya (the oldest epic of the world)
    c) Shatasahastri Samhita
    d) Jay Samhita
    Answer: b) Adi Kavya (the oldest epic of the world)

107. At present, the Ramayana consists of how many shlokas (verses)?
    a) 6,000
    b) 12,000
    c) 24,000
    d) 1,00,000
    Answer: c) 24,000

108. Which Kandas (sections) were the latest additions to the Ramayana?
    a) 2nd and 3rd Kandas
    b) 4th and 5th Kandas
    c) 1st and 7th Kandas
    d) 6th and 7th Kandas
    Answer: c) 1st and 7th Kandas

109. Who is the author of the Mahabharata?
    a) Valmiki
    b) Ved Vyasa
    c) Vishwamitra
    d) Panini
    Answer: b) Ved Vyasa

110. The Mahabharata is described as:
    a) The oldest epic
    b) The shortest epic
    c) The longest epic of the world
    d) A collection of hymns
    Answer: c) The longest epic of the world

111. At present, the Mahabharata consists of how many shlokas (verses)?
    a) 24,000
    b) 8,800
    c) 1,00,000
    d) 50,000
    Answer: c) 1,00,000

112. Jay Samhita was the original name of Mahabharata with how many verses?
    a) 8,800
    b) 24,000
    c) 1,00,000
    d) 6,000
    Answer: a) 8,800

113. From which Parvan (chapter) of the Mahabharata is the Bhagavad Gita extracted?
    a) Shanti Parvan
    b) Udyoga Parvan
    c) Bhishma Parvan
    d) Drona Parvan
    Answer: c) Bhishma Parvan

114. Which is the largest Parvan (chapter) of the Mahabharata?
    a) Bhishma Parvan
    b) Shanti Parvan
    c) Virata Parvan
    d) Karna Parvan
    Answer: b) Shanti Parvan

115. The word 'Purana' means:
    a) The new
    b) The old
    c) The sacred
    d) The truth
    Answer: b) The old

116. How many famous Puranas are there?
    a) 10
    b) 12
    c) 18
    d) 24
    Answer: c) 18

117. Which Purana is mentioned as the oldest Puranic text?
    a) Bhagavata Purana
    b) Vishnu Purana
    c) Matsya Purana
    d) Vayu Purana
    Answer: c) Matsya Purana

118. What do the Puranas primarily describe?
    a) Vedic rituals
    b) Philosophical concepts
    c) Genealogies of various royal dynasties
    d) Geographical features
    Answer: c) Genealogies of various royal dynasties

119. Ayurveda (Medicine) is traditionally associated with which Veda?
    a) Sama Veda
    b) Rig Veda
    c) Yajur Veda
    d) Atharva Veda
    Answer: b) Rig Veda

120. Gandharvaveda (Music) is traditionally associated with which Veda?
    a) Rig Veda
    b) Sama Veda
    c) Yajur Veda
    d) Atharva Veda
    Answer: b) Sama Veda

121. Dhanurveda (Archery) is traditionally associated with which Veda?
    a) Rig Veda
    b) Sama Veda
    c) Yajur Veda
    d) Atharva Veda
    Answer: c) Yajur Veda

122. Shilpveda/Arthaveda (the science of craft/wealth) is traditionally associated with which Veda?
    a) Rig Veda
    b) Sama Veda
    c) Yajur Veda
    d) Atharva Veda
    Answer: d) Atharva Veda

123. How many schools of Indian philosophy are known as Shad-Darshanas?
    a) 4
    b) 5
    c) 6
    d) 7
    Answer: c) 6

124. Who founded the Sankhya Darshana?
    a) Patanjali
    b) Kapila
    c) Akshapada Gautama
    d) Jaimini
    Answer: b) Kapila

125. Who founded the Yoga Darshana?
    a) Kapila
    b) Patanjali
    c) Uluka Kanada
    d) Badarayana
    Answer: b) Patanjali

126. The basic text for Nyaya Darshana is:
    a) Sankhya Sutra
    b) Yoga Sutra
    c) Nayaya Sutra
    d) Vaishesika Sutra
    Answer: c) Nayaya Sutra

127. Who founded the Vaishesika Darshana?
    a) Jaimini
    b) Uluka Kanada
    c) Akshapada Gautama
    d) Badarayana
    Answer: b) Uluka Kanada

128. Jaimini is the founder of which Darshana?
    a) Vedanta
    b) Mimansa/Purva Mimansa
    c) Sankhya
    d) Yoga
    Answer: b) Mimansa/Purva Mimansa

129. Badarayana is the founder of which Darshana?
    a) Nyaya
    b) Vaishesika
    c) Vedanta/Uttara-Mimansa
    d) Mimansa
    Answer: c) Vedanta/Uttara-Mimansa

Rigvedic Names and Modern Names of Rivers

    c) Jhelum
    d) Ravi
    Answer: c) Jhelum

    b) Chenab
    c) Beas
    d) Ravi
    Answer: b) Chenab

    b) Sutlej
    c) Beas

    b) Sutlej

    d) Jhelum

Six Systems of Philosophy & Religious Ideas

140. Which other scholar is associated with Sankhya philosophy besides Kapila?
    a) Vatsayayan
    b) Ishwar Krishna
    c) Myas
    d) Shankaracharya
    Answer: b) Ishwar Krishna

141. Vatsayayan and Udyanacharya are associated with which philosophy?
    a) Sankhya
    b) Nyaya
    c) Yoga
    d) Vedanta
    Answer: b) Nyaya

142. Myas is an associated scholar for which philosophy?
    a) Sankhya
    b) Nyaya
    c) Yoga
    d) Vaisheshika
    Answer: c) Yoga

143. Keshav raishra and Vishvanath are associated with which philosophy?
    a) Yoga
    b) Mimansa
    c) Vaisheshika
    d) Vedanta
    Answer: c) Vaisheshika

144. Sabrasvamin and Kumaril Bhatt are associated with which philosophy?
    a) Nyaya
    b) Mimansa
    c) Vedanta
    d) Sankhya
    Answer: b) Mimansa

145. Shankaracharya, Ramanuj, and Madhvacharya are prominent scholars of which philosophy?
    a) Mimansa
    b) Yoga
    c) Vaisheshika
    d) Vedanta
    Answer: d) Vedanta

146. Who founded the Pasupatas religious sect?
    a) Basava
    b) Lakulisa/Nakulisa
    c) Vasugupta
    d) Srikanth-Sivacharya
    Answer: b) Lakulisa/Nakulisa

147. Basava is the founder of which religious idea/sect?
    a) Pasupatas
    b) Lingayats
    c) Pratyabhijna
    d) Advait
    Answer: b) Lingayats

148. Vasugupta is the founder of which religious idea/sect?
    a) Lingayats
    b) Pratyabhijna
    c) Spanda-sastra
    d) Siva-Siddhanata
    Answer: b) Pratyabhijna

149. Kallata and Samnanda are the founders of which religious idea/sect?
    a) Pratyabhijna
    b) Spanda-sastra
    c) Siva-Siddhanata
    d) Advait
    Answer: b) Spanda-sastra

150. Srikanth-Sivacharya is the founder of which religious idea/sect?
    a) Spanda-sastra
    b) Siva-Siddhanata
    c) Advait
    d) Vishistadvait
    Answer: b) Siva-Siddhanata

151. Who founded Advait philosophy?
    a) Ramanujacharya
    b) Madhvacharya
    c) Shankaracharya
    d) Nimbakacharya
    Answer: c) Shankaracharya

152. Ramanujacharya founded which religious idea/sect?
    a) Advait
    b) Vishistadvait
    c) Brahm Sampradaya
    d) Sanak Sampradaya
    Answer: b) Vishistadvait

153. Madhvacharya founded which religious idea/sect?
    a) Vishistadvait
    b) Brahm Sampradaya
    c) Sanak Sampradaya
    d) Advait
    Answer: b) Brahm Sampradaya

154. Nimbakacharya founded which religious idea/sect?
    a) Advait
    b) Vishistadvait
    c) Brahm Sampradaya
    d) Sanak Sampradaya
    Answer: d) Sanak Sampradaya

RELIGIOUS MOVEMENTS

SAIVISM

1.  The origin of Saivism can be traced back to:
    a) Vedic times
    b) Post-Vedic times
    c) Pre-Vedic times
    d) Christian era
    Answer: c) Pre-Vedic times

2.  By the early centuries of the Christian era, Saivism was a popular sect all over India, with which deity as its principal deity?
    a) Vishnu
    b) Brahma
    c) Siva
    d) Indra
    Answer: c) Siva

3.  In what form was Siva primarily worshipped according to the text?
    a) Human idol
    b) Linga
    c) Animal form
    d) Tree form
    Answer: b) Linga

4.  Tamil Sangam work refers to Siva as:
    a) Harihara
    b) Mamudu mudalvan
    c) Ardhanarishvara
    d) Dakshinamurti
    Answer: b) Mamudu mudalvan

5.  Siva’s consort Parvati was adored as:
    a) Lakshmi
    b) Saraswati
    c) Sakti
    d) Ganga
    Answer: c) Sakti

6.  In the Satavahana kingdom, Parvati was worshipped as:
    a) Durga
    b) Kali
    c) Gauri
    d) Uma
    Answer: c) Gauri

7.  In the Tamil country, Skanda was worshipped in which form?
    a) Ganesa
    b) Murugan
    c) Kartikeya
    d) Subramanya
    Answer: b) Murugan

8.  Ganesa was also known as:
    a) Kartikeya
    b) Murugan
    c) Vinayak
    d) Skanda
    Answer: c) Vinayak

9.  Which was the most popular Saiva sect?
    a) Kapalika
    b) Kalamukha
    c) Pasupata
    d) Nath
    Answer: c) Pasupata

10. Which Saiva sects developed much later?
    a) Pasupata and Kapalika
    b) Kapalika and Kalamukha
    c) Pasupata and Kalamukha
    d) Nath and Pasupata
    Answer: b) Kapalika and Kalamukha

VAISHNAVISM

11. The principal deity of Vaishnavism was:
    a) Siva
    b) Brahma
    c) Vishnu
    d) Devi
    Answer: c) Vishnu

12. In the early period, the cult of Vishnu was known by what name?
    a) Vaishnavism
    b) Bhagavatism
    c) Pancharatra
    d) Krishnaism
    Answer: b) Bhagavatism

13. Bhagavatism owed its origin to the:
    a) Vedas
    b) Puranas
    c) Upanishadas
    d) Epics
    Answer: c) Upanishadas

14. Bhagavatism arose around which region?
    a) Ujjain
    b) Kashi
    c) Mathura region
    d) Pataliputra
    Answer: c) Mathura region

15. Who are the great leader philosophers of Vaishnavism who defended and popularised the religion?
    a) Rishis
    b) Munis
    c) Acharyas
    d) Gurus
    Answer: c) Acharyas

16. Which of the following is NOT mentioned as a prominent Vaishnava Acharya?
    a) Ramanuja
    b) Chaitanya
    c) Shankaracharya
    d) Madhava
    Answer: c) Shankaracharya

17. Vaishnavism was developed and popularised in the South by how many saints?
    a) Ten
    b) Eleven
    c) Twelve
    d) Thirteen
    Answer: c) Twelve

18. What were these twelve Vaishnava saints known as?
    a) Nayanars
    b) Alvars
    c) Sants
    d) Bhaktas
    Answer: b) Alvars

19. Which two Alvars are mentioned as the greatest among the twelve?
    a) Andal and Tiruppan Alvar
    b) Nammalvar and Tirumalsalvar
    c) Poigai Alvar and Pey Alvar
    d) Bhutath Alvar and Kulasekhara Alvar
    Answer: b) Nammalvar and Tirumalsalvar

CHRISTIANITY

20. Christianity was founded by:
    a) Prophet Muhammad
    b) Jesus Christ
    c) Zoroaster
    d) Gautama Buddha
    Answer: b) Jesus Christ

21. Jesus Christ was born in Bethlehem, near:
    a) Mecca
    b) Medina
    c) Jerusalem
    d) Rome
    Answer: c) Jerusalem

22. Christmas is celebrated on:
    a) December 24th
    b) December 25th
    c) January 1st
    d) March 25th
    Answer: b) December 25th

23. Who were Jesus Christ's first two disciples mentioned in the text?
    a) John and James
    b) Peter and John
    c) Andrews and Peter
    d) Thomas and Matthew
    Answer: c) Andrews and Peter

24. By whom were Jesus Christ's first two disciples hanged in AD 33?
    a) Roman Emperor Nero
    b) Roman Governor Portius
    c) Jewish authorities
    d) King Herod
    Answer: b) Roman Governor Portius

25. The holy book of Christians is the:
    a) Quran
    b) Zend Avesta
    c) Bible
    d) Tripitaka
    Answer: c) Bible

26. The holy symbol of Christians is the sign of:
    a) Star
    b) Crescent
    c) Cross
    d) Wheel
    Answer: c) Cross

ISLAM

27. Who founded the Islamic religion?
    a) Ali Hussain
    b) Abu Bakr
    c) Hazrat Muhammad Saheb
    d) Omar
    Answer: c) Hazrat Muhammad Saheb

28. Hazrat Muhammad Saheb was born in AD 570 at:
    a) Medina
    b) Bethlehem
    c) Mecca
    d) Jerusalem
    Answer: c) Mecca

29. What was the name of Hazrat Muhammad Saheb's mother?
    a) Khajida
    b) Fatima
    c) Amina
    d) Mary
    Answer: c) Amina

30. At what age did Hazrat Muhammad Saheb marry Khajida?
    a) 20 years
    b) 25 years
    c) 30 years
    d) 35 years
    Answer: b) 25 years

31. Hazrat Muhammad attained supreme knowledge or enlightenment in AD 610 in which cave near Mecca?
    a) Mount Hira
    b) Mount Sinai
    c) Hira Cave
    d) Thawr Cave
    Answer: c) Hira Cave

32. The teachings of Hazrat Muhammad are compiled in the Holy:
    a) Bible
    b) Zend Avesta
    c) Quran
    d) Hadith
    Answer: c) Quran

33. The beginning of the Hijri Era is marked by Hazrat Muhammad's journey from Mecca to Medina on which date?
    a) AD 570
    b) AD 610
    c) 24th September AD 622
    d) 8th June, AD 632
    Answer: c) 24th September AD 622

34. When did Hazrat Muhammad die?
    a) AD 610
    b) AD 622
    c) 8th June, AD 632
    d) AD 640
    Answer: c) 8th June, AD 632

35. After Hazrat Muhammad's death, Islam divided into which two cults?
    a) Sunni and Sufi
    b) Shia and Sunni
    c) Shia and Ahmadiyya
    d) Hanafi and Maliki
    Answer: b) Shia and Sunni

36. Hazrat Muhammad's successors were known as:
    a) Imams
    b) Sultans
    c) Khalifa
    d) Emirs
    Answer: c) Khalifa

37. The designation of Caliphate was ended by which Turkish ruler in 1924 A.D.?
    a) Ottoman Sultan
    b) Mushtafa Kamal Pasha
    c) Ataturk (indirectly, but the name mentioned is Mushtafa Kamal Pasha)
    d) Erdogan
    Answer: b) Mushtafa Kamal Pasha

ZOROASTRIANISM (PARSI)

38. Parsi religion was founded by:
    a) Prophet Muhammad
    b) Zoroaster (Zarathustra)
    c) Jesus Christ
    d) Gautama Buddha
    Answer: b) Prophet Zoroaster (Zarathustra)

39. The teachings of Prophet Zoroaster are compiled in the holy book:
    a) Bible
    b) Quran
    c) Zend Avesta
    d) Tripitaka
    Answer: c) Zend Avesta

40. Followers of Prophet Zoroaster believed in one God, named:
    a) Allah
    b) Yahweh
    c) Ahur
    d) Brahma
    Answer: c) Ahur

BUDDHISM

41. Buddhism was founded by:
    a) Vardhamana Mahavira
    b) Gautama Buddha
    c) Mahakassapa
    d) Moggliputta Tissa
    Answer: b) Gautama Buddha

42. Gautama Buddha was born on which day in 563 B.C.?
    a) Diwali
    b) Holi
    c) Vaisakh Purnima
    d) Dussehra
    Answer: c) Vaisakh Purnima

43. Buddha belonged to which clan of Kshatriyas?
    a) Lichchavi
    b) Jantriak
    c) Sakya
    d) Kuru
    Answer: c) Sakya

44. What was the name of Buddha's father?
    a) Chetak
    b) Siddhartha
    c) Suddhodana
    d) Rahul
    Answer: c) Suddhodana

45. Buddha was born in Lumbini in:
    a) Rajagriha
    b) Vaishali
    c) Kapilvastu
    d) Kushinagar
    Answer: c) Kapilvastu

46. What was the name of Buddha's mother?
    a) Yashoda
    b) Trishala
    c) Mahamaya
    d) Gautami
    Answer: c) Mahamaya

47. At what age did Buddha get Nirvana?
    a) 29 years
    b) 30 years
    c) 35 years
    d) 40 years
    Answer: c) 35 years

48. Buddha got Nirvana at Uruvela on the bank of which river?
    a) Ganga
    b) Yamuna
    c) Niranjana
    d) Rijupalika
    Answer: c) Niranjana

49. Where did Buddha give his first Sermon?
    a) Lumbini
    b) Bodh Gaya
    c) Kushinagar
    d) Sarnath
    Answer: d) Sarnath

50. Buddha’s first sermon is called as:
    a) Mahaparinirvana
    b) Dharmachakraparivartana
    c) Mahabhinishkraman
    d) Sambodhi
    Answer: b) “Dharma Chakraparivartana”

51. When did Buddha die?
    a) 563 B.C.
    b) 483 B.C.
    c) 383 B.C.
    d) 236 B.C.
    Answer: b) 483 B.C.

52. Buddha died at which place?
    a) Sarnath
    b) Bodh Gaya
    c) Kushinagar
    d) Rajagriha
    Answer: c) Kushinagar

53. Kushinagar has been identified with which village in Deoria district of U.P.?
    a) Piprahwa
    b) Kasia
    c) Sravasti
    d) Vaishali
    Answer: b) Kasia

54. Buddha was brought up by his stepmother named:
    a) Mahamaya
    b) Yashoda
    c) Gautami
    d) Trishala
    Answer: c) Gautami

55. After seeing which four sights did Buddha decide to become a wanderer?
    a) A rich man, a poor man, a warrior, and a king
    b) An old man, a sick man, a corpse, and an ascetic
    c) A child, a woman, a man, and an animal
    d) A tree, a river, a mountain, and a flower
    Answer: b) An old man, a sick man, a corpse, and an ascetic

56. Which of the following was NOT one of the five disciples of Buddha mentioned?
    a) Asvajit
    b) Upali
    c) Devadatta
    d) Ananda
    Answer: c) Devadatta

Five Great Events of Buddha’s Life and their symbols

57. What is the symbol for Buddha's Birth?
    a) Horse
    b) Bodhi tree
    c) Lotus and Bull
    d) Stupa
    Answer: c) Lotus and Bull

58. What is the symbol for Buddha's Great Renunciation?
    a) Lotus
    b) Bull
    c) Horse
    d) Wheel
    Answer: c) Horse

59. What is the symbol for Buddha's Nirvana?
    a) Lotus
    b) Horse
    c) Bodhi tree
    d) Dharmachakra
    Answer: c) Bodhi tree

60. What is the symbol for Buddha's First Sermon?
    a) Stupa
    b) Bodhi tree
    c) Dharmachakra or wheel
    d) Lotus
    Answer: c) Dharmachakra or wheel

61. What is the symbol for Buddha's Parinirvana or Death?
    a) Wheel
    b) Horse
    c) Stupa
    d) Bodhi tree
    Answer: c) Stupa

Four Noble Truths

62. Which of the following is NOT one of the Four Noble Truths?
    a) The world is full of sorrows.
    b) Desire is the root cause of sorrow.
    c) Desire cannot be removed.
    d) Desire can be removed by following the eight-fold path.
    Answer: c) Desire cannot be removed.

Eight Fold Path

63. Which of the following is NOT part of the Eight-Fold Path?
    a) Right understanding
    b) Right speech
    c) Right wealth
    d) Right action
    Answer: c) Right wealth

Three Ratnas

64. Which of the following is NOT one of the Three Ratnas of Buddhism?
    a) Buddha
    b) Dhamma
    c) Karma
    d) Sangha
    Answer: c) Karma

Code of Conduct

65. Which of the following is NOT part of the Buddhist Code of Conduct mentioned?
    a) Do not covet the property of others
    b) Do not commit violence
    c) Do not speak a lie
    d) Indulge in corrupt practices
    Answer: d) Indulge in corrupt practices

BUDDHIST COUNCILS

66. Where was the First Buddhist Council held?
    a) Vaisali
    b) Pataliputra
    c) Kashmir
    d) Saptaparni cave near Rajagriha
    Answer: d) Saptaparni cave near Rajagriha

67. When was the First Buddhist Council held?
    a) 563 BC
    b) 483 BC
    c) 383 BC
    d) 236 BC
    Answer: b) 483 BC

68. Who was the Chairman of the First Buddhist Council?
    a) Sabakami
    b) Moggliputta Tissa
    c) Vasumitra
    d) Mahakassapa
    Answer: d) Mahakassapa

69. Who was the Patron of the First Buddhist Council?
    a) Kalashoka
    b) Ashoka
    c) Kanishka
    d) Ajatshatru
    Answer: d) Ajatshatru

70. The First Buddhist Council was held to compile:
    a) Abhidhamma Pitaka
    b) Dhamma Pitaka and Vinaya Pitaka
    c) Mahayana texts
    d) Jain Angas
    Answer: b) Dhamma Pitaka and Vinaya Pitaka

71. The Second Buddhist Council was held at:
    a) Rajagriha
    b) Vaisali
    c) Pataliputra
    d) Kashmir
    Answer: b) Vaisali

72. When was the Second Buddhist Council held?
    a) 483 BC
    b) 383 BC
    c) 236 BC
    d) 513 AD
    Answer: b) 383 BC

73. What was a significant outcome of the Second Buddhist Council?
    a) Compilation of Tripitaka
    b) Division into Sthaviravadins and Mahasanghikas
    c) Belief in idol worship
    d) Foundation of Vajrayana
    Answer: b) Division into Sthaviravadins and Mahasanghikas

74. Who was the Chairman of the Second Buddhist Council?
    a) Mahakassapa
    b) Sabakami
    c) Moggliputta Tissa
    d) Vasumitra
    Answer: b) Sabakami

75. Who was the Patron of the Second Buddhist Council?
    a) Ajatshatru
    b) Kalashoka
    c) Ashoka
    d) Kanishka
    Answer: b) Kalashoka

76. The Third Buddhist Council was held at:
    a) Vaisali
    b) Pataliputra
    c) Kashmir
    d) Rajagriha
    Answer: b) Pataliputra

77. The Third Buddhist Council was held during the reign of:
    a) Ajatshatru
    b) Kalashoka
    c) Ashoka
    d) Kanishka
    Answer: c) Ashoka

78. The Third Buddhist Council was held under the Presidentship of:
    a) Sabakami
    b) Vasumitra
    c) Moggliputta Tissa
    d) Mahakassapa
    Answer: c) Moggliputta Tissa

79. The Third Buddhist Council was held to:
    a) Resolve differences in rites
    b) Divide the Buddhist Sangha
    c) Revise the scriptures
    d) Promote idol worship
    Answer: c) Revise the scriptures

80. The Fourth Buddhist Council was held during the reign of:
    a) Ashoka
    b) Kanishka
    c) Kalashoka
    d) Ajatshatru
    Answer: b) Kanishka

81. The Fourth Buddhist Council was held in:
    a) Pataliputra
    b) Vaisali
    c) Kashmir
    d) Rajagriha
    Answer: c) Kashmir

82. Who was the President of the Fourth Buddhist Council?
    a) Moggliputta Tissa
    b) Mahakassapa
    c) Vasumitra
    d) Aswvaghosha
    Answer: c) Vasumitra

83. Who helped Vasumitra at the Fourth Buddhist Council?
    a) Sabakami
    b) Moggliputta Tissa
    c) Aswvaghosha
    d) Ananda
    Answer: c) Aswvaghosha

84. The Fourth Buddhist Council resulted in the division of Buddhists into:
    a) Digambaras and Shvetambaras
    b) Sthaviravadins and Mahasanghikas
    c) Mahayanists and Hinayanists
    d) Pasupata and Kalamukha
    Answer: c) Mahayanists and Hinayanists

SECTS OF BUDDHISM

85. Which Buddhist sect believes in the original teachings of Buddha and sought individual salvation through self-discipline and meditation?
    a) Mahayana
    b) Vajrayana
    c) Hinayana
    d) Zen
    Answer: c) Hinayana

86. Which Buddhist sect does NOT believe in idol worship?
    a) Mahayana
    b) Hinayana
    c) Vajrayana
    d) Both Mahayana and Hinayana
    Answer: b) Hinayana

87. Which Buddhist sect is described as a religion without God, with Karma taking the place of God?
    a) Mahayana
    b) Hinayana
    c) Vajrayana
    d) All Buddhist sects
    Answer: b) Hinayana

88. The oldest school of Hinayana Buddhism is:
    a) Mahasanghikas
    b) Sthaviravada (Theravada)
    c) Sarvastivada
    d) Sautrantika
    Answer: b) Sthaviravada (Theravada)

89. Which Buddhist sect believes in the heavenliness of Buddha and sought salvation of all through the grace and help of Buddha and Bodhisattvas?
    a) Hinayana
    b) Vajrayana
    c) Mahayana
    d) Sthaviravada
    Answer: c) Mahayana

90. Which Buddhist sect believes in idol worship?
    a) Hinayana
    b) Mahayana
    c) Both Hinayana and Mahayana
    d) Neither Hinayana nor Mahayana
    Answer: b) Mahayana

91. Which Buddhist sect believes that salvation could be best attained by acquiring magical power, called Vajra?
    a) Hinayana
    b) Mahayana
    c) Vajrayana
    d) Sthaviravada
    Answer: c) Vajrayana

92. The chief divinities of the Vajrayana sect were the:
    a) Bodhisattvas
    b) Taras
    c) Devas
    d) Buddhas
    Answer: b) Taras

93. Vajrayana Buddhism became popular in which region of India?
    a) Western India
    b) Southern India
    c) Northern India
    d) Eastern India, particularly Bengal and Bihar
    Answer: d) Eastern India, particularly Bengal and Bihar

Buddhist Universities/Monasteries and Founders

94. Nalanda Buddhist monastery was founded by:
    a) Dharmapala
    b) Kumargupta-I
    c) Gopala
    d) Bhattarka
    Answer: b) Kumargupta-I

95. Vikramshila Buddhist monastery was founded by which Pala ruler?
    a) Gopala
    b) Dharmapala
    c) Ramapala
    d) Kumargupta-I
    Answer: b) Dharmapala

96. Somapuri Buddhist monastery was founded by which Pala ruler?
    a) Gopala
    b) Dharmapala
    c) Ramapala
    d) Bhattarka
    Answer: b) Dharmapala

97. Jagadal Buddhist monastery in Bengal was founded by which Pala ruler?
    a) Gopala
    b) Dharmapala
    c) Ramapala
    d) Kumargupta-I
    Answer: c) Ramapala

98. Odantpuri Buddhist monastery in Bihar was founded by which Pala ruler?
    a) Dharmapala
    b) Ramapala
    c) Gopala
    d) Bhattarka
    Answer: c) Gopala

99. Vallabhi Buddhist monastery in Gujarat was founded by:
    a) Dharmapala
    b) Gopala
    c) Bhattarka (Maitrak Rule)
    d) Kumargupta-I
    Answer: c) Bhattarka (Maitrak Rule)

BUDDHIST LITERATURE

100. The Buddhist scriptures in Pali are commonly referred to as:
    a) Angas
    b) Pitakas
    c) Tripitaka
    d) Nikayas
    Answer: c) Tripitaka

101. Tripitaka includes which of the following?
    a) Vinaya Pitaka, Sutta Pitaka, Abhidhamma Pitaka
    b) Anguttara Nikaya, Digh Nikaya, Majjhima Nikaya
    c) Jataka tales, Mahavastu, Lalitavistara
    d) Milindapanho, Buddhacharita, Saundarananda
    Answer: a) Vinaya Pitaka, Sutta Pitaka, Abhidhamma Pitaka

102. The Vinayapitaka primarily deals with:
    a) Discourses of Buddha
    b) Profound philosophy
    c) Rules and regulations for monks and the Sangha
    d) Stories of Buddha's previous lives
    Answer: c) Rules and regulations for monks and the Sangha

103. The Sutta Pitaka is divided into how many Nikayas?
    a) Three
    b) Four
    c) Five
    d) Six
    Answer: c) Five

104. Which of the following is NOT one of the five Nikayas of the Sutta Pitaka?
    a) Digh Nikaya
    b) Khandakas Nikaya
    c) Majjhima Nikaya
    d) Anguttara Nikaya
    Answer: b) Khandakas Nikaya

105. The Sutta Pitaka consists chiefly of:
    a) Rules for monastic discipline
    b) Discourses delivered by Buddha himself
    c) Philosophical questions and answers
    d) Biographical accounts of monks
    Answer: b) Discourses delivered by Buddha himself

106. The Abhidhamma Pitaka is written in the form of:
    a) Poetry
    b) Narratives
    c) Questions and Answers
    d) Ritual instructions
    Answer: c) Questions and Answers

107. What does the Abhidhamma Pitaka contain?
    a) Rules for Vinaya
    b) Discourses of Buddha
    c) Profound philosophy of the Buddha’s teachings
    d) Jataka stories
    Answer: c) Profound philosophy of the Buddha’s teachings

BUDDHIST ARCHITECTURE

108. Buddhist architecture essentially developed in how many forms?
    a) Two
    b) Three
    c) Four
    d) Five
    Answer: b) Three

109. Which form of Buddhist architecture is a prayer hall?
    a) Stupa
    b) Chaitya
    c) Vihara
    d) Mandala
    Answer: b) Chaitya

110. Which form of Buddhist architecture refers to the residence for monks?
    a) Stupa
    b) Chaitya
    c) Vihara
    d) Gompa
    Answer: c) Vihara

111. Stupas are structures where:
    a) Monks reside
    b) Prayers are held
    c) Relics of the Buddha or prominent Buddhist monks are preserved
    d) Buddhist texts are written
    Answer: c) Relics of the Buddha or some prominent Buddhist monks are preserved

JAINISM

112. Jainism was founded by:
    a) Rishabha
    b) Parsavanath
    c) Vardhamana Mahavira
    d) Siddhartha
    Answer: c) Vardhamana Mahavira

113. Mahavira was born in:
    a) 563 B.C.
    b) 540 B.C.
    c) 483 B.C.
    d) 383 B.C.
    Answer: b) 540 B.C.

114. Mahavira’s birth place was:
    a) Sarnath
    b) Lumbini
    c) Kundagram in Vaishali
    d) Pataliputra
    Answer: c) Kundagram in Vaishali

115. Mahavira belonged to which Kshatriya clan?
    a) Sakya
    b) Kuru
    c) Jantriak
    d) Lichchavi
    Answer: c) Jantriak

116. What was the name of Mahavira’s father?
    a) Chetak
    b) Suddhodana
    c) Siddhartha
    d) Rahul
    Answer: c) Siddhartha

117. What was the name of Mahavira’s mother, who was a sister of the Lichchavi prince Chetak?
    a) Yashoda
    b) Gautami
    c) Mahamaya
    d) Trishala
    Answer: d) Trishala

118. Mahavira was married to:
    a) Yashoda
    b) Kundalini
    c) Devadatta
    d) Mayadevi
    Answer: a) Yashoda

119. At what age did Mahavira leave home?
    a) 25
    b) 29
    c) 30
    d) 35
    Answer: c) 30

120. Mahavira is regarded as which Tirthankara?
    a) 1st
    b) 23rd
    c) 24th
    d) 25th
    Answer: c) 24th

121. Who was the 1st Tirthankara?
    a) Mahavira
    b) Parsavanath
    c) Rishabha
    d) Arishtanemi
    Answer: c) Rishabha

122. Who was the 23rd Tirthankara?
    a) Rishabha
    b) Parsavanath
    c) Mahavira
    d) Ajitanatha
    Answer: b) Parsavanath

123. Who was Mahavira’s first disciple?
    a) Ananda
    b) Jamali
    c) Mokhliputta
    d) Bhadrabahu
    Answer: b) Jamali

124. Mahavira attained Kaivalya on the bank of which river?
    a) Niranjana
    b) Ganga
    c) Rijupalika
    d) Yamuna
    Answer: c) Rijupalika

125. Mahavira met Mokhliputta, the founder of which sect, during his wanderings?
    a) Buddhist
    b) Ajivika
    c) Saiva
    d) Vaishnava
    Answer: b) Ajivika

126. Which grandson of Ashoka is said to have accepted Jainism?
    a) Dasharatha
    b) Samparati
    c) Kunala
    d) Dasaratha
    Answer: b) Samparati

127. In the first century A.D., which city became the center of Jaina art and culture?
    a) Pataliputra
    b) Vaishali
    c) Mathura
    d) Shravanabelagola
    Answer: c) Mathura

128. The spread of Jainism in Karnataka is attributed to whom?
    a) Ashoka
    b) Chandragupta Maurya
    c) Mahavira
    d) Bhadrabahu
    Answer: b) Chandragupta Maurya

129. Under whose leadership did many Jaina followers go to the South during a severe famine?
    a) Sthalabahu
    b) Jamali
    c) Bhadrabahu
    d) Mokhliputta
    Answer: c) Bhadrabahu

130. Those Jaina followers who went to the South under Bhadrabahu later formed which sect?
    a) Shvetambar
    b) Digambara
    c) Sthaviravada
    d) Mahasanghika
    Answer: b) Digambara

131. Those Jaina followers who remained at Magadha under the leadership of Sthalabahu formed which sect?
    a) Digambara
    b) Shvetambar
    c) Hinayana
    d) Mahayana
    Answer: b) Shvetambar

FIVE MAIN TEACHINGS of Jainism

132. Which of the following was added to the five main teachings of Jainism by Mahavira?
    a) Non-injury (ahimsa)
    b) Non-lying
    c) Non-stealing (asateya)
    d) Observe continence (Brahamcharya)
    Answer: d) Observe continence (Brahamcharya)

JAINA LITERATURE

133. Most of the Jaina literature is written in which language?
    a) Sanskrit
    b) Pali
    c) Prakrit
    d) Apabhramsha
    Answer: c) Prakrit

134. Which of the following is part of Jaina Literature?
    a) Tripitaka
    b) Vedas
    c) The 12 Angas
    d) Puranas
    Answer: c) The 12 Angas

JAINA COUNCILS

135. When was the First Jaina Council held?
    a) In the beginning of the 3rd century AD
    b) In the beginning of the 3rd century BC
    c) In the 6th century AD
    d) In the 5th century BC
    Answer: b) In the beginning of the 3rd century BC

136. Where was the First Jaina Council held?
    a) Valabhi
    b) Mathura
    c) Pataliputra
    d) Shravanabelagola
    Answer: c) Pataliputra

137. Who presided over the First Jaina Council?
    a) Bhadrabahu
    b) Devaradhi Kshamasramana
    c) Sthulabahu
    d) Mahavira
    Answer: c) Sthulabahu

138. The First Jaina Council resulted in the compilation of:
    a) 12 Upangas
    b) 12 Angas
    c) 14 Purvas
    d) 6 Chhedsutras
    Answer: b) 12 Angas

139. When was the Second Jaina Council held?
    a) In the 3rd century BC
    b) In the 1st century AD
    c) In the 6th (513 AD) century AD
    d) In the 7th century AD
    Answer: c) In the 6th (513 AD) century AD

140. Where was the Second Jaina Council held?
    a) Pataliputra
    b) Mathura
    c) Valabhi
    d) Sravasti
    Answer: c) Valabhi

141. Who led the Second Jaina Council?
    a) Sthulabahu
    b) Bhadrabahu
    c) Devaradhi Kshamasramana
    d) Jamali
    Answer: c) Devaradhi Kshamasramana

142. The Second Jaina Council resulted in the final compilation of:
    a) 14 Purvas
    b) 12 Angas only
    c) 12 Angas and 12 Upangas
    d) 10 Prakirna
    Answer: c) 12 Angas and 12 Upangas

Digambaras and Shvetambaras

143. The division of Jains into Digambaras and Shvetambaras occurred during the reign of which king?
    a) Ashoka
    b) Chandragupta Maurya
    c) Kanishka
    d) Harshavardhana
    Answer: b) Chandragupta Maurya

144. Which leader led some Jains to the Deccan during a severe famine, leading to the formation of the Digambara sect?
    a) Sthalabhadra
    b) Bhadrabahu
    c) Jamali
    d) Mokhliputta
    Answer: b) Bhadrabahu

145. Which leader remained in North India and allowed the wearing of white garments, leading to the formation of the Shvetambar sect?
    a) Bhadrabahu
    b) Sthalabhadra
    c) Mahavira
    d) Chandragupta Maurya
    Answer: b) Sthalabhadra

Similarities between Buddhism and Jainism

146. Which of the following is a similarity between Buddhism and Jainism mentioned in the text?
    a) Both believed in idol worship.
    b) Both supported the Brahmanical domination.
    c) Both upheld the essence of Vedas.
    d) Both were confined to India.
    Answer: c) Both upheld the essence of Vedas.

147. Which text provides rules and regulations for Jain Bhikshus?
    a) Kalpasutra
    b) Bhagavati Sutra
    c) Aacharangsutra
    d) Tattvarthasutra
    Answer: c) Aacharangsutra

Differences between Buddhism and Jainism

148. Regarding salvation, Buddhism is described as:
    a) Extreme
    b) Moderate
    c) Not concerned with salvation
    d) Achieving salvation through magical power
    Answer: b) Moderate

149. In terms of "Ahimsa", Jainism had a:
    a) Liberal Policy
    b) Moderate Policy
    c) Over-emphasis
    d) No emphasis
    Answer: c) Over-emphasis

150. Which religion did NOT believe in the concept of a soul?
    a) Jainism
    b) Hinduism
    c) Buddhism
    d) Saivism
    Answer: c) Buddhism


RISE OF MAHAJANAPADAS

1.  Which Buddhist text gives the list of sixteen Mahajanapadas at the time of Buddha?
    a) Majjhima Nikaya
    b) Vinaya Pitaka
    c) Anguttara Nikaya
    d) Sutta Pitaka
    Answer: c) Anguttara Nikaya

2.  The Mahajanapadas extended from North western Pakistan to East Bihar and from the Himalayas to which river?
    a) Ganga
    b) Yamuna
    c) Godavari
    d) Indus
    Answer: c) Godavari

3.  What was the capital of the Anga Mahajanapada?
    a) Rajgriha
    b) Varanasi
    c) Champa
    d) Vaishali
    Answer: c) Champa

4.  Which region corresponds to the Magadha Mahajanapada?
    a) Bhagalpur, Munger (Bihar)
    b) Patna, Gaya (Bihar)
    c) Near Varanasi
    d) Eastern Uttar Pradesh
    Answer: b) Patna, Gaya (Bihar)

5.  The capital of the Kasi Mahajanapada was:
    a) Saketa
    b) Sravasti
    c) Varanasi
    d) Kaushambi
    Answer: c) Varanasi

6.  Kosal Mahajanapada had its capital at:
    a) Kusinara
    b) Pava
    c) Saketa, Sravasti
    d) Suktimati
    Answer: c) Saketa, Sravasti

7.  Vajji (Ganasangha) had its capital at:
    a) Muzaffarpur
    b) Vaishali
    c) Gorakhpur
    d) Allahabad
    Answer: b) Vaishali

8.  The Malla Mahajanapada had its capitals at:
    a) Kaushambi
    b) Ahicchatra
    c) Kusinara/Pava
    d) Mathura
    Answer: c) Kusinara/Pava

9.  The Chedi Mahajanapada was located in:
    a) Eastern Uttar Pradesh
    b) Bundelkhand (U.P.)
    c) Meerut Delhi region
    d) Mathura (U.P.)
    Answer: b) Bundelkhand (U.P.)

10. The Vatsa Mahajanapada had its capital at:
    a) Indraprastha
    b) Ahicchatra
    c) Kaushambi
    d) Kampilya
    Answer: c) Kaushambi

11. The Kuru Mahajanapada was located in:
    a) Western U.P.
    b) Mathura (U.P.)
    c) Meerut Delhi region
    d) Peshawar (Pakistan)
    Answer: c) Meerut Delhi region

12. The Panchal Mahajanapada had its capitals at:
    a) Taxila
    b) Rajpura
    c) Ahicchatra, Kampilya
    d) Paithan
    Answer: c) Ahicchatra, Kampilya

13. The capital of the Sursena Mahajanapada was:
    a) Ujjain
    b) Mahishmati
    c) Viratnagar
    d) Mathura
    Answer: d) Mathura

14. The Gandhar Mahajanapada had its capital at:
    a) Rajpura
    b) Paithan
    c) Taxila
    d) Ujjain
    Answer: c) Taxila

15. Kamboja Mahajanapada was located:
    a) In the Godavari area
    b) In the Malwa region
    c) Near Gandhar
    d) Near Jaipur
    Answer: c) Near Gandhar

16. The Asmak Mahajanapada was located in:
    a) Malwa region
    b) Near Jaipur
    c) Godavari area (M.H.)
    d) Near Varanasi
    Answer: c) Godavari area (M.H.)

17. The Avanti Mahajanapada had its capitals at:
    a) Viratnagar
    b) Ujjain, Mahishmati
    c) Champa
    d) Rajgriha
    Answer: b) Ujjain, Mahishmati

18. The Matsya Mahajanapada was located:
    a) Near Varanasi
    b) Eastern Uttar Pradesh
    c) Near Jaipur
    d) Gorakhpur
    Answer: c) Near Jaipur

19. Which Mahajanapada was famous for its cotton textiles and market for horses?
    a) Anga
    b) Kasi
    c) Kosal
    d) Vatsa
    Answer: b) Kasi

20. Which city was noted for its trade and Commerce?
    a) Varanasi
    b) Rajgriha
    c) Champa
    d) Vaishali
    Answer: c) Champa

21. Vajji represented a confederacy of how many clans?
    a) Six
    b) Seven
    c) Eight
    d) Nine
    Answer: c) Eight

22. The Buddha died in the vicinity of:
    a) Sarnath
    b) Bodh Gaya
    c) Kusinara
    d) Vaishali
    Answer: c) Kusinara

23. The Northern Panchalas had their capital at:
    a) Kampilya
    b) Ahicchatra
    c) Mathura
    d) Kaushambi
    Answer: b) Ahicchatra

24. The Southern Panchalas had their capital at:
    a) Ahicchatra
    b) Kampilya
    c) Mathura
    d) Kaushambi
    Answer: b) Kampilya

25. Which city was used as the hiding place by Pandavas?
    a) Indraprastha
    b) Viratnagar
    c) Hastinapura
    d) Mathura
    Answer: b) Viratnagar

26. Which city was located at the junction of two famous trade routes, Uttrapatha and Dakshinapatha?
    a) Varanasi
    b) Pataliputra
    c) Mathura
    d) Ujjain
    Answer: c) Mathura

27. Which Mahajanapada's people were regarded as uncultured by the Brahmanical texts?
    a) Mallas
    b) Kambojas
    c) Chedis
    d) Kurus
    Answer: b) Kambojas

28. In which text does the Buddha call himself Kosalan?
    a) Anguttara Nikaya
    b) Vinaya Pitaka
    c) Majjhima Nikaya
    d) Digh Nikaya
    Answer: c) Majjhima Nikaya

29. By the time of Buddha, which city had emerged as a cloth manufacturing centre?
    a) Champa
    b) Varanasi (Kashi)
    c) Mathura
    d) Sravasti
    Answer: b) Kashi

RISE OF MAGADHA

HARYANK DYNASTY

30. Which epic has provided information about the early period of Magadha?
    a) Ramayana
    b) Mahabharata
    c) Puranas
    d) Jataka tales
    Answer: b) Mahabharata

31. Who were the rulers during the period of Mahabharata in Magadha?
    a) Bimbisara and Ajatshatru
    b) Jarasandh and Brihdrath
    c) Udayin and Nagdasak
    d) Mahapadmananda and Dhanananda
    Answer: b) Jarasandh and Brihdrath

32. What was the Magadhan capital during the early period, as mentioned in the text?
    a) Rajgir
    b) Patliputra
    c) Girivraja
    d) Vaishali
    Answer: c) Girivraja

BIMBISARA (544-492 B.C.)

33. Who established the Haryank dynasty?
    a) Ajatshatru
    b) Udayin
    c) Bimbisara
    d) Nagdasak
    Answer: c) Bimbisara

34. What was the capital of Bimbisara's state?
    a) Pataliputra
    b) Vaishali
    c) Rajgir
    d) Girivraja
    Answer: c) Rajgir

35. When did Bimbisara ascend the throne?
    a) 492 B.C.
    b) 544 B.C.
    c) 460 B.C.
    d) 412 B.C.
    Answer: b) 544 B.C.

36. How many wives did Bimbisara have, as mentioned in the text?
    a) Two
    b) Three
    c) Four
    d) Five
    Answer: b) Three

37. Mahakosala, one of Bimbisara's wives, was the sister of which Kosal ruler?
    a) Ajatshatru
    b) Prasenjit
    c) Udayin
    d) Kalashoka
    Answer: b) Prasenjit

38. Which Mahajanapada did Bimbisara defeat and annex?
    a) Kasi
    b) Vajji
    c) Anga
    d) Avanti
    Answer: c) Anga

39. Bimbisara was a contemporary of:
    a) Mahavira
    b) Alexander
    c) Buddha
    d) Chandragupta Maurya
    Answer: c) Buddha

40. Bimbisara sent his physician Jivak to treat Chand Pradyot of which kingdom?
    a) Kasi
    b) Kosal
    c) Avanti
    d) Vatsa
    Answer: c) Avanti

41. By whom was Bimbisara imprisoned and later died?
    a) Chand Pradyot
    b) Prasenjit
    c) His son Ajatshatru
    d) His minister Vassakar
    Answer: c) His son Ajatshatru

AJATSHATRU (492-460 B.C.)

42. When did Ajatshatru sit on the Magadha throne?
    a) 544 B.C.
    b) 492 B.C.
    c) 460 B.C.
    d) 412 B.C.
    Answer: b) 492 B.C.

43. Which policy did Ajatshatru follow?
    a) Conciliatory policy
    b) Expansionist policy
    c) Non-aggression policy
    d) Isolationist policy
    Answer: b) Expansionist policy

44. Which confederacy did Ajatshatru defeat after a long struggle?
    a) Malla
    b) Kuru
    c) Vajji
    d) Panchal
    Answer: c) Vajji

45. Ajatshatru took the services of his minister to divide the Vajji confederacy. What was his minister's name?
    a) Chanakya
    b) Vassakar
    c) Kautilya
    d) Jivak
    Answer: b) Vassakar

46. Where did Ajatshatru get a large stupa constructed?
    a) Vaishali
    b) Pataliputra
    c) Rajgriha
    d) Sarnath
    Answer: c) Rajgriha

47. During whose reign did Gautama Buddha die, and the first Buddhist council was arranged?
    a) Bimbisara
    b) Ajatshatru
    c) Udayin
    d) Kalashoka
    Answer: b) Ajatshatru

48. Ajatshatru constructed a fort around Rajgriha to protect it from which attack?
    a) Avanti
    b) Kosal
    c) Lichchavi
    d) Kasi
    Answer: c) Lichchavi

49. By whom was Ajatshatru killed?
    a) His minister Vassakar
    b) His son Udayin
    c) Chand Pradyot
    d) Prasenjit
    Answer: b) His son Udayin

50. Who transferred the Capital to Patliputra?
    a) Bimbisara
    b) Ajatshatru
    c) Udayin
    d) Nagdasak
    Answer: c) Udayin

51. Udayin was a follower of:
    a) Buddhism
    b) Jainism
    c) Saivism
    d) Vaishnavism
    Answer: b) Jainism

52. Who was the last Haryank ruler?
    a) Udayin
    b) Ajatshatru
    c) Nagdasak
    d) Bimbisara
    Answer: c) Nagdasak

SHISUNAGA DYNASTY (412-344 B.C.)

53. Who killed Nagdasak and established the Shisunaga dynasty?
    a) Kalashoka
    b) Shisunaga
    c) Nandivardhan
    d) Mahapadmananda
    Answer: b) Shisunaga

54. When was the Shisunaga dynasty established?
    a) 460 B.C.
    b) 412 B.C.
    c) 344 B.C.
    d) 323 B.C.
    Answer: b) 412 B.C.

55. Which kingdom did Shisunaga annex to Magadha?
    a) Kasi
    b) Kosal
    c) Vajji
    d) Avanti
    Answer: d) Avanti

56. Shisunaga established his capital at:
    a) Rajgir
    b) Pataliputra
    c) Vaishali
    d) Girivraja
    Answer: c) Vaishali

57. When did Kalashoka come to the throne?
    a) 412 B.C.
    b) 344 B.C.
    c) 323 B.C.
    d) 289 B.C.
    Answer: b) 344 B.C.

58. Kalashoka again transferred the capital to:
    a) Rajgir
    b) Vaishali
    c) Pataliputra
    d) Ujjain
    Answer: c) Pataliputra

59. During whose reign was the second Buddhist council organized at Vaishali?
    a) Shisunaga
    b) Kalashoka
    c) Nandivardhan
    d) Mahapadmananda
    Answer: b) Kalashoka

60. Who was the last Shisunaga ruler?
    a) Shisunaga
    b) Kalashoka
    c) Nandivardhan
    d) Nagdasak
    Answer: c) Nandivardhan

NANDA DYNASTY (344-323 B.C.)

61. Who established the Nanda Dynasty?
    a) Dhanananda
    b) Mahapadmananda
    c) Chandragupta Maurya
    d) Chanakya
    Answer: b) Mahapadmananda

62. According to Puranas, Mahapadmananda was not a:
    a) Brahmana
    b) Vaishya
    c) Kshatriya
    d) Shudra
    Answer: c) Kshatriya

63. Mahapadmananda was known as:
    a) Amitraghata
    b) Devanampiya Priyadarshi
    c) Ekarat and Sarvakshatrantaka
    d) Simhasen
    Answer: c) Ekarat and Sarvakshatrantaka

64. Which kingdom did Mahapadmananda conquer?
    a) Avanti
    b) Kosal
    c) Kalinga
    d) Gandhara
    Answer: c) Kalinga

65. Who was the last Nanda ruler?
    a) Mahapadmananda
    b) Nandivardhan
    c) Dhanananda
    d) Chandragupta Maurya
    Answer: c) Dhanananda

66. Dhanananda was the contemporary of:
    a) Buddha
    b) Mahavira
    c) Alexander
    d) Ashoka
    Answer: c) Alexander

67. Alexander attacked India in the reign of which Nanda ruler?
    a) Mahapadmananda
    b) Nandivardhan
    c) Dhanananda
    d) Shisunaga
    Answer: c) Dhanananda

68. The Nandas have been termed as:
    a) The first traders of India
    b) The first empire builders of India
    c) The greatest patrons of Jainism
    d) The earliest rulers of Magadha
    Answer: b) The first empire builders of India

69. By whom was the Nanda Dynasty destroyed?
    a) Alexander
    b) Darius
    c) Chandragupta Maurya and Chanakya
    d) Porus
    Answer: c) Chandragupta Maurya and Chanakya

FACTORS FOR THE RISE OF MAGADHA

70. Which of the following was a key factor for the rise of Magadha?
    a) Its location in the mountainous region.
    b) Abundance of gold deposits.
    c) Advantageous geographical position.
    d) Lack of foreign invasions.
    Answer: c) Advantageous geographical position.

71. Iron deposits were situated close to which early capital of Magadha?
    a) Pataliputra
    b) Vaishali
    c) Rajgir
    d) Champa
    Answer: c) Rajgir

72. Pataliputra was called as 'jaladurga' because it was surrounded by:
    a) Mountains
    b) Forests
    c) Rivers from all sides
    d) Walls
    Answer: c) Rivers from all sides

73. Magadha lay at the center of the:
    a) Western Gangetic plain
    b) Eastern Gangetic plain
    c) Middle Gangetic plain
    d) Southern Gangetic plain
    Answer: c) Middle Gangetic plain

74. What new military resource did Magadha use on a large scale in wars for the first time?
    a) Chariots
    b) Cavalry
    c) Elephants
    d) Archery
    Answer: c) Elephants

FOREIGN INVASION

75. The period of the 6th century B.C. was marked by political instability in:
    a) South India
    b) Eastern India
    c) North-West India
    d) Central India
    Answer: c) North-West India

76. In 516 B.C., which Iranian ruler penetrated into North-West India?
    a) Cyrus
    b) Darius
    c) Xerxes
    d) Artaxerxes
    Answer: b) Darius

77. Darius annexed which regions, constituting the 20th kshatrapy (province) of Iran?
    a) Bengal and Bihar
    b) Gujarat and Rajasthan
    c) Punjab (west of Indus) and Sindh
    d) Malwa and Avanti
    Answer: c) Punjab (west of Indus) and Sindh

78. Which script did the Iranian Scribes bring into India?
    a) Brahmi script
    b) Kharoshti script
    c) Greek script
    d) Aramaic script
    Answer: b) Kharoshti script

79. Under the leadership of whom did the Greeks destroy the Iranian Empire?
    a) Cyrus
    b) Darius
    c) Alexander of Macedonia
    d) Seleucus Nicator
    Answer: c) Alexander of Macedonia

80. Through which pass did Alexander march to India in 326 B.C.?
    a) Bolan Pass
    b) Gomal Pass
    c) Khyber Pass
    d) Shipki La
    Answer: c) Khyber Pass

81. Who was the king of Taxila when Alexander invaded India?
    a) Porus
    b) Ambhi
    c) Chandragupta
    d) Dhanananda
    Answer: b) Ambhi

82. Who ruled between the Jhelum and Ravi rivers and provided strong resistance to Alexander?
    a) Ambhi
    b) Porus
    c) Chandragupta Maurya
    d) Dhanananda
    Answer: b) Porus

83. The Battle of Hydaspes was fought between:
    a) Alexander and Ambhi
    b) Alexander and Porus
    c) Alexander and Dhanananda
    d) Porus and Ambhi
    Answer: b) Porus and Alexander

84. Alexander’s invasion paved the way for the expansion of which empire in North West India?
    a) Nanda Empire
    b) Persian Empire
    c) Mauryan Empire
    d) Gupta Empire
    Answer: c) Mauryan Empire

Effects of Alexander’s Invasion

85. Alexander’s invasion exposed India to Europe by opening up how many distinct lines of communication?
    a) Two
    b) Three
    c) Four
    d) Five
    Answer: c) Four

86. What kind of school of art came up in Gandhara due to cultural contacts from Alexander's invasion?
    a) Indigenous school
    b) Cosmopolitan school
    c) Persian school
    d) Greek school
    Answer: b) Cosmopolitan school

87. Alexander's invasion paved the way for the unification of North India under Chandragupta by:
    a) Directly conquering all states
    b) Weakening small states
    c) Introducing new military strategies
    d) Promoting a unified religion
    Answer: b) Weakening small states

THE MAURYAN EMPIRE (321 BC - 289 BC)

CHANDRAGUPTA MAURYA (322-295 B.C)
