88. The Mauryan dynasty was founded by Chandragupta Maurya with the help of:
    a) Megasthenes
    b) Seleucus
    c) Chanakya or Kautilya
    d) Bindusara
    Answer: c) Chanakya or Kautilya

89. According to Brahmanical texts, Chandragupta Maurya was born of Mura, a Sudra woman in the court of the:
    a) Haryanks
    b) Shisunagas
    c) Nandas
    d) Magadhas
    Answer: c) Nandas

90. An earlier Buddhist tradition says Chandragupta Maurya belonged to the:
    a) Lichchavi Kshatriya clan
    b) Jantriak Kshatriya clan
    c) Sakya Kshatriya clan
    d) Moriya Kshatriya clan
    Answer: d) Moriya Kshatriya clan

91. When did Chandragupta Maurya fight Seleucus?
    a) 326 B.C.
    b) 322 B.C.
    c) 305 B.C.
    d) 298 B.C.
    Answer: c) 305 B.C.

92. Seleucus surrendered a large territory to Chandragupta Maurya in return for:
    a) Gold and silver
    b) Military alliance
    c) 500 elephants
    d) Marriage alliance
    Answer: c) 500 elephants

93. According to Jaina tradition, Chandragupta Maurya was converted to:
    a) Buddhism
    b) Vaishnavism
    c) Jainism
    d) Saivism
    Answer: c) Jainism

94. Where did Chandragupta Maurya pass his last days and die by starvation (Sallekhana/Santhara)?
    a) Pataliputra
    b) Taxila
    c) Sravanbelagola near Mysore
    d) Ujjain
    Answer: c) Sravanbelagola near Mysore

95. Who did Seleucus send as an ambassador to the Mauryan court?
    a) Deimachus
    b) Dionysius
    c) Megasthenes
    d) Ptolemy
    Answer: c) Megasthenes

96. Megasthenes wrote the text:
    a) Arthashastra
    b) Mudrarakshasa
    c) Indica
    d) Ashtadyayi
    Answer: c) ‘Indica’

BINDUSARA (298-272 B.C.)

97. Who succeeded Chandragupta Maurya?
    a) Ashoka
    b) Bindusara
    c) Kautilya
    d) Seleucus
    Answer: b) Bindusara

98. In Jain literature, Bindusara has been called as:
    a) Amitraghata
    b) Devanampiya
    c) Simhasen
    d) Piyadassi
    Answer: c) ‘Simhasen’

99. Which ambassador did Antiochus send to Bindusara’s court?
    a) Megasthenes
    b) Dionysius
    c) Deimachus
    d) Ptolemy
    Answer: c) Deimachus

100. Ptolemy Philedelphus of Egypt sent which ambassador to Bindusara’s court?
    a) Deimachus
    b) Megasthenes
    c) Dionysius
    d) Seleucus
    Answer: c) Dionysius

101. By what name was Bindusara also known as, meaning 'slayer of enemies'?
    a) Simhasen
    b) Amitraghata / Amitrochates
    c) Piyadassi
    d) Devanampiya
    Answer: b) Amitraghata / Amitrochates

102. Bindusara sent his son Ashoka to quell a rebellion in:
    a) Ujjain
    b) Pataliputra
    c) Kalinga
    d) Taxila
    Answer: d) Taxila

103. Bindusara was a follower of which sect?
    a) Buddhism
    b) Jainism
    c) Ajivika sect
    d) Saivism
    Answer: c) Ajivika sect

ASHOKA (273-232 B.C.)

104. When did Ashoka succeed Bindusara?
    a) 298 B.C.
    b) 273 B.C.
    c) 260 B.C.
    d) 232 B.C.
    Answer: b) 273 B.C.

105. Ashoka was holding viceroyalty of which two places during his father's lifetime?
    a) Pataliputra and Rajgir
    b) Taxila and Ujjain
    c) Kalinga and Magadha
    d) Gandhara and Avanti
    Answer: b) Taxila and Ujjain

106. In which year did Ashoka fight the Kalinga war?
    a) 273 B.C.
    b) 260 B.C.
    c) 250 B.C.
    d) 232 B.C.
    Answer: b) 260 B.C.

107. The Kalinga war was fought in which year of Ashoka's reign?
    a) 8th year
    b) 9th year
    c) 10th year
    d) 13th year
    Answer: b) 9th year

108. Under Ashoka, the empire reached its zenith and for the first time, the entire Indian sub-continent came under a single umbrella, with the exception of:
    a) Eastern India
    b) Western India
    c) Extreme Southern India
    d) North Western India
    Answer: c) Extreme Southern India

109. Ashoka banned animal sacrifice and regulated the slaughter of animals for food as per which major rock edict?
    a) 13th major rock edict
    b) 1st major rock edict
    c) 7th major rock edict
    d) 9th major rock edict
    Answer: b) 1st major rock edict

110. Which rock edict mentions the Kalinga war?
    a) 1st rock edict
    b) 9th rock edict
    c) 13th rock edict
    d) 7th rock edict
    Answer: c) 13th rock edict

111. According to his Maski and Gurjara inscriptions, Ashoka was known as:
    a) Amitraghata
    b) Simhasen
    c) Devanam Priya Priyadarshi
    d) Ekarat
    Answer: c) Devanam Priya Priyadarshi

112. Ashoka was converted to Buddhism by:
    a) Upagupta
    b) Nigrodh
    c) Moggaliputta Tissa
    d) Mahakassapa
    Answer: b) Nigrodh

Location of Major Rock Edicts

113. Which of the following Major Rock Edicts is located in Gujarat?
    a) Kalshi
    b) Manshera
    c) Shahbazgarhi
    d) Girnar
    Answer: d) Girnar

114. Which two Major Rock Edicts are located in Odisha?
    a) Kalshi and Manshera
    b) Shahbazgarhi and Girnar
    c) Dhauli and Jaugada
    d) Maski and Yerragudi
    Answer: c) Dhauli and Jaugada

115. Which Major Rock Edict is located near Mumbai?
    a) Maski
    b) Yerragudi
    c) Sopara
    d) Dhauli
    Answer: c) Sopara

FACTS ABOUT ASHOKAN INSCRIPTIONS

116. Which is the only edict that mentions the name Ashoka, discovered in 1915?
    a) Gurjara edict
    b) Maski edict
    c) Bairat inscription
    d) Kandhar inscription
    Answer: b) Maski edict

117. Which two pillars were brought to Delhi by Feroz Shah Tughlaq?
    a) Allahabad and Kausambi
    b) Delhi-Topra and Delhi-Meerut
    c) Lauriya-Araraj and Lauriya-Nandangarh
    d) Rampurva and Nigalisagar
    Answer: b) Delhi-Topra and Delhi-Meerut

118. Who deciphered Ashokan edicts in 1837?
    a) Alexander Cunningham
    b) James Princep
    c) John Marshall
    d) Sir Mortimer Wheeler
    Answer: b) James Princep

119. Which pillar was brought to Calcutta by Jahangir?
    a) Delhi-Topra
    b) Delhi-Meerut
    c) The Kausambi pillar
    d) Allahabad pillar
    Answer: c) The Kausambi pillar

120. The Bairat inscription was brought to Allahabad by:
    a) Jahangir
    b) Feroz Shah Tughlaq
    c) Cunningham
    d) James Princep
    Answer: c) Cunningham

121. Which two major rock edicts are in Kharoshti script?
    a) Girnar and Sopara
    b) Dhauli and Jaugada
    c) Mansehra and Sahbazgarhi
    d) Maski and Yerragudi
    Answer: c) Mansehra and Sahbazgarhi

122. The Kandhar inscription is bilingual in which two languages/scripts?
    a) Prakrit and Brahmi
    b) Greek and Aramaic
    c) Kharoshti and Greek
    d) Prakrit and Greek
    Answer: b) Greek and Aramaic

123. Most of the Ashokan edicts are written in which script?
    a) Kharoshti
    b) Greek
    c) Aramaic
    d) Brahmi script
    Answer: d) Brahmi script

124. The language used in Ashokan edicts, except Kandhar, is:
    a) Sanskrit
    b) Pali
    c) Prakrit
    d) Ardhamagadhi
    Answer: c) Prakrit

125. The inscription in fragmentary condition found at Lampak/Lamghan is in which script?
    a) Brahmi
    b) Greek
    c) Aramaic
    d) Kharoshti
    Answer: c) Aramaic

126. In all the edicts except the Maski edict, Ashoka refers to himself by which title?
    a) Amitraghata
    b) Simhasen
    c) Devanampiya Piyadassi
    d) Ekarat
    Answer: c) Devanampiya Piyadassi

127. The Allahabad pillar contains the inscription of which two later rulers in addition to Ashoka's?
    a) Akbar and Shah Jahan
    b) Samudra Gupta and Jehangir
    c) Bindusara and Chandragupta Maurya
    d) Darius and Alexander
    Answer: b) Samudra Gupta and Jehangir

128. How many major rock edicts are there?
    a) Ten
    b) Twelve
    c) Fourteen
    d) Sixteen
    Answer: c) Fourteen

129. What is the total number of pillar edicts mentioned in the text?
    a) Seven
    b) Ten
    c) Thirteen
    d) Fourteen
    Answer: c) Thirteen

130. How many major pillar edicts are there?
    a) Four
    b) Six
    c) Seven
    d) Ten
    Answer: c) Seven

131. The Queen’s edict is located on which pillar?
    a) Delhi-Topra pillar
    b) Delhi-Meerut pillar
    c) Allahabad pillar
    d) Lauriya-Nandangarh pillar
    Answer: c) Allahabad pillar

132. The commemorative pillar edicts are:
    a) Allahabad and Delhi-Topra
    b) Rumeindei and Nigalisagar
    c) Lauriya-Araraj and Lauriya-Nandangarh
    d) Rampurva and Delhi-Meerut
    Answer: b) Rumeindei and Nigalisagar

133. Which is the longest among the major rock edicts?
    a) 1st rock edict
    b) 7th rock edict
    c) 13th rock edict
    d) 9th rock edict
    Answer: c) 13th rock edict

134. Which is the longest among all the edicts (including pillar edicts)?
    a) 13th rock edict
    b) 7th pillar edict
    c) Queen's edict
    d) Rumeindei pillar edict
    Answer: b) 7th pillar edict

135. Kharosthi script was derived from:
    a) Greek
    b) Brahmi
    c) Persian Aramaic
    d) Sanskrit
    Answer: c) Persian Aramaic

136. Ashoka constructed the Sanchi Stupa at:
    a) Sarnath
    b) Bodh Gaya
    c) Vidisha (M.P.)
    d) Kushinagar
    Answer: c) Vidisha (M.P.)


POST-MAURYAN DYNASTIES

SUNGAS AND KANVAS

1.  Who laid the foundation of the Sunga dynasty?
    a) Brihdrath
    b) Patanjali
    c) Pushyamitra
    d) Devabhuti
    Answer: c) Pushyamitra

2.  Pushyamitra killed the last Mauryan ruler Brihdrath in which year?
    a) 185 A.D.
    b) 185 B.C.
    c) 544 B.C.
    d) 322 B.C.
    Answer: b) 185 B.C.

3.  Who among the following was a contemporary of Pushyamitra Sunga?
    a) Kalidasa
    b) Patanjali
    c) Charak
    d) Harisena
    Answer: b) Patanjali

4.  Malavikagnimitram was a work by which contemporary of Pushyamitra Sunga?
    a) Patanjali
    b) Kalidasa (Implicit, as Patanjali is mentioned as contemporary, and Malavikagnimitram is by Kalidasa, though the text only names Patanjali next to Malavikagnimitram).
    c) Charak
    d) Harisena
    Answer: b) Kalidasa

5.  Who was the last Sunga king?
    a) Pushyamitra
    b) Agnimitra
    c) Devabhuti
    d) Vasudeva
    Answer: c) Devabhuti

THE SATVAHANA DYNASTY

6.  According to the Puranas, they speak only of which rule, not Satvahana rule?
    a) Andhra
    b) Chalukya
    c) Pallava
    d) Chola
    Answer: a) Andhra

7.  Who was the first Satvahana king?
    a) Gautamiputra Satkarni
    b) Hala
    c) Simuka
    d) Pulumavi
    Answer: c) Simuka

8.  Satvahanas mostly issued coins of which metal?
    a) Gold
    b) Silver
    c) Copper
    d) Lead
    Answer: d) Lead

9.  The Satvahanas were the successors of the Mauryans in the Deccan and:
    a) North India
    b) Eastern India
    c) Central India
    d) South India
    Answer: c) Central India

10. Which Satvahana king called himself the "only Brahmana"?
    a) Simuka
    b) Hala
    c) Gautamiputra Satkarni
    d) Vasishthiputra Pulumavi
    Answer: c) Gautamiputra Satkarni

11. The achievements of Gautamiputra Satkarni are recorded in which inscription?
    a) Junagarh Inscription
    b) Aihole Inscription
    c) Nasik Inscription
    d) Prayag-Prasasti
    Answer: c) Nasik Inscription

12. The Nasik Inscription belongs to Gautamiputra Satkarni's mother named:
    a) Dhruvadevi
    b) Kubernaga
    c) Gautami Balasri
    d) Kundavai
    Answer: c) Gautami Balasri

13. The Satvahanas were the first rulers to make land grants to:
    a) Buddhists
    b) Jains
    c) Brahmanas
    d) Kshatriyas
    Answer: c) Brahmanas

14. The Prakrit text Gathasaptashati is attributed to which Satvahana king?
    a) Simuka
    b) Gautamiputra Satkarni
    c) Hala
    d) Pulumavi
    Answer: c) Hala

15. The Satvahanas had brisk trade with:
    a) Greeks
    b) Chinese
    c) Romans
    d) Persians
    Answer: c) Romans

16. Which two are important inscriptions of the Satvahana period?
    a) Junagarh and Aihole
    b) Nasik and Nanaghat
    c) Prayag-Prasasti and Mehrauli
    d) Bilsad and Karandanda
    Answer: b) Nasik and Nanaghat

THE INDO-GREEKS

17. A series of invasions from central Asia began around:
    a) 322 B.C.
    b) 200 B.C.
    c) 78 A.D.
    d) 320 A.D.
    Answer: b) 200 B.C.

18. Who were the first to cross the Hindukush into India?
    a) Sakas
    b) Kushans
    c) Indo-Greeks
    d) Hunas
    Answer: c) Indo-Greeks

19. The most famous Indo-Greek ruler was:
    a) Seleucus Nicator
    b) Demetrius
    c) Menander
    d) Antialcidas
    Answer: c) Menander

20. Menander's capital was Sakala, also known as:
    a) Taxila
    b) Sialkot
    c) Peshawar
    d) Mathura
    Answer: b) Sialkot

21. Menander was converted to Buddhism by whom?
    a) Ashvaghosha
    b) Nagarjuna (Nagasena)
    c) Vasubandhu
    d) Patanjali
    Answer: b) Nagasena or Nagarjuna

22. The conversation between Menander and Nagasena is recorded in a book named:
    a) Buddhacharita
    b) Milindapanho
    c) Abhidhamma Pitaka
    d) Jataka Tales
    Answer: b) ‘Milindapanho’

23. Who were the first to issue gold coins in India?
    a) Mauryans
    b) Satvahanas
    c) Indo-Greeks
    d) Kushans
    Answer: c) Indo-Greeks

24. Indo-Greeks were the first rulers in India to issue coins definitely attributed to:
    a) Dynasties
    b) Regions
    c) Kings
    d) Gods
    Answer: c) Kings

25. The Indo-Greeks introduced the practice of military governorship. The governors were called:
    a) Senapati
    b) Amatya
    c) Strategos
    d) Dandanayaka
    Answer: c) ‘Strategos’

THE SAKAS

26. Who followed the Greeks into India?
    a) Kushans
    b) Hunas
    c) Sakas
    d) Parthians
    Answer: c) Sakas

27. The Sakas are referred to as:
    a) Tocharians
    b) Yuch-Chis
    c) Scythians
    d) Hunas
    Answer: c) Scythians

28. Through which pass did the Sakas pour into India?
    a) Khyber Pass
    b) Bolan Pass
    c) Gomal Pass
    d) Shipki La
    Answer: b) Bolan Pass

29. The earliest Indian textual reference to the Sakas are found in the:
    a) Arthashastra
    b) Indica
    c) Mahabhasya
    d) Puranas
    Answer: c) Mahabhasya

30. The most famous of the Saka rulers was:
    a) Kanishka
    b) Maues
    c) Azes
    d) Rudradaman
    Answer: d) Rudradaman

31. Rudradaman's achievements are highlighted in which inscription?
    a) Nasik Inscription
    b) Aihole Inscription
    c) Junagarh Inscription
    d) Prayag-Prasasti
    Answer: c) Junagarh Inscription

32. Which was the first major inscription to be written in Sanskrit?
    a) Nasik Inscription
    b) Aihole Inscription
    c) Junagarh Inscription of Rudradaman
    d) Mehrauli iron pillar inscription
    Answer: c) Junagarh Inscription

33. The Junagarh Inscription records the repair of which lake undertook by Rudradaman's officers?
    a) Chilika lake
    b) Sudarshan lake
    c) Dal lake
    d) Wular lake
    Answer: b) Sudarshan lake

THE KUSHAN DYNASTY

34. The Kushans are referred to as:
    a) Scythians
    b) Yuch-Chis or Tocharians
    c) Hunas
    d) Sakas
    Answer: b) Yuch-Chis or Tocharians

35. The Kushans belonged to one of the five clans of which tribe?
    a) Saka
    b) Huna
    c) Yuch-Chis
    d) Mongol
    Answer: c) Yuch-Chis

36. From where did the Kushans come?
    a) North Central Asia near China
    b) Central Europe
    c) Middle East
    d) North Africa
    Answer: a) North Central Asia near China

37. Who started the Shaka era (78 A.D.)?
    a) Rudradaman
    b) Menander
    c) Kanishka
    d) Simuka
    Answer: c) Kanishka

38. Kanishka's two capitals were Purushpura and:
    a) Taxila
    b) Mathura
    c) Sialkot
    d) Ujjain
    Answer: b) Mathura

39. What was Purushpura also known as?
    a) Taxila
    b) Peshawar
    c) Mathura
    d) Gandhara
    Answer: b) Peshawar

40. Who was the court physician of Kanishka?
    a) Patanjali
    b) Ashvaghosha
    c) Charak
    d) Nagarjuna
    Answer: c) Charak

41. The Fourth Buddhist Council was held under Kanishka's patronage at:
    a) Rajgriha
    b) Vaishali
    c) Pataliputra
    d) Kundalvan
    Answer: d) Kundalvan

42. Kanishka controlled the famous which route in central Asia?
    a) Spice route
    b) Silk route
    c) Roman route
    d) Gold route
    Answer: b) Silk route

43. Which school of art received royal patronage from the Kushans?
    a) Mathura School of art
    b) Gandhara School of art
    c) Amravati School of art
    d) Sarnath School of art
    Answer: b) Gandhara School of art

44. Kanishka provided patronage to whom among the following?
    a) Patanjali and Charak
    b) Ashvaghosha and Nagarjuna
    c) Harisena and Vasubandhu
    d) Kalidasa and Amarsimha
    Answer: b) Ashvaghosha and Nagarjuna

THE GUPTAS (320-550 A.D.)

45. Who was the founder of Gupta rule?
    a) Chandragupta I
    b) Samudragupta
    c) Sri Gupta
    d) Ghatotkacha
    Answer: c) Sri Gupta

46. Who succeeded Sri Gupta?
    a) Chandragupta I
    b) Samudragupta
    c) Ghatotkacha
    d) Kumargupta I
    Answer: c) Ghatotkacha

47. Who is considered the real founder of the Gupta power?
    a) Sri Gupta
    b) Ghatotkacha
    c) Chandragupta I
    d) Samudragupta
    Answer: c) Chandragupta I

48. Chandragupta I was the first independent king with the title of:
    a) Maharajadhiraja
    b) Chakravartin
    c) Ekarat
    d) Vikramaditya
    Answer: a) Maharajadhiraja

49. When did Chandragupta I start the Gupta era?
    a) 78 A.D.
    b) 185 B.C.
    c) 319-320 A.D.
    d) 355 A.D.
    Answer: c) 319-320 A.D.

50. Chandragupta I married a princess from which clan?
    a) Naga
    b) Vakataka
    c) Lichchavi
    d) Gupta
    Answer: c) Lichchavi Princess

51. Samudragupta's achievements are mentioned in which inscription?
    a) Nasik Inscription
    b) Junagarh Inscription
    c) Prayag-Prasasti
    d) Aihole inscription
    Answer: c) Prayag-Prasasti

52. Who composed the Prayag-Prasasti?
    a) Kalidasa
    b) Vasubandhu
    c) Harisena
    d) Charak
    Answer: c) Harisena

53. Samudragupta defeated how many rulers of Dakshinapatha?
    a) 10
    b) 11
    c) 12
    d) 13
    Answer: c) 12

54. Which two scholars adorned Samudragupta's court?
    a) Kalidasa and Amarsimha
    b) Harisena and Vasubandhu
    c) Fa-hien and Dignaga
    d) Banabhatta and Hiuen Tsang
    Answer: b) Harisena and Vasubandhu

55. Samudragupta adopted the title of:
    a) Vikramaditya
    b) Kaviraja
    c) Ekarat
    d) Sarvakshatrantaka
    Answer: b) Kaviraja

56. On some coins, Samudragupta is shown playing which musical instrument?
    a) Sitar
    b) Flute
    c) Veena
    d) Drum
    Answer: c) Veena

57. Who calls Samudragupta the 'Napoleon of India'?
    a) Megasthenes
    b) Hiuen Tsang
    c) V.A. Smith
    d) James Princep
    Answer: c) V.A. Smith

58. Who was the commander of Samudragupta during his southern campaign?
    a) Harisena
    b) Vasubandhu
    c) Virasena
    d) Amarkhaddava
    Answer: c) Virasena

59. Samudragupta performed which Yajna and struck gold coins of Yupa type?
    a) Rajasuya Yajna
    b) Ashvamedha Yajna
    c) Vajapeya Yajna
    d) Agnishtoma Yajna
    Answer: b) Aswamedha Yajna

60. To which Buddhist king of Ceylon did Samudragupta grant permission to build a Monastery at Bodh Gaya?
    a) Meghavarman
    b) Mahendravarman I
    c) Narasimhavarman I
    d) Pulakesin II
    Answer: a) Meghavarman

61. Who succeeded Samudragupta, and was later killed by Chandragupta II?
    a) Kumargupta I
    b) Skandagupta
    c) Ramgupta
    d) Virasena
    Answer: c) Ramgupta

62. Chandragupta II married Ramgupta's queen, whose name was:
    a) Kubernaga
    b) Dhruvadevi
    c) Chellana
    d) Mahakosala
    Answer: b) Dhruvadevi

63. Chandragupta II entered into a matrimonial alliance with the Nagas by marrying princess:
    a) Dhruvadevi
    b) Kubernaga
    c) Khema
    d) Kundavai
    Answer: b) Kubernaga

64. Chandragupta II defeated which Saka king and annexed his kingdom?
    a) Rudradaman
    b) Maues
    c) Rudrasimha III
    d) Azes
    Answer: c) Rudrasimha III

65. Chandragupta II adopted the title of:
    a) Kaviraja
    b) Ekarat
    c) Vikramaditya
    d) Maharajadhiraja
    Answer: c) Vikramaditya

66. Which inscription mentions Chandragupta II's authority over North Western India and a portion of Bengal?
    a) Prayag-Prasasti
    b) Junagarh Inscription
    c) Mehrauli iron pillar inscription
    d) Udaigiri cave inscription
    Answer: c) Mehrauli iron pillar inscription

67. Virasena’s Udaigiri cave inscription refers to whose conquest of the whole world?
    a) Samudragupta
    b) Chandragupta I
    c) Chandragupta II
    d) Kumargupta I
    Answer: c) Chandragupta II

68. Chandragupta II made which city his second capital?
    a) Pataliputra
    b) Ayodhya
    c) Ujjain
    d) Mathura
    Answer: c) Ujjain

69. How many 'gems' (scholars) did Chandragupta II's court contain?
    a) Seven
    b) Eight
    c) Nine
    d) Ten
    Answer: c) 9 gems

70. Which of the following was NOT among the '9 gems' of Chandragupta II's court, as mentioned?
    a) Kalidasa
    b) Amarsimha
    c) Fa-hien
    d) Acharya Dignaga
    Answer: c) Fa-hien (Fa-hien was a traveler, not explicitly listed as one of the 9 gems here, though he was present during his reign.)

71. Who was the court poet and minister of Chandragupta II?
    a) Harisena
    b) Vasubandhu
    c) Veerasena Saba
    d) Amarkhaddava
    Answer: c) Veerasena Saba

72. Who was Chandragupta II's army general?
    a) Veerasena Saba
    b) Amarkhaddava
    c) Kalidasa
    d) Amarsimha
    Answer: b) Amarkhaddava

73. Which Chinese traveller came during the time of Chandragupta II?
    a) Hiuen Tsang
    b) I-tsing
    c) Fa-hien
    d) Xuanzang
    Answer: c) Fa-hien

74. Chandragupta II issued coins of which metals?
    a) Gold only
    b) Gold and silver only
    c) Silver and copper
    d) Gold, silver and copper
    Answer: c) Silver and copper

Taxes Levied during the Gupta Dynasty

75. What was 'Bhag' during the Gupta Dynasty?
    a) Irrigation tax
    b) Police tax
    c) Kings share of produce
    d) Security tax
    Answer: c) Kings share of produce

76. 'Hiranya' was a tax on special produce taken in:
    a) Kind
    b) Cash
    c) Services
    d) Labor
    Answer: b) Cash

77. What was 'Bedakbhog' during the Gupta Dynasty?
    a) Grazing tax
    b) Irrigation tax
    c) Security tax
    d) General tribute
    Answer: b) Irrigation tax

78. 'Bhatta' was a:
    a) Grazing tax
    b) Irrigation tax
    c) Police tax
    d) Security tax
    Answer: c) Police tax

79. 'Chat' was a:
    a) Grazing tax
    b) Security tax
    c) Irrigation tax
    d) Police tax
    Answer: b) Security tax

Gupta Temples : Places

80. The Vishnu temple at Tigawa is located in:
    a) Deogarh (Jhansi)
    b) Bhitargaon
    c) Jabalpur
    d) Kanchi
    Answer: c) Jabalpur

81. The Dasavtar temple is located at:
    a) Tigawa (Jabalpur)
    b) Deogarh (Jhansi)
    c) Bhitargaon
    d) Mamallapuram
    Answer: b) Deogarh (Jhansi)

82. The Bhitargaon temple is located at:
    a) Jabalpur
    b) Jhansi
    c) Bhitargaon
    d) Kanchi
    Answer: c) Bhitargaon

83. The gold coins issued by the Guptas were called:
    a) Pana
    b) Karshapana
    c) Dinara
    d) Dramma
    Answer: c) ‘Dinara’

84. Who succeeded Chandragupta II?
    a) Samudragupta
    b) Kumargupta I
    c) Skandagupta
    d) Ramgupta
    Answer: b) Kumargupta I

85. Which of the following is NOT an inscription of Kumargupta I?
    a) Bilsad inscription
    b) Karandanda inscription
    c) Mandsor inscription
    d) Mehrauli iron pillar inscription
    Answer: d) Mehrauli iron pillar inscription

86. Who founded the Nalanda University?
    a) Chandragupta I
    b) Samudragupta
    c) Kumargupta I
    d) Skandagupta
    Answer: c) Kumargupta I

87. Towards the last year of whose reign did the Gupta empire face foreign invasions?
    a) Chandragupta I
    b) Samudragupta
    c) Kumargupta I
    d) Skandagupta
    Answer: c) Kumargupta I

88. Who had to fight the Pushyamitras and the Hunas?
    a) Kumargupta I
    b) Skandagupta
    c) Chandragupta II
    d) Samudragupta
    Answer: b) Skandagupta

89. The Junagarh inscription of Skandagupta's reign tells that whose governor repaired the Sudarshan lake?
    a) Parnadatta
    b) Virasena
    c) Amarkhaddava
    d) Harisena
    Answer: a) Parnadatta

90. Skandagupta adopted the title of:
    a) Kaviraja
    b) Ekarat
    c) Vikramaditya
    d) Maharajadhiraja
    Answer: c) Vikramaditya

HARSHVARDHAN (606-647 A.D.)

91. When did Harshavardhan ascend the Pushyabhuti throne?
    a) 550 A.D.
    b) 606 A.D.
    c) 647 A.D.
    d) 750 A.D.
    Answer: b) 606 A.D.

92. Harshavardhan was also known as:
    a) Kaviraja
    b) Vikramaditya
    c) Siladitya
    d) Chakravartin
    Answer: c) Siladitya

93. Which two individuals have provided important information about Harsha’s reign?
    a) Kalidasa and Fa-hien
    b) Banabhatta and Hiuen Tsang
    c) Amarsimha and Charak
    d) Harisena and Vasubandhu
    Answer: b) Banabhatta and Hiuen Tsang

94. Who wrote 'Harshacharita'?
    a) Hiuen Tsang
    b) Harsha
    c) Banabhatta
    d) Kalidasa
    Answer: c) Banabhatta

95. In his second expedition, Harsha conquered Magadha and whose empire?
    a) Pulakesin II's
    b) Sasanka’s
    c) Dhruva's
    d) Nagabhatta II's
    Answer: b) Sasanka’s

96. The Aihole inscription mentions that Harsha met defeat at the hands of:
    a) Pulakesin II
    b) Narasimhavarman I
    c) Rajaraja
    d) Rajendra I
    Answer: a) Pulakesin II

97. Harsha’s empire extended from the Punjab to northern Orissa and from Himalayas to the banks of which river?
    a) Ganga
    b) Yamuna
    c) Godavari
    d) Narmada
    Answer: d) Narmada

98. Harsha wrote three Sanskrit plays. Which of the following is NOT one of them?
    a) Nagananda
    b) Ratnavali
    c) Priyadarshika
    d) Kadambri
    Answer: d) Kadambri

99. Who wrote Harshacharita and Kadambri?
    a) Harsha
    b) Hiuen Tsang
    c) Banabhatta
    d) Kalidasa
    Answer: c) Banabhatta

100. The Kannauj assembly was held in the honour of:
    a) Banabhatta
    b) Hiuen-Tsang
    c) Harsha
    d) Pulakesin II
    Answer: b) Hiuen-Tsang

101. Harsha used to celebrate religious festivals at the end of every five years at:
    a) Kannauj
    b) Ujjain
    c) Prayag
    d) Pataliputra
    Answer: c) Prayag

102. When did Harsha send an embassy to the Chinese emperor?
    a) 606 A.D.
    b) 641 A.D.
    c) 647 A.D.
    d) 750 A.D.
    Answer: b) 641 A.D.

SOUTH INDIAN DYNASTIES

THE CHALUKYAS OF BADAMI (543-757 A.D.)

103. The Vakatakas in the Deccan were followed by:
    a) Pallavas
    b) Chalukyas of Badami
    c) Cholas
    d) Rashtrakutas
    Answer: b) Chalukyas of Badami

104. The Chalukyas established their base at Badami, also known as:
    a) Vengi
    b) Kanchi
    c) Vatapi
    d) Mamallapuram
    Answer: c) Vatapi

105. Who was the founder of the Chalukya dynasty, establishing a small kingdom with Badami/Vatapi as its capital?
    a) Pulakesin II
    b) Vishnuvardhan
    c) Pulakesin-I
    d) Narsimhavarman I
    Answer: c) Pulakesin-I

106. Which Chalukya ruler was able to check Harsha’s design to conquer Deccan?
    a) Pulakesin-I
    b) Vishnuvardhan
    c) Pulakesin II
    d) Tailap
    Answer: c) Pulakesin II

107. The Aihole inscription is a eulogy written by Pulakesin II's court poet:
    a) Banabhatta
    b) Harisena
    c) Ravikirti
    d) Kalidasa
    Answer: c) Ravikirti

108. Which Chinese pilgrim visited Pulakesin II's kingdom?
    a) Fa-hien
    b) I-tsing
    c) Hiuen Tsang
    d) Xuanzang
    Answer: c) Hiuen Tsang

109. Which Pallava ruler invaded the Chalukya kingdom, killed Pulakesin II, and captured Badami?
    a) Mahendravarman-I
    b) Narasimhavarman I
    c) Narasimhavarman II
    d) Nandivarman-II
    Answer: b) Narsimhavarman I

110. Who founded the eastern branch of the Chalukyas with its capital at Vengi?
    a) Pulakesin II
    b) Vishnuvardhan, son of Pulakesin II
    c) Pulakesin-I
    d) Narsimhavarman I
    Answer: b) Vishnuvardhan, son of Pulakesin II

THE PALLAVAS (560-803)

111. Who founded the Pallava dynasty?
    a) Mahendravarman-I
    b) Narasimhavarman I
    c) Simhavisnu
    d) Aparajita Pallava
    Answer: c) Simhavisnu

112. Which Pallava ruler was defeated by Pulakesin-II?
    a) Simhavisnu
    b) Mahendravarman-I
    c) Narasimhavarman I
    d) Nandivarman-II
    Answer: b) Mahendravarman-I

113. Who was the greatest Pallava king, who defeated Pulakesin II and took the title of Vatapikonda?
    a) Mahendravarman-I
    b) Narasimhavarman
    c) Narasimhavarman II
    d) Nandivarman-II
    Answer: b) Narasimhavarman

114. During whose reign did Hieun Tsang visit Kanchi?
    a) Mahendravarman-I
    b) Narasimhavarman
    c) Narasimhavarman II
    d) Nandivarman-II
    Answer: b) Narsimhavarman

115. Who constructed the shore temple of Mamallapuram and the Kailasnath temple of Kanchi?
    a) Narasimhavarman I
    b) Narasimhavarman II
    c) Mahendravarman-I
    d) Nandivarman-II
    Answer: b) Narsimhavarman II

116. Who built the Vaikuntaperumal temple at Kanchi?
    a) Simhavisnu
    b) Mahendravarman-I
    c) Narasimhavarman I
    d) Nandivarman-II
    Answer: d) Nandivarman-II

117. Who was the last Pallava ruler, defeated by Aditya Chola?
    a) Nandivarman-II
    b) Simhavisnu
    c) Aparajita Pallava
    d) Mahendravarman-I
    Answer: c) Aparajita Pallava

118. Kanchi, the Pallavas' capital, was a great centre of:
    a) Pali learning
    b) Prakrit learning
    c) Sanskrit learning
    d) Tamil learning
    Answer: c) Sanskrit learning

119. Bharavi, the author of 'Kirtarjuniyam', lived in whose court?
    a) Chalukya
    b) Chola
    c) Pallava
    d) Ganga
    Answer: c) Pallava court

120. Dandin, the author of 'Dashkumarcharitam', lived in whose court?
    a) Chalukya
    b) Chola
    c) Pallava
    d) Ganga
    Answer: c) Pallava court

121. Who wrote the famous burlesque 'Mattavilasa Prahasana'?
    a) Bharavi
    b) Dandin
    c) Mahendravarman-I
    d) Narasimhavarman I
    Answer: c) Mahendravarman-I

THE GANGAS

122. The Gangas ruled over a large part of modern:
    a) Tamil Nadu
    b) Kerala
    c) Mysore (Karnataka)
    d) Andhra Pradesh
    Answer: c) Mysore

123. The region ruled by the Gangas was called:
    a) Chalukya-vadi
    b) Pallava-nadu
    c) Gangavadi
    d) Chola-mandalam
    Answer: c) Gangavadi

124. Who was the founder of the Ganga dynasty?
    a) Pulakesin-I
    b) Simhavisnu
    c) Konkanivarman Dharmamahadhiraja
    d) Mayur Sarman
    Answer: c) Konkanivarman Dharmamahadhiraja

THE KADAMBAS

125. The Kadamba dynasty was founded by:
    a) Konkanivarman Dharmamahadhiraja
    b) Kakusthavarman
    c) Mayur Sarman
    d) Simhavisnu
    Answer: c) Mayur Sarman

126. From which city did the Kadambas rule?
    a) Badami
    b) Kanchi
    c) Banvasi
    d) Tanjore
    Answer: c) Banvasi

127. Who was the most powerful ruler of the Kadamba dynasty?
    a) Mayur Sarman
    b) Konkanivarman Dharmamahadhiraja
    c) Kakusthavarman
    d) Pulakesin II
    Answer: c) Kakusthavarman

128. Kakusthavarman established matrimonial relations with the Gangas and the:
    a) Pallavas
    b) Chalukyas
    c) Guptas
    d) Rashtrakutas
    Answer: c) Guptas

THE CHOLAS

129. The Cholas were originally feudatories of the:
    a) Chalukyas
    b) Pallavas
    c) Rashtrakutas
    d) Pandyas
    Answer: b) Pallavas

130. Who was the founder of the Chola dynasty?
    a) Rajaraja
    b) Rajendra I
    c) Vijayalaya
    d) Kulotunga-I
    Answer: c) Vijayalaya

131. When did Vijayalaya capture Tanjore?
    a) 560 A.D.
    b) 850 A.D.
    c) 985 A.D.
    d) 1014 A.D.
    Answer: b) 850 A.D.

132. The two greatest Chola rulers were Rajaraja and his son:
    a) Vijayalaya
    b) Rajendra I
    c) Kulotunga-I
    d) Adhirajendra
    Answer: b) Rajendra I

133. Rajaraja built a Saiva temple of Rajarajeshwara at:
    a) Gangaikondacholapuram
    b) Kanchi
    c) Tanjore
    d) Mamallapuram
    Answer: c) Tanjore

134. Rajendra I assumed the title of:
    a) Vatapikonda
    b) Vikramaditya
    c) Gangaikondachola
    d) Kaviraja
    Answer: c) Gangaikondachola

135. Rajendra I built a city called:
    a) Tanjore
    b) Gangaikondacholapuram
    c) Kanchi
    d) Mamallapuram
    Answer: b) Gangaikondacholapuram

136. The Chola empire was divided into Mandalams or:
    a) Districts
    b) Villages
    c) Provinces
    d) Divisions
    Answer: c) Provinces

137. During the reign of Rajendra I, a naval expedition was sent against which empire?
    a) Chinese empire
    b) Sri Vijaya empire
    c) Roman empire
    d) Arabian empire
    Answer: b) Sri Vijaya empire

138. Which body of water was converted into a 'Chola lake' during the Chola period?
    a) Arabian Sea
    b) Bay of Bengal
    c) Indian Ocean
    d) Laccadive Sea
    Answer: b) Bay of Bengal

139. Rajaraja gave his daughter Kundavai to Vimaladitya, the Eastern Chalukya of:
    a) Badami
    b) Kalyani
    c) Vengi
    d) Pattadakal
    Answer: c) Vengi

140. Rajaraja conferred the title of 'Mahadandanayaka' upon Rajendra-I for his victory over which two kingdoms?
    a) Chalukyas & Pallavas
    b) Pandyas & Keralas
    c) Gangas & Kadambas
    d) Cholas & Cheras
    Answer: b) Pandyas & Keralas

141. Who started the system of land survey called 'kadamb'?
    a) Vijayalaya
    b) Rajaraja-I
    c) Rajendra I
    d) Kulotunga-I
    Answer: b) Rajaraja-I

142. Who was killed in a public revolt during the Chola period?
    a) Rajaraja
    b) Rajendra I
    c) Adhirajendra
    d) Kulotunga-I
    Answer: c) Adhirajendra

143. Who united the kingdom of Eastern Chalukyas of Vengi and the Chola kingdom?
    a) Rajaraja
    b) Rajendra I
    c) Kulotunga-I of Vengi
    d) Rajendra II
    Answer: c) Kulotunga-I of Vengi

144. Kulotunga-I was given the title of:
    a) Prakesari
    b) Sangam Tavirtta (he who abolished tolls)
    c) Gangaikondachola
    d) Mahadandanayaka
    Answer: b) Sangam Tavirtta (he who abolished tolls)

145. Rajendra II was crowned in the battlefield of:
    a) Tanjore
    b) Gangaikondacholapuram
    c) Koppam
    d) Vengi
    Answer: c) Koppam

146. Which architectural style was developed under the Cholas?
    a) Nagara style
    b) Vesara style
    c) Gopuram style
    d) Dravida style (though Gopuram specifically developed during their rule, Dravida is the broader style)
    Answer: c) Gopuram style

147. The system of canals in the south is a contribution of the:
    a) Pallavas
    b) Chalukyas
    c) Cholas
    d) Pandyas
    Answer: c) Cholas

148. The Cholas were famous for the bronze statue of:
    a) Shiva
    b) Vishnu
    c) Nataraja
    d) Parvati
    Answer: c) Nataraja

149. Who wrote Ramayana during the Chola period?
    a) Pampa
    b) Ranna
    c) Kamban
    d) Ponna
    Answer: c) Kamban

150. The style of architecture which came into vogue during the Chola period is called:
    a) Nagara
    b) Vesara
    c) Dravida
    d) Indo-Islamic
    Answer: c) Dravida

151. In Chola temple architecture, 'Mandap' was the:
    a) Main shrine
    b) Pillared hall
    c) Entrance tower
    d) Circumambulatory path
    Answer: b) Pillared hall

152. In Chola temple architecture, 'Garbhgriha' was the:
    a) Pillared hall
    b) Kitchen
    c) Room of the chief-deity
    d) Treasury
    Answer: c) Room of the chief-deity

Administrative Units of Chola Dynasty

153. What was a 'Mandalam' in the Chola administrative units?
    a) District
    b) Village
    c) Province
    d) Group of villages
    Answer: c) Province

154. What was a 'Nadu' in the Chola administrative units?
    a) Province
    b) Division
    c) District
    d) Group of villages
    Answer: c) District

155. What was a 'Kurtam' in the Chola administrative units?
    a) Province
    b) Division
    c) District
    d) Group of villages
    Answer: d) Group of villages

Tripartite Struggle (Palas, Pratiharas and Rastrakutas)

156. The Gurjara Pratiharas, the Palas, and the Rashtrakutas fought for supremacy over:
    a) Delhi
    b) Pataliputra
    c) Kannauj
    d) Ujjain
    Answer: c) Kannauj

THE PRATIHARAS

157. The Pratiharas emerged from Gurjaratra or:
    a) North East India
    b) South West Rajasthan
    c) Deccan
    d) Bengal
    Answer: b) South West Rajasthan

158. Who founded the Pratihara dynasty?
    a) Bhoja
    b) Nagabhatta-I
    c) Nagabhatta-II
    d) Devapala
    Answer: b) Nagabhatta-I

159. Pratihara power revived under:
    a) Nagabhatta-I
    b) Nagabhatta-II
    c) Bhoja
    d) Dharmapala
    Answer: b) Nagabhatta-II

160. Nagabhatta-II defeated Dharmapala near:
    a) Kannauj
    b) Monghyr (Mudgir)
    c) Manyakhet
    d) Ellora
    Answer: b) Monghyr (Mudgir)

161. The capital of the Pratiharas was at Mahodaya, which is:
    a) Delhi
    b) Ujjain
    c) Kannauj
    d) Varanasi
    Answer: c) Kannauj

162. Who was the real founder and the greatest king of the Pratihara dynasty?
    a) Nagabhatta-I
    b) Nagabhatta-II
    c) Bhoja
    d) Devapala
    Answer: c) Bhoja

163. Bhoja is also called:
    a) Dharmapala
    b) Mihir Bhoja
    c) Dantidurga
    d) Amoghavarsha
    Answer: b) Mihir Bhoja

164. Bhoja was a devotee of which deity and adopted the title of Adivaraha?
    a) Shiva
    b) Brahma
    c) Vishnu
    d) Devi
    Answer: c) Vishnu

THE PALAS

165. The Pala empire was founded by:
    a) Dharmapala
    b) Devapala
    c) Gopala
    d) Indra
    Answer: c) Gopala

166. When was the Pala empire founded?
    a) 606 A.D.
    b) 750 A.D.
    c) 770 A.D.
    d) 810 A.D.
    Answer: b) 750 A.D.

167. Who succeeded Gopala?
    a) Devapala
    b) Dharmapala
    c) Dhruva
    d) Nagabhatt II
    Answer: b) Dharmapala

168. Palas established their rule in:
    a) Rajasthan and Gujarat
    b) Deccan
    c) Bengal and Bihar
    d) Uttar Pradesh and Madhya Pradesh
    Answer: c) Bengal and Bihar

169. Dharmapala was defeated by Dhruva (Rashtrakuta) and:
    a) Nagabhatt II (Pratihara)
    b) Indra III (Rashtrakuta)
    c) Bhoja (Pratihara)
    d) Tailap (Chalukya)
    Answer: a) Nagabhatt II (Pratihara)

170. During Dharmapala's reign, two brothers clashed for Kannauj. Their names were:
    a) Nagabhatta and Bhoja
    b) Indra and Chakra
    c) Dhruva and Amoghavarsha
    d) Gopala and Dharmapala
    Answer: b) Indra & Chakra

171. Dharmapala revived which university?
    a) Vikramshila University
    b) Valabhi University
    c) Odantpuri University
    d) Nalanda University
    Answer: d) Nalanda University

172. Dharmapala also founded which university?
    a) Nalanda University
    b) Vikramshila University
    c) Valabhi University
    d) Jagadal University
    Answer: b) Vikramshila University

THE RASHTRAKUTAS

173. The Rashtrakuta dynasty was founded by:
    a) Krishna-I
    b) Amoghavarsha
    c) Dantidurga
    d) Tailap
    Answer: c) Dantidurga

174. The capital of the Rashtrakutas was at Manyakhet or Malkhed, near modern Sholapur (earlier at):
    a) Badami
    b) Kanchi
    c) Ellora
    d) Vengi
    Answer: c) Ellora

175. Which Rashtrakuta ruler ruled for 68 years and wrote 'Kavirajamarga'?
    a) Dantidurga
    b) Krishna-I
    c) Amoghavarsha
    d) Indra III
    Answer: c) Amoghavarsha

176. 'Kavirajamarga' is a book on:
    a) History
    b) Philosophy
    c) Poetics (Kannada)
    d) Administration
    Answer: c) Poetics Kannada

177. Amoghavarsha first transferred the capital from Ellora to:
    a) Kannauj
    b) Manyakhet or Malkhed
    c) Sholapur
    d) Badami
    Answer: b) Manyakhet or Malkhed

178. Which Rashtrakuta ruler defeated Mahipala (Pratihara) and sacked Kannauj in 915 A.D.?
    a) Dantidurga
    b) Amoghavarsha
    c) Indra III
    d) Krishna-I
    Answer: c) Indira III

179. From where did the Rashtrakutas import horses?
    a) China and Japan
    b) Southeast Asia
    c) Arabia, West-Asia and central Asia
    d) Europe and Africa
    Answer: c) Arabia, West-Asia and central Asia

180. In the Rashtrakuta kingdom, the directly administered areas were divided into Rashtras (provinces), Visaya, and:
    a) Nadus
    b) Bhukti
    c) Kottam
    d) Kurtam
    Answer: b) Bhukti

181. Who was the last Rashtrakuta ruler, overthrown by Tailap?
    a) Dantidurga
    b) Amoghavarsha
    c) Krishna-I
    d) Karka
    Answer: d) Karka

182. Who was the founder of the Chalukyas of Kalyani?
    a) Dantidurga
    b) Karka
    c) Tailap
    d) Krishna-I
    Answer: c) Tailap

183. Who built the Dashavatara temple at Ellora?
    a) Krishna-I
    b) Amoghavarsha
    c) Dantidurga
    d) Indra III
    Answer: c) Dantidurga

184. Who built the Kailasa or Shiva temple at Ellora in the 9th century?
    a) Dantidurga
    b) Krishna-I
    c) Amoghavarsha
    d) Indra III
    Answer: b) Krishna-I

185. The great Apabhramsha poet Svayambhu and his son lived at the court of which dynasty?
    a) Pratihara
    b) Pala
    c) Rashtrakuta
    d) Chola
    Answer: c) Rashtrakuta

REGIONAL DYNASTIES

THE PARMARAS

1.  The Parmaras initially had their capital at:
    a) Dhara
    b) Bhojapur
    c) Ujjain
    d) Kannauj
    Answer: c) Ujjain

2.  The Parmaras later transferred their capital to:
    a) Ujjain
    b) Dhara
    c) Bhojapur
    d) Kannauj
    Answer: b) Dhara

3.  Who was the founder of the Parmara dynasty?
    a) Bhoja
    b) Padmagupta
    c) Vakpati Munj
    d) Dhananjya
    Answer: c) Vakpati Munj

4.  Which two scholars graced the court of Vakpati Munj?
    a) Kalidasa and Amarsimha
    b) Padmagupta and Dhananjya
    c) Harisena and Vasubandhu
    d) Banabhatta and Hiuen Tsang
    Answer: b) Padmagupta, Dhananjya

5.  Who was the greatest ruler of the Parmara dynasty?
    a) Vakpati Munj
    b) Padmagupta
    c) Bhoja
    d) Dhananjya
    Answer: c) Bhoja

6.  Who built the city of Bhojapur?
    a) Vakpati Munj
    b) Bhoja
    c) Chandradeva
    d) Prithviraja Chauhan
    Answer: b) Bhoja

7.  Bhoja founded a college, Bhojshala, near which city?
    a) Ujjain
    b) Bhojapur
    c) Dhara
    d) Kannauj
    Answer: c) Dhara

THE GAHADAVALS

8.  The Gahadaval dynasty was founded by:
    a) Jaichandra
    b) Muhammad Ghori
    c) Chandradeva
    d) Harischandra
    Answer: c) Chandradeva

9.  The capital of the Gahadaval dynasty was:
    a) Delhi
    b) Ajmer
    c) Kannauj
    d) Madurai
    Answer: c) Kannauj

10. In the Battle of Chandawar (1194 A.D.), Muhammad Ghori defeated whom?
    a) Prithviraja Chauhan
    b) Jaichandra
    c) Harischandra
    d) Parmardideva
    Answer: b) Jaichandra

11. Who was the last ruler of the Gahadaval dynasty?
    a) Jaichandra
    b) Chandradeva
    c) Harischandra
    d) Muhammad Ghori
    Answer: c) Harischandra

THE CHAUHANS

12. The Chahamana (Chauhan) dynasty emerged near Sakambhari, which is now:
    a) Delhi
    b) Ajmer
    c) Kannauj
    d) Mathura
    Answer: b) Ajmer

13. The Chauhans were the feudatories of which dynasty?
    a) Palas
    b) Rashtrakutas
    c) Pratihara
    d) Senas
    Answer: c) Pratihara

14. Who declared independence for the Chauhan dynasty in the middle of the 10th century?
    a) Ajayraja
    b) Prithviraja Chauhan
    c) Simharaja
    d) Muhammad Ghori
    Answer: c) Simharaja

15. Who founded the city of Ajaymeru (Ajmer)?
    a) Simharaja
    b) Prithviraja Chauhan
    c) Ajayraja
    d) Muhammad Ghori
    Answer: c) Ajayraja

16. When did Prithviraja Chauhan ascend to the throne?
    a) 1191 A.D.
    b) 1192 A.D.
    c) 1177 A.D.
    d) 1194 A.D.
    Answer: c) 1177 A.D.

17. Prithviraja Chauhan defeated Muhammad Ghori in which battle?
    a) Battle of Chandawar
    b) First battle of Tarain (1191 A.D.)
    c) Second battle of Tarain (1192 A.D.)
    d) Battle of Panipat
    Answer: b) First battle of Tarain (1191 A.D.)

18. In the second battle of Tarain (1192 A.D.), who defeated Prithviraja Chauhan?
    a) Jaichandra
    b) Muhammad Ghori
    c) Qutub-ud-din Aibak
    d) Alauddin Khalji
    Answer: b) Muhammad Ghori

KASHMIR

19. Which three dynasties ruled Kashmir during 800 A.D. to 1200 A.D.?
    a) Ganga, Kadamba, Chola
    b) Parmara, Gahadaval, Chauhan
    c) Karkota, Utpala, Lohar
    d) Sena, Chandella, Satvahana
    Answer: c) Karkota, Utpala and Lohar

20. The Karkota dynasty was founded by:
    a) Lalitaditya Muktapida
    b) Durlabh Vardhana
    c) Avantivarman
    d) Sangramraja
    Answer: b) Durlabh Vardhana

21. Lalitaditya Muktapida defeated whom from Kannauj?
    a) Harischandra
    b) Jaichandra
    c) Yasovarman
    d) Prithviraja Chauhan
    Answer: c) Yasovarman

22. Lalitaditya built the famous Sun temple at:
    a) Konark
    b) Modhera
    c) Martand
    d) Puri
    Answer: c) Martand

23. The Utpala dynasty was founded by:
    a) Durlabh Vardhana
    b) Lalitaditya Muktapida
    c) Avantivarman
    d) Sangramraja
    Answer: c) Avantivarman

24. Queen Didda ruled Kashmir during which period?
    a) 800 A.D. to 850 A.D.
    b) 900 A.D. to 950 A.D.
    c) 980 A.D. to 1003 A.D.
    d) 1000 A.D. to 1050 A.D.
    Answer: c) 980 A.D. to 1003 A.D.

25. Who founded the Lohara dynasty?
    a) King Harsha
    b) Queen Didda
    c) Sangramraja
    d) Kalhana
    Answer: c) Sangramraja

26. King Harsha belonged to which dynasty?
    a) Karkota
    b) Utpala
    c) Lohara
    d) Sena
    Answer: c) Lohara dynasty

27. Who wrote 'Rajatarangini'?
    a) Banabhatta
    b) Hiuen Tsang
    c) Kalhana
    d) Jayadeva
    Answer: c) Kalhana

28. In which century was 'Rajatarangini' written?
    a) 10th century
    b) 11th century
    c) 12th century
    d) 13th century
    Answer: c) 12th century

THE CHANDELLAS

29. The Chandelas ruled in:
    a) Malwa
    b) Bundelkhand
    c) Bengal
    d) Kashmir
    Answer: b) Bundelkhand

30. Who founded the Chandella dynasty?
    a) Dhanga
    b) Ganda
    c) Nanuka
    d) Vidyadhara
    Answer: c) Nanuka

31. What was the capital of the Chandella dynasty established by Nanuka?
    a) Kalinjar
    b) Khajuraho
    c) Dhara
    d) Ujjain
    Answer: b) Khajuraho

32. Who was the first independent king of the Chandella dynasty and assumed the title Maharajadhiraja?
    a) Nanuka
    b) Ganda
    c) Dhanga
    d) Vidyadhara
    Answer: c) Dhanga

33. During whose reign did Mahmud of Ghazni invade Kalinjar in 1019 and again in 1022?
    a) Dhanga
    b) Ganda
    c) Vidyadhara
    d) Parmardideva
    Answer: c) Vidyadhara

34. In 1165, Parmardideva was defeated by:
    a) Muhammad Ghori
    b) Qutub-ud-din Aibak
    c) Prithviraja Chauhan
    d) Alauddin Khalji
    Answer: c) Prithviraja Chauhan

35. Who defeated Parmadideva and occupied a greater part of Bundelkhand?
    a) Muhammad Ghori
    b) Qutub-ud-din Aibak
    c) Alauddin Khalji
    d) Prithviraja Chauhan
    Answer: b) Qutub-ud-din Aibak

36. Who captured the whole of the Chandella kingdom?
    a) Qutub-ud-din Aibak
    b) Muhammad Ghori
    c) Alauddin Khalji
    d) Iltutmish
    Answer: c) Alauddin Khalji

37. The famous temples of Khajuraho were built by which dynasty?
    a) Parmaras
    b) Gahadavals
    c) Chauhans
    d) Chandellas
    Answer: d) Chandellas

38. Which of the following is NOT mentioned as a prominent temple among the Khajuraho temples?
    a) Kandariya Mahadeva
    b) Devi Jagadamba
    c) Parsvanatha
    d) Brihadishvara Temple
    Answer: d) Brihadishvara Temple

THE SENAS

39. The Senas supplanted which dynasty in Bengal?
    a) Cholas
    b) Pallavas
    c) Palas
    d) Rashtrakutas
    Answer: c) Palas

40. The Senas called themselves:
    a) Kshatriya
    b) Shudra
    c) Brahma Kshatriya
    d) Vaishya
    Answer: c) ‘Brahma Kshatriya’

41. Which two cities were the capitals of the Senas?
    a) Lakhnauti and Gaur
    b) Vijayapuri and Vikrampuri
    c) Tanjore and Madurai
    d) Kannauj and Dhara
    Answer: b) Vijayapuri and Vikrampuri

42. Who succeeded Vijayasena?
    a) Ballalsena
    b) Lakshmansena
    c) Jayadeva
    d) Halayudha
    Answer: a) Ballalsena

43. Who established the tradition of ‘Kulinism’?
    a) Vijayasena
    b) Ballalsena
    c) Lakshmansena
    d) Jayadeva
    Answer: b) Ballalsena

44. Ballalsena wrote which two books?
    a) Harshacharita and Kadambri
    b) Adbhutsagar and Dansagar
    c) Gita Govind and Rajatarangini
    d) Mrichhkatikam and Mudra Rakshasha
    Answer: b) ‘Adbhutsagar’ and ‘Dansagar’

45. Who invaded Bengal during the reign of Lakshmansena?
    a) Muhammad Ghori
    b) Qutub-ud-din Aibak
    c) Bakhtiyar Khalji
    d) Alauddin Khalji
    Answer: c) Bakhtiyar Khalji

46. Which three scholars adorned Lakshmansena’s court?
    a) Kalidasa, Amarsimha, Fa-hien
    b) Bharavi, Dandin, Varahamihira
    c) Jayadeva, Dhoyi, Halayudha
    d) Padmagupta, Dhananjya, Patanjali
    Answer: c) Jayadeva, Dhoyi, Halayudha

THE SANGAM AGE

47. The word Sangam is associated with the assembly of Tamil Scholars and Poets flourished under the royal patronage of which kings?
    a) Chola Kings
    b) Chera Kings
    c) Pandyan Kings
    d) Pallava Kings
    Answer: c) Pandyan Kings

48. Where was the first Sangam held?
    a) Kapatpuram
    b) Madurai
    c) Uraiyaur
    d) Venji
    Answer: b) Madurai

49. Who was the Chairman of the First Sangam?
    a) Tolkapiyar
    b) Nakkirar
    c) Agastya
    d) Tiruvalluvar
    Answer: c) Agastya

50. Where was the second Sangam held?
    a) Madurai
    b) Kapatpuram
    c) Uraiyaur
    d) Puhar
    Answer: b) Kapatpuram

51. Who was the Chairman of the Second Sangam?
    a) Agastya
    b) Tolkapiyar
    c) Nakkirar
    d) Ilango Adigal
    Answer: b) Tolkapiyar

52. Where was the third Sangam held?
    a) Kapatpuram
    b) Madurai
    c) Uraiyaur
    d) Venji
    Answer: b) Madurai

53. Who was the chairman of the Third Sangam?
    a) Agastya
    b) Tolkapiyar
    c) Nakkirar
    d) Sattalai Sattanar
    Answer: c) Nakkirar

54. The text 'Tolkapiyam' belongs to which Sangam?
    a) First Sangam
    b) Second Sangam
    c) Third Sangam
    d) None of the above
    Answer: b) Second Sangam

55. 'Tolkapiyam' is a work on:
    a) Poetry
    b) History
    c) Grammar
    d) Philosophy
    Answer: c) grammar

56. When was the Sangam literature compiled?
    a) Circa A.D. 100-300
    b) Circa A.D. 300-600
    c) Circa A.D. 600-900
    d) Circa A.D. 900-1200
    Answer: b) circa A.D. 300-600

57. 'Thirukural' or 'Kural' by Tiruvalluvar is sometimes called:
    a) The 'Mahabharata of the Tamil land'
    b) The 'fifth Veda' or 'Bible of the Tamil land'
    c) The 'Odysseus of Tamil poetry'
    d) The 'Iliad of the Tamil land'
    Answer: b) the ‘fifth Veda’ or ‘Bible of the Tamil land’

58. 'Silpaddikaram' literally means:
    a) The Golden Flower
    b) The Jeweled Anklet
    c) The Lost Gem
    d) The Brave Warrior
    Answer: b) ‘The jeweled Anklet’

59. Who wrote 'Silpaddikaram'?
    a) Tiruvalluvar
    b) Sattalai Sattanar
    c) Ilango Adigal
    d) Tiruttakadevar
    Answer: c) Ilango Adigal

60. 'Silpaddikaram' deals with the love stories of:
    a) Rama and Sita
    b) Krishna and Radha
    c) Kovalan and Madhavi
    d) Shiva and Parvati
    Answer: c) Kovalan and Madhavi

61. 'Manimekhalai' is written by:
    a) Ilango Adigal
    b) Sattalai Sattanar
    c) Tiruttakadevar
    d) Nakkirar
    Answer: b) Sattalai Sattanar

62. 'Manimekhalai' is considered the:
    a) 'Fifth Veda'
    b) 'Bible of the Tamil land'
    c) 'Odysseus of Tamil poetry'
    d) 'Jeweled Anklet'
    Answer: c) ‘Odysseus of Tamil poetry’

63. 'Jivaga Chintamani', a third epic, was written by the Jaina:
    a) Ilango Adigal
    b) Sattalai Sattanar
    c) Tiruttakadevar
    d) Nakkirar
    Answer: c) Tiruttakadevar

64. Which Roman king built a temple of Augustus at Muziris?
    a) Julius Caesar
    b) Augustus
    c) Nero
    d) Trajan
    Answer: b) Augustus

65. Who was the God par excellence of the Tamils, also known as Subramaniyam?
    a) Shiva
    b) Vishnu
    c) Murugan
    d) Brahma
    Answer: c) Murugan

Sangam Literature

66. According to Tamil sources, who is the father of Tamil literature?
    a) Tolakappiyan
    b) Nakkirar
    c) Agastya
    d) Tiruvalluvar
    Answer: c) ‘Agastya.’

SANGAM AGE (AD First-Third Century)

Kingdoms

67. The Chola kingdom (Cholamandalam) was located North-East of Pandyas between which two rivers?
    a) Kaveri and Vaigai
    b) Penner and Vellar
    c) Periyar and Pamba
    d) Godavari and Krishna
    Answer: b) Penner and Vellar rivers

68. What was the Chola capital famous for cotton trade?
    a) Puhar
    b) Uraiyaur
    c) Tanjore
    d) Gangaikondacholapuram
    Answer: b) Uraiyaur

69. What was the emblem of the Chola kingdom?
    a) Bow
    b) Carp
    c) Tiger
    d) Fish
    Answer: c) Tiger

70. What was the famous port of the Chola kingdom?
    a) Muzris
    b) Korkai
    c) Puhar
    d) Saliyur
    Answer: c) Puhar

71. Who was the earliest known Chola king, who conquered Sri Lanka and ruled over it for 50 years?
    a) Karikala
    b) Rajaraja
    c) Rajendra I
    d) Elara
    Answer: d) Elara

72. Who founded the capital city Puhar/Kaveripatnam and constructed an embankment along the Cauveri river?
    a) Elara
    b) Karikala
    c) Rajaraja
    d) Rajendra I
    Answer: b) Karikala

73. The Chera kingdom was part of which two present-day regions?
    a) Karnataka and Andhra Pradesh
    b) Kerala and Tamil Nadu
    c) Maharashtra and Gujarat
    d) Odisha and West Bengal
    Answer: b) Kerala and Tamil Nadu

74. What was the capital of the Chera kingdom?
    a) Uraiyaur
    b) Madurai
    c) Venji of Karur
    d) Muzris
    Answer: c) Venji of Karur

75. What was the emblem of the Chera kingdom?
    a) Tiger
    b) Carp
    c) Bow
    d) Fish
    Answer: c) Bow

76. Which of the following was NOT a famous port of the Chera kingdom?
    a) Muzris
    b) Todi
    c) Bandar
    d) Puhar
    Answer: d) Puhar

77. Who was one of the earliest known Chera rulers, given the title Udiyangeral because he served both armies of the Kurukshetra War?
    a) Senguttuvan
    b) Udiyangera
    c) Nedunjelian
    d) Mudukudumi
    Answer: b) Udiyangera

78. Who was the greatest Chera King, also known as Red Chera, who invaded the north and crossed Ganga?
    a) Udiyangera
    b) Senguttuvan
    c) Nedunjelian
    d) Mudukudumi
    Answer: b) Senguttuvan / Red Chera

79. The Pandya kingdom was located in the:
    a) North-East of Pandyas
    b) Part of Kerala and Tamil Nadu
    c) Southern most part of India
    d) Bundelkhand region
    Answer: c) Southern most part of India

80. What was the capital of the Pandya kingdom?
    a) Uraiyaur
    b) Venji
    c) Madurai
    d) Korkai
    Answer: c) Mudurai

81. What was the emblem of the Pandya kingdom?
    a) Tiger
    b) Bow
    c) Carp
    d) Elephant
    Answer: c) Carp

82. Which of the following was NOT a famous port of the Pandya kingdom?
    a) Korkai
    b) Saliyur
    c) Muzris
    d) Puhar (implicitly, as it is Chola)
    Answer: c) Muzris (Muzris was a Chera port)

83. Who was the earliest known Pandyan ruler?
    a) Nedunjelian
    b) Kovalan
    c) Mudukudumi
    d) Senguttuvan
    Answer: c) Mudukudumi

84. Who was the most important king of Pandya, who accused Kovalan of theft, leading to Madurai being laid under a curse by Kannagi?
    a) Mudukudumi
    b) Nedunjelian
    c) Kovalan
    d) Nakkirar
    Answer: b) Nedunjelian

Sangams Venue Chairman Surviving Text

85. Which Sangam's work has not survived?
    a) 1st Sangam
    b) 2nd Sangam
    c) 3rd Sangam
    d) All Sangams
    Answer: a) 1st Sangam

86. Which text survived from the 2nd Sangam?
    a) Ettutogati
    b) Patinenki lakanakku
    c) Pattu-Pattu
    d) Tolakappiyan (Tamil Grammar)
    Answer: d) Tolakappiyan (Tamil Grammar)

87. Which texts survived from the 3rd Sangam?
    a) Tolakappiyan
    b) Ettutogati, Patinenki lakanakku, pattu-Pattu
    c) Silpaddikaram
    d) Manimekhalai
    Answer: b) Ettutogati, Patinenki lakanakku, pattu-Pattu

ANCIENT INDIAN BOOKS & ITS AUTHORS

88. Who wrote 'Ashtadhyayi'?
    a) Kalidas
    b) Panini
    c) Asvaghosha
    d) Charak
    Answer: b) Panini

89. Who wrote 'Buddhacharita'?
    a) Asvaghosha
    b) Nagarjuna
    c) Vasubandhu
    d) Charak
    Answer: a) Asvaghosha

90. Who wrote 'Charak Samhita'?
    a) Sushruta
    b) Charak
    c) Patanjali
    d) Vijnaneshwara
    Answer: b) Charak

91. Who wrote 'Devi Chandraguptam'?
    a) Sudraka
    b) Vishakhdatta
    c) Harsha
    d) Jayadeva
    Answer: b) Vishakadutta

92. Who wrote 'Gita Govind'?
    a) Banabhatta
    b) Jayadeva
    c) Harshavardhana
    d) Kalidas
    Answer: b) Jayadeva

93. Who wrote 'Harsha Charita'?
    a) Harsha
    b) Hiuen Tsang
    c) Banabhatta
    d) Kalhana
    Answer: c) Banabhatta

94. Who wrote 'Kadambari'?
    a) Harsha
    b) Banabhatta
    c) Jayadeva
    d) Kalhana
    Answer: b) Banabhatta

95. Who wrote 'Kumar Sambhava'?
    a) Kalidas
    b) Bhavabhuti
    c) Magha
    d) Sudraka
    Answer: a) Kalidas

96. Who wrote 'Kumarpalcharita'?
    a) Hemchandra
    b) Patanjali
    c) Vijnaneshwara
    d) Sandhyakara Nandi
    Answer: a) Hemchandra

97. Who wrote 'Mahabhashya'?
    a) Panini
    b) Patanjali
    c) Vijnaneshwara
    d) Susruta
    Answer: b) Patanjali

98. Who wrote 'Mahavir Charita'?
    a) Bhavabhuti
    b) Jayadeva
    c) Hemchandra
    d) Sandhyakara Nandi
    Answer: a) Bhavabhuti

99. Who wrote 'Malvikagnimitram'?
    a) Kalidas
    b) Sudraka
    c) Vishakhdatta
    d) Harshavardhana
    Answer: a) Kalidas

100. Who wrote 'Meghduta'?
    a) Kalidas
    b) Bhavabhuti
    c) Magha
    d) Jayadeva
    Answer: a) Kalidas

101. Who wrote 'Mitakshara'?
    a) Patanjali
    b) Vijnaneshwara
    c) Susruta
    d) Charak
    Answer: b) Vijnaneshwara

102. Who wrote 'Mrichhkatikam'?
    a) Vishakhdatta
    b) Sudraka
    c) Harshavardhana
    d) Bharata
    Answer: b) Sudraka

103. Who wrote 'Mudra Rakshasha'?
    a) Vishakhdatta
    b) Sudraka
    c) Kalidas
    d) Harshavardhana
    Answer: a) Vishakhdatta

104. Who wrote 'Nagananda'?
    a) Kalidas
    b) Banabhatta
    c) Harshavardhana
    d) Jayadeva
    Answer: c) Harshavardhana

105. Who wrote 'Natya Shashtra'?
    a) Bharata
    b) Kamandaki
    c) Hemchandra
    d) Sandhyakara Nandi
    Answer: a) Bharata

106. Who wrote 'Nitisara'?
    a) Bharata
    b) Kamandaki
    c) Hemchandra
    d) Sandhyakara Nandi
    Answer: b) Kamandaki

107. Who wrote 'Priyadarshika'?
    a) Kalidas
    b) Banabhatta
    c) Harshavardhana
    d) Jayadeva
    Answer: c) Harshavardhana

108. Who wrote 'Ramcharita'?
    a) Bhavabhuti
    b) Hemchandra
    c) Sandhyakara Nandi
    d) Kamban
    Answer: c) Sandhyakara Nandi

109. Who wrote 'Ritu Samhara'?
    a) Kalidas
    b) Bhavabhuti
    c) Magha
    d) Sudraka
    Answer: a) Kalidas

110. Who wrote 'Sisupal Vadha'?
    a) Bhavabhuti
    b) Magha
    c) Jayadeva
    d) Hemchandra
    Answer: b) Magha

111. Who wrote 'Susruta Samhita'?
    a) Charak
    b) Susruta
    c) Patanjali
    d) Vijnaneshwara
    Answer: b) Susruta

Revenue Terminologies

112. What was 'Karai'?
    a) Tribute paid by feudatories
    b) Extra demand
    c) Custom duties
    d) Land tax
    Answer: d) Land tax

113. What was 'Irai'?
    a) Land tax
    b) Tribute paid by feudatories and body collected in war
    c) Extra demand
    d) Custom duties
    Answer: b) Tribute paid by feudatories and body collected in war

114. What was 'Iravu'?
    a) Land tax
    b) Tribute paid by feudatories
    c) Extra demand or forced gift
    d) Custom duties
    Answer: c) Extra demand or forced gift

115. What was 'Ulgu'?
    a) Land tax
    b) Tribute paid by feudatories
    c) Extra demand
    d) Custom duties
    Answer: d) Custom duties

116. Who was a 'Variyar'?
    a) Administrator
    b) Tax collector
    c) Military commander
    d) Village headman
    Answer: b) Tax collector

117. What was a 'Variyan'?
    a) A type of tax
    b) A well-known unit of territory yielding tax
    c) A military unit
    d) A type of coin
    Answer: b) A well known unit of territory yielding tax



MEDIEVAL INDIA

1.  Between 750 A.D. – 1000 A.D., a number of powerful empires arose in Northern India and the Deccan, commonly known as the:
    A) Mughal Age
    B) Gupta Age
    C) Rajput Age
    D) Maurya Age
    Answer: C) Rajput Age

2.  Which of the following empires were prominent in Northern India and the Deccan between 750 A.D. – 1000 A.D.?
    A) Gurjara Prathihars, Palas, Rastrakutas
    B) Chauhan, Parmars, Chalukya
    C) Gahadawal, Tomars
    D) All of the above
    Answer: D) All of the above

3.  When did Mahmud Ghazni invade Kannauj?
    A) 1001 A.D.
    B) 1025 A.D.
    C) 1018 A.D.
    D) 1030 A.D.
    Answer: C) 1018 A.D.

4.  In which year did Mahmud Ghazni invade Somnath?
    A) 1001 A.D.
    B) 1018 A.D.
    C) 1025 A.D.
    D) 1030 A.D.
    Answer: C) 1025 A.D.

5.  When did Muhammad Ghori return to India after the Second Battle of Tarain?
    A) 1191 A.D.
    B) 1192 A.D.
    C) 1194 A.D.
    D) 1206 A.D.
    Answer: C) 1194 A.D.

6.  The battles of Tarain and Chandawar laid the foundations of which rule in India?
    A) Mughal rule
    B) Maratha rule
    C) Turkish rule
    D) British rule
    Answer: C) Turkish rule

7.  Who laid the foundation of the Slave dynasty in 1206 A.D. after the death of Muhammad Ghori?
    A) Iltutmish
    B) Razia Sultan
    C) Qutubuddin Aibak
    D) Balban
    Answer: C) Qutubuddin Aibak

MUSLIM INVASION - THE GHAZNAVIS

8.  Who established an independent kingdom with its capital at Ghazni?
    A) Mahmud
    B) Firdausi
    C) Alpatgin
    D) Jayapala
    Answer: C) Alpatgin

9.  When did Mahmud ascend the throne at Ghazni?
    A) 998 A.D.
    B) 1001 A.D.
    C) 1018 A.D.
    D) 1025 A.D.
    Answer: A) 998 A.D.

10. Who was the poet laureate at the court of Mahmud?
    A) Amir Khusro
    B) Minhaj-us-Siraj
    C) Firdausi
    D) Al-Biruni
    Answer: C) Firdausi

11. Who wrote ‘Shah Namah’?
    A) Mahmud
    B) Alpatgin
    C) Firdausi
    D) Jayapala
    Answer: C) Firdausi

12. How many raids is Mahmud said to have made into India?
    A) 10
    B) 12
    C) 15
    D) 17
    Answer: D) 17

13. In which year was Jayapala, the Hindushahi ruler, defeated by Mahmud?
    A) 998 A.D.
    B) 1001 A.D.
    C) 1018 A.D.
    D) 1025 A.D.
    Answer: B) 1001 A.D.

14. After the defeat of Jayapala, which region passed into the hands of Ghaznavis?
    A) Delhi
    B) Kannauj
    C) Punjab
    D) Somnath
    Answer: C) Punjab

15. When did Mahmud die?
    A) 1018 A.D.
    B) 1025 A.D.
    C) 1030 A.D.
    D) 1001 A.D.
    Answer: C) 1030 A.D.

MUSLIM INVASION - THE TURKISH CONQUEST

16. Where did the Ghurid empire rise into prominence?
    A) North-East Afghanistan
    B) North-West Afghanistan
    C) South-East Afghanistan
    D) South-West Afghanistan
    Answer: B) North-West Afghanistan

17. In which year did Shahabuddin Muhammad (Muhammad Ghori) ascend the throne at Ghazni?
    A) 1170
    B) 1173
    C) 1190
    D) 1191
    Answer: B) 1173

18. By which year had Muhammad Ghori conquered Peshawar, Lahore, and Sialkot?
    A) 1173
    B) 1180
    C) 1190
    D) 1191
    Answer: C) 1190

19. Which dynasty had captured Delhi from the Tomars in India?
    A) Gahadawal
    B) Parmars
    C) Chauhans
    D) Chalukya
    Answer: C) Chauhans

20. The conflict between Muhammad Ghori and Prithviraj Chauhan started with rival claims for which place?
    A) Delhi
    B) Lahore
    C) Tabarhinda (Bhatinda)
    D) Kannauj
    Answer: C) Tabarhinda (Bhatinda)

21. When was the First Battle of Tarain fought?
    A) 1190 A.D.
    B) 1191 A.D.
    C) 1192 A.D.
    D) 1194 A.D.
    Answer: B) 1191 A.D.

22. In the First Battle of Tarain, whose forces were completely routed?
    A) Prithviraj Chauhan's
    B) Muhammad Ghori's
    C) Jaichandra's
    D) Qutubuddin Aibak's
    Answer: B) Muhammad Ghori's

23. Who saved Muhammad Ghori's life in the First Battle of Tarain?
    A) A Rajput soldier
    B) A Turkish archer
    C) A young Khalji horseman
    D) An Afghan general
    Answer: C) A young Khalji horseman

24. When was the Second Battle of Tarain fought?
    A) 1191 A.D.
    B) 1192 A.D.
    C) 1194 A.D.
    D) 1206 A.D.
    Answer: B) 1192 A.D.

25. The Second Battle of Tarain is regarded as one of the turning points in:
    A) World History
    B) Indian History
    C) Afghan History
    D) Persian History
    Answer: B) Indian History

26. After the Second Battle of Tarain, where was Prithviraj Chauhan captured?
    A) Near Delhi
    B) Near Ghazni
    C) Near Saraswati
    D) Near Bhatinda
    Answer: C) Near Saraswati

27. After Tarain, where did Muhammad Ghori return?
    A) Delhi
    B) Lahore
    C) Ghazni
    D) Peshawar
    Answer: C) Ghazni

28. Which Sufi Saint came with Muhammad Ghori from Afghanistan?
    A) Sheikh Salim Chisti
    B) Nizamuddin Auliya
    C) Khwaja Moinuddin Chisti
    D) Baba Farid
    Answer: C) Khwaja Moinuddin Chisti

29. To whom did Muhammad Ghori leave the affairs in India?
    A) Iltutmish
    B) Balban
    C) Qutubuddin Aibak
    D) Malik Kafur
    Answer: C) Qutubuddin Aibak

30. When was the Battle of Chandawar fought?
    A) 1191 A.D.
    B) 1192 A.D.
    C) 1194 A.D.
    D) 1206 A.D.
    Answer: C) 1194 A.D.

31. The Battle of Chandawar was fought between Muhammad Ghori and:
    A) Prithviraj Chauhan
    B) Jaichandra
    C) Hammirdev
    D) Ratan Singh
    Answer: B) Jaichandra

32. Who was defeated in the Battle of Chandawar?
    A) Muhammad Ghori
    B) Prithviraj Chauhan
    C) Jaichandra
    D) Qutubuddin Aibak
    Answer: C) Jaichandra

33. Which battles laid the foundations of Turkish rule in India?
    A) Battle of Panipat and Battle of Tarain
    B) Battle of Chandawar and Battle of Plassey
    C) Battles of Tarain and Chandawar
    D) Battle of Talikota and Battle of Halidighati
    Answer: C) Battles of Tarain and Chandawar

DELHI SULTANATE

34. How many ruling dynasties did the Sultanate of Delhi have?
    A) Three
    B) Four
    C) Five
    D) Six
    Answer: C) Five

35. What was the tenure of The Ilbari (The Slave Dynasty)?
    A) 1206-1290 AD
    B) 1290-1320 AD
    C) 1320-1413 AD
    D) 1414 - 1451 AD
    Answer: A) 1206-1290 AD

36. What was the tenure of The Khalji Dynasty?
    A) 1206-1290 AD
    B) 1290-1320 AD
    C) 1320-1413 AD
    D) 1414 - 1451 AD
    Answer: B) 1290-1320 AD

37. What was the tenure of The Tughluq Dynasty?
    A) 1206-1290 AD
    B) 1290-1320 AD
    C) 1320-1413 AD
    D) 1414 - 1451 AD
    Answer: C) 1320-1413 AD

38. What was the tenure of The Saiyid Dynasty?
    A) 1290-1320 AD
    B) 1320-1413 AD
    C) 1414 - 1451 AD
    D) 1451 - 1526 AD
    Answer: C) 1414 - 1451 AD

39. What was the tenure of The Lodhi Dynasty?
    A) 1320-1413 AD
    B) 1414 - 1451 AD
    C) 1451 - 1526 AD
    D) 1206-1290 AD
    Answer: C) 1451 - 1526 AD

DELHI SULTANATE - The Ilbari (The Slave Dynasty)

QUTUBUDDIN AIBAK (1206-1210)

40. Where was Qutubuddin Aibak crowned?
    A) Delhi
    B) Agra
    C) Lahore
    D) Kannauj
    Answer: C) Lahore

41. How did Qutubuddin Aibak die?
    A) In battle
    B) From illness
    C) While playing chaugan (horse polo)
    D) Assassinated
    Answer: C) While playing chaugan (horse polo)

42. What sobriquet did Qutubuddin Aibak earn due to his generosity?
    A) Sultan-e-Azam
    B) Lak-Baksha
    C) Malik-Naib
    D) Ghazi
    Answer: B) Lak-Baksha

43. To whom did Qutubuddin Aibak get his daughter married?
    A) Muhammad Ghori
    B) Aram Shah
    C) Iltutmish
    D) Balban
    Answer: C) Iltutmish

44. Qutubuddin Aibak laid the foundation of Qutab Minar after the name of which famous Sufi saint?
    A) Sheikh Salim Chisti
    B) Nizamuddin Auliya
    C) Khwaja Moinuddin Chisti
    D) Khwaja Qutbuddin Bakhtiar Kaki
    Answer: D) Khwaja Qutbuddin Bakhtiar Kaki

45. Who completed the construction of Qutub Minar?
    A) Qutubuddin Aibak
    B) Aram Shah
    C) Iltutmish
    D) Balban
    Answer: C) Iltutmish

46. Who succeeded Qutubuddin Aibak?
    A) Iltutmish
    B) Ruknuddin Firoz
    C) Aram Shah
    D) Razia
    Answer: C) Aram Shah

47. For how long did Aram Shah rule at Lahore?
    A) One year
    B) About eight months
    C) Two years
    D) Five months
    Answer: B) About eight months

48. Which sport did the Turks introduce in India?
    A) Chess
    B) Wrestling
    C) Polo
    D) Archery
    Answer: C) Polo

49. What was the name of the first mosque constructed by Aibak in India?
    A) Adhye Din ka Jhopara
    B) Jama Masjid
    C) Quwwatul- Islam
    D) Moti Masjid
    Answer: C) Quwwatul- Islam

50. Where is Adhye Din ka Jhopara located?
    A) Delhi
    B) Lahore
    C) Ajmer, Rajasthan
    D) Agra
    Answer: C) Ajmer, Rajasthan

ILTUTMISH

51. At the time of Qutubuddin’s death, what was Iltutmish the governor of?
    A) Delhi
    B) Lahore
    C) Badaun
    D) Sirhind
    Answer: C) Badaun

52. Who made Delhi the capital of his empire?
    A) Qutubuddin Aibak
    B) Iltutmish
    C) Balban
    D) Razia
    Answer: B) Iltutmish

53. Who is considered the real founder of Delhi Sultanate?
    A) Qutubuddin Aibak
    B) Iltutmish
    C) Muhammad Ghori
    D) Balban
    Answer: B) Iltutmish

54. Who was the slave of Qutubuddin Aibak?
    A) Balban
    B) Iltutmish
    C) Malik Kafur
    D) Aram Shah
    Answer: B) Iltutmish

55. Which two surviving officers of Mohd. Ghori did Iltutmish defeat?
    A) Muhammad Ghori and Qutubuddin Aibak
    B) Tajuddin Yalduz and Nasiruddin Qubacha
    C) Malik Chajju and Abdullah
    D) Altunia and Jamaluddin Yakut
    Answer: B) Tajuddin Yalduz and Nasiruddin Qubacha

56. What did Iltutmish form to suppress rebels?
    A) Diwan-i-Mustakharaj
    B) Turkan-i-Chahalgani or chalisa
    C) Diwan-I-Ariz
    D) Malik Naib
    Answer: B) Turkan-i-Chahalgani or chalisa

57. What was 'Iqta' under Iltutmish's reign?
    A) A type of currency
    B) A tax levied on land
    C) An assignment of land in lieu of salary
    D) A military rank
    Answer: C) An assignment of land in lieu of salary

58. Which two coins did Iltutmish introduce as the basic coins of the sultanate?
    A) Gold Dinar and Silver Dirham
    B) Silver Tanka and Copper Jital
    C) Gold Mohur and Silver Rupee
    D) Bronze Fulus and Copper Paisa
    Answer: B) Silver Tanka and Copper Jital

59. What kind of coin did Iltutmish introduce for the first time in India?
    A) Persian coin
    B) Arabian coin
    C) Indian coin
    D) Turkish coin
    Answer: B) Arabian coin

60. From where did Iltutmish transfer his capital to Delhi?
    A) Ghazni
    B) Kannauj
    C) Lahore
    D) Agra
    Answer: C) Lahore

61. In which year did Iltutmish receive a deed of investiture from the Abassid caliph of Baghdad?
    A) 1206
    B) 1210
    C) 1229
    D) 1236
    Answer: C) 1229

62. What was the significance of the deed of investiture received by Iltutmish?
    A) It made him the sole ruler of the entire India.
    B) It was a formal recognition of his independent position as a sultan of Delhi Sultanate and as a member of the world fraternity of Islamic states.
    C) It declared him as the successor of Muhammad Ghori.
    D) It allowed him to invade further into South India.
    Answer: B) It was a formal recognition of his independent position as a sultan of Delhi Sultanate and as a member of the world fraternity of Islamic states.

63. Who completed the construction of Qutub Minar?
    A) Qutubuddin Aibak
    B) Ruknuddin Firoz
    C) Iltutmish
    D) Balban
    Answer: C) Iltutmish

    B) Sajda and Paibos

    B) Ratan Singh

    B) Malik Kafur

    D) Tota-i-Hind
