    A) Delhi
    B) Ajmer
    C) Multan
    D) Ajodhan (Punjab)
    Answer: D) Ajodhan (Punjab)

SUFI MOVEMENT

73. Who founded the Suhrawardi order in India?
    A) Muinuddin Chishti
    B) Bahauddin Zakaria of Multan
    C) Shaikh Nizamuddin Auliya
    D) Khwaja Baqi Billah
    Answer: B) Bahauddin Zakaria of Multan

74. In which region of India did the Suhrawardi sufis mainly establish themselves?
    A) South India
    B) East India
    C) North-West India
    D) Central India
    Answer: C) North-West India

75. What did the Chishti mystics believe in regarding music?
    A) They forbade music.
    B) They believed in the spiritual value of Music.
    C) They used music only for entertainment.
    D) They allowed music only in specific rituals.
    Answer: B) They believed in the spiritual value of Music.

76. Who died in a state of ecstasy under the spell of Music?
    A) Muinuddin Chishti
    B) Khwaja Qutubuddin Bakhtiar Kaki
    C) Farid Ganj-i-Shakar
    D) Shaikh Nizamuddin Auliya
    Answer: B) Khwaja Qutubuddin Bakhtiar Kaki

77. By what name was Khwaja Fariduddin Masud known?
    A) Chiragh-i-Delhi
    B) Baba Farid
    C) Imam-i-Adil
    D) Zinda Pir
    Answer: B) Baba Farid

78. Who was the Chief disciple of Baba Farid?
    A) Khwaja Qutubuddin Bakhtiar Kaki
    B) Shaikh Nizamuddin Auliya
    C) Shaikh Husamuddin
    D) Shah Barkatullah
    Answer: B) Shaikh Nizamuddin Auliya

79. Which two rulers did Nizamuddin Auliya have problems with?
    A) Akbar and Jahangir
    B) Humayun and Sher Shah
    C) Mubarak Khalji and Ghayasuddin Tughlaq
    D) Babur and Ibrahim Lodi
    Answer: C) Mubarak Khalji and Ghayasuddin Tughlaq

80. Who were prominent Sufis in Gujarat?
    A) Shaikh Nizamuddin Auliya and Baba Farid
    B) Shaikh Husamuddin and Shah Barkatullah
    C) Shaikh Abdul Quddus Gangohi and Salim Chishti
    D) Khwaja Mir Dard and Mullah daud
    Answer: B) Shaikh Husamuddin and Shah Barkatullah

81. To which order did Shaikh Abdul Quddus Gangohi belong?
    A) Chishti
    B) Suhrawardi
    C) Sabiria
    D) Naqshabandi
    Answer: C) Sabiria order

82. To which Sufi saint was Akbar greatly devoted?
    A) Muinuddin Chishti
    B) Shaikh Nizamuddin Auliya
    C) Salim Chishti of Fatehpur
    D) Khwaja Baqi Billah
    Answer: C) Salim Chishti of Fatehpur

83. Who was the disciple of Mohammad Ghaus?
    A) Abul Fazal
    B) Faizi
    C) Tansen
    D) Birbal
    Answer: C) Tansen

84. Dara belonged to which Sufi order?
    A) Chishti
    B) Suhrawardi
    C) Qadariya
    D) Naqshabandi
    Answer: C) Qadariya order

Sufi Sect and Founders

85. Who was the founder of the Chisti Sufi Sect?
    A) Shiabuddin Shurawardi
    B) Khwaza Moin-ud-din
    C) Sheikh Abdul Kadir Jalani
    D) Shah Abdul Satari
    Answer: B) Khwaza Moin-ud-din

86. Who was the founder of the Shurawardi Sufi Sect?
    A) Khwaza Moin-ud-din
    B) Shiabuddin Shurawardi
    C) Sheikh Abdul Kadir Jalani
    D) Shah Abdul Satari
    Answer: B) Shiabuddin Shurawardi

87. Who was the founder of the Kadari Sufi Sect?
    A) Shiabuddin Shurawardi
    B) Khwaza Moin-ud-din
    C) Sheikh Abdul Kadir Jalani
    D) Shah Abdul Satari
    Answer: C) Sheikh Abdul Kadir Jalani

88. Who was the founder of the Satari Sufi Sect?
    A) Shiabuddin Shurawardi
    B) Khwaza Moin-ud-din
    C) Sheikh Abdul Kadir Jalani
    D) Shah Abdul Satari
    Answer: D) Shah Abdul Satari

89. Who was the founder of the Firdoshi Sufi Sect?
    A) Badruddin
    B) Khwaza Baqi Billah
    C) Shah Abdul Satari
    D) Sheikh Abdul Kadir Jalani
    Answer: A) Badruddin

90. Who was the founder of the Naqshabandi Sufi Sect?
    A) Badruddin
    B) Khwaza Baqi Billah
    C) Shah Abdul Satari
    D) Sheikh Abdul Kadir Jalani
    Answer: B) Khwaza Baqi Billah.

91. Which Sufi order was introduced in India by Baqi Billah?
    A) Chisti
    B) Suhrawardi
    C) Kadari
    D) Naqshabandi
    Answer: D) Naqshabandi

92. Shah Waliullah belonged to which Sufi order?
    A) Chisti
    B) Suhrawardi
    C) Naqshabandi
    D) Kadari
    Answer: C) Naqshabandi order

93. What did Shah Waliullah try to reconcile?
    A) Hindu and Muslim religions
    B) The two doctrines of Wahadat-ul Wujud and Wahadat-ul-Shuhud
    C) Sufism and Bhakti
    D) Political and religious authority
    Answer: B) The two doctrines of Wahadat-ul Wujud and Wahadat-ul-Shuhud

94. Who was the famous Urdu Poet named Khwaja Mir?
    A) Khwaja Mir Dard
    B) Khwaja Mir Taqi
    C) Khwaja Mir Alam
    D) Khwaja Mir Khan
    Answer: A) Khwaja Mir Dard

95. Who wrote 'Chhanda' in Hindavi?
    A) Tulsidas
    B) Surdas
    C) Mullah daud
    D) Kabir
    Answer: C) Mullah daud

96. Who was known as Chiragh-i-Delhi?
    A) Shaikh Nizamuddin Auliya
    B) Shaikh Nasiruddin
    C) Khwaja Muinuddin Chishti
    D) Baba Farid
    Answer: B) Shaikh Nasiruddin

THE MARATHAS

97. Where was Shivaji born?
    A) Poona
    B) Raigarh
    C) Javli
    D) Surat
    Answer: A) In the fort of Shivneri in Poona

98. In which year was Shivaji born?
    A) 1627
    B) 1646
    C) 1656
    D) 1663
    Answer: A) 1627

99. To which clan did Shivaji belong?
    A) Rajput
    B) Maratha
    C) Bhonsle
    D) Yadav
    Answer: C) Bhonsle

100. Who was Shivaji's father, who was in the service of Ahmadnagar?
    A) Sambhaji
    B) Shahji Bhonsle
    C) Dadaji Kondadeo
    D) Guru Ramdas
    Answer: B) Shahji Bhonsle

101. Which two individuals had great influence on the life of Shivaji?
    A) Shahji Bhonsle and Sambhaji
    B) Dadaji Kondadeo and Guru Ramdas
    C) Afzal Khan and Shaista Khan
    D) Aurangzeb and Jai Singh
    Answer: B) Dadaji Kondadeo and Guru Ramdas

102. What did Guru Ramdas write?
    A) Dasabodh
    B) Abhangas
    C) Padavali
    D) Zafarnama
    Answer: A) ‘Dasabodh’

103. Which jagir did Shahji give to Shivaji?
    A) Raigarh
    B) Torna
    C) Poona
    D) Javli
    Answer: C) Poona

104. Which fort did Shivaji first capture?
    A) Javli
    B) Torna
    C) Raigarh
    D) Purandhar
    Answer: B) Torna

105. In which year did Shivaji first capture Torna?
    A) 1627 A.D.
    B) 1646 A.D.
    C) 1656 A.D.
    D) 1663 A.D.
    Answer: B) 1646 A.D.

106. When did Shivaji begin his real career of conquest by conquering Javli from the Maratha Chief Chandra Rao More?
    A) 1646
    B) 1656
    C) 1663
    D) 1664
    Answer: B) 1656

107. In which year did Shivaji injure Shaista Khan, the Mughal Governor?
    A) 1656
    B) 1663
    C) 1664
    D) 1665
    Answer: B) 1663

108. When did Shivaji sack Surat for the first time?
    A) 1663
    B) 1664
    C) 1665
    D) 1666
    Answer: B) 1664

109. On which date was the Treaty of Purandhar signed?
    A) June 24, 1664 A.D.
    B) June 24, 1665 A.D.
    C) August 29, 1666 A.D.
    D) 1674 A.D.
    Answer: B) June 24, 1665 A.D.

110. In which year did Shivaji visit Agra?
    A) 1664
    B) 1665
    C) 1666
    D) 1674
    Answer: C) 1666

111. When did Shivaji escape from Agra?
    A) June 24, 1665
    B) August 29, 1666
    C) 1674
    D) 1680
    Answer: B) August 29, 1666

112. Where was Shivaji coronated?
    A) Poona
    B) Surat
    C) Raigarh
    D) Agra
    Answer: C) Raigarh

113. In which year was Shivaji coronated?
    A) 1666
    B) 1674
    C) 1680
    D) 1689
    Answer: B) 1674

114. What title did Shivaji assume at his coronation?
    A) Maharajadhiraja
    B) Samrat
    C) Chhatrapati Haindava Dharmodharak
    D) Sultan-e-Hind
    Answer: C) Chhatrapati Haindava Dharmodharak

115. When did Shivaji die?
    A) 1674
    B) 1680
    C) 1689
    D) 1707
    Answer: B) 1680

116. At what age did Shivaji die?
    A) 45
    B) 53
    C) 60
    D) 65
    Answer: B) 53

117. Who succeeded Shivaji?
    A) Rajaram
    B) Shambhaji
    C) Tarabai
    D) Shahji
    Answer: B) Sambhaji (1680-89)

118. What was the name of the administrative and revenue reforms introduced by Shivaji?
    A) Mansabdari system
    B) Zabti system
    C) Ashtapradhan
    D) Dahsala System
    Answer: C) Ashtapradhan

119. How were the provinces in Shivaji's administration divided?
    A) Into Sarkars and Parganas
    B) Into Prants and further subdivided into parganas or tarafs
    C) Into Subas and Districts
    D) Into Mandals and Kottams
    Answer: B) Into Prants and further subdivided into parganas or tarafs

120. Into what two classes was the cavalry divided under Shivaji?
    A) Faujdars and Thanedars
    B) Bargis and Silahdars
    C) Mansabdars and Zamindars
    D) Sowars and Dakhilis
    Answer: B) Bargis and Silahdars

121. What was 'Chauth'?
    A) A type of military service
    B) 1/4 of the land revenue paid to the Marathas to prevent raids
    C) A tax on trade
    D) A gift to the king
    Answer: B) 1/4 of the land revenue paid to the Marathas to that land would not be sullied to the Maratha raids.

122. What was 'Sardeshmukhi'?
    A) A tax on specific goods
    B) An additional levy of 10% demanded by Shivaji as hereditary Sardeshmukh
    C) A tribute from conquered territories
    D) A fee for judicial services
    Answer: B) An additional levy of 10%, which Shivaji demanded on the basis of being the hereditary Sardeshmukh.

123. What was 'Kathi' in Shivaji's revenue system?
    A) A type of currency
    B) A unit of land measurement in rods
    C) A tax on cattle
    D) A land grant
    Answer: B) Measuring in rods.

124. Who introduced various reforms in revenue in 1679?
    A) Shivaji
    B) Dadaji Kondadeo
    C) Annaji Datto
    D) Peshwa Balaji Vishwanath
    Answer: C) Annaji Datto

125. From which territory were Chauth and Sardeshmukhi collected?
    A) Maratha territory
    B) Mughal territory
    C) Deccan Sultanate territory
    D) Portuguese territory
    Answer: B) Mughal territory

ASHTAPRADHANA

126. What was the role of the 'Peshwa' in Ashtapradhana?
    A) Senapati
    B) Accountant
    C) General Administration; like Prime Minister
    D) Justice
    Answer: C) General Administration; like Prime Minister.

127. What was the role of the 'Sar-i-Naubat' in Ashtapradhana?
    A) Intelligence, posts and household affairs
    B) Senapati
    C) Correspondence
    D) Foreign affairs
    Answer: B) Senapati

128. What was the role of the 'Mazumdar' in Ashtapradhana?
    A) General Administration
    B) Accountant
    C) Foreign affairs
    D) Religious affairs
    Answer: B) Accountant

129. What was the role of the 'Waqia-i-navis' in Ashtapradhana?
    A) General Administration
    B) Accountant
    C) Intelligence, posts and household affairs
    D) Justice
    Answer: C) Intelligence, posts and household affairs.

130. What was the role of the 'Surnavis Chitnis' in Ashtapradhana?
    A) Intelligence, posts and household affairs
    B) Correspondence
    C) Foreign affairs
    D) Religious affairs
    Answer: B) Correspondence

131. What was the role of the 'Dabir/Sumant' in Ashtapradhana?
    A) Correspondence
    B) Foreign affairs and master of ceremonies
    C) Justice
    D) Religious affairs
    Answer: B) Foreign affairs and master of ceremonies.

132. What was the role of the 'Nyayadhis' in Ashtapradhana?
    A) General Administration
    B) Accountant
    C) Justice
    D) Foreign affairs
    Answer: C) Justice

133. What was the role of the 'Panditrao' in Ashtapradhana?
    A) Justice
    B) Religious affairs
    C) Military command
    D) Finance
    Answer: B) religious affairs.

MARATHA PESHWAS (1714-1818 A.D.)

134. Who was the first Peshwa listed in the provided text?
    A) Balaji Vishwanath
    B) Baji Rao I
    C) Balaji Baji Rao
    D) Madhava Rao Ballal
    Answer: A) Balaji Vishwanath

135. What was the period of Balaji Vishwanath's Peshwaship?
    A) 1714-1720
    B) 1720-1740
    C) 1740-1761
    D) 1761-1772
    Answer: A) 1714-1720

136. Who succeeded Balaji Vishwanath as Peshwa?
    A) Balaji Baji Rao
    B) Baji Rao I
    C) Madhava Rao Ballal
    D) Narayan Rao
    Answer: B) Baji Rao I

137. What was the period of Baji Rao I's Peshwaship?
    A) 1714-1720
    B) 1720-1740
    C) 1740-1761
    D) 1761-1772
    Answer: B) 1720-1740

138. Who was Peshwa after Baji Rao I?
    A) Madhava Rao Ballal
    B) Narayan Rao
    C) Balaji Baji Rao
    D) Raghunath Rao
    Answer: C) Balaji Baji Rao

    A) 1720-1740
    B) 1740-1761
    C) 1761-1772

140. Who was Peshwa after Balaji Baji Rao?

    A) 1740-1761
    B) 1761-1772
    C) 1772-1773
    D) 1773-1774
    Answer: B) 1761-1772

142. Who was Peshwa after Madhava Rao Ballal?
    A) Raghunath Rao
    B) Madhava Rao Narayan

    A) 1761-1772
    B) 1772-1773
    C) 1773-1774
    D) 1774-1796

144. Who was Peshwa after Narayan Rao?
    A) Madhava Rao Narayan
    B) Baji Rao II
    C) Raghunath Rao
    D) Balaji Vishwanath
    Answer: C) Raghunath Rao

145. What was the period of Raghunath Rao's Peshwaship?
    A) 1772-1773
    B) 1773-1774
    C) 1774-1796
    D) 1796-1818
    Answer: B) 1773-1774

146. Who was Peshwa after Raghunath Rao?
    A) Baji Rao II
    B) Balaji Vishwanath
    C) Madhava Rao Narayan
    D) Narayan Rao
    Answer: C) Madhava Rao Narayan

147. What was the period of Madhava Rao Narayan's Peshwaship?
    A) 1773-1774
    B) 1774-1796
    C) 1796-1818
    D) 1720-1740
    Answer: B) 1774-1796

148. Who was the last Peshwa listed in the provided text?
    A) Madhava Rao Ballal
    B) Madhava Rao Narayan
    C) Baji Rao II
    D) Balaji Baji Rao
    Answer: C) Baji Rao II

149. What was the period of Baji Rao II's Peshwaship?
    A) 1774-1796
    B) 1796-1818
    C) 1714-1720
    D) 1740-1761
    Answer: B) 1796-1818


MODERN INDIA
COMING OF EUROPEANS

1.  When did Vasco da Gama land at Calicut?
    A) 1492 A.D.
    B) 1498 A.D.
    C) 1502 A.D.
    D) 1505 A.D.
    Answer: B) 1498 A.D.

2.  With cargo worth how many times his expenses did Vasco da Gama return to Portugal in 1499?
    A) 10 times
    B) 20 times
    C) 40 times
    D) 60 times
    Answer: D) 60 times

3.  In which year did the Portuguese set up trading stations at Calicut, Cochin, and Cannore?
    A) 1498
    B) 1499
    C) 1502
    D) 1505
    Answer: C) 1502

4.  Which system, involving passes given to ships, was introduced by the Portuguese?
    A) Farman system
    B) Mansab system
    C) Cartaz system
    D) Zamindari system
    Answer: C) Cartaz system

5.  What was the early capital of the Portuguese in India?
    A) Goa
    B) Cochin
    C) Calicut
    D) Cannore
    Answer: B) Cochin

6.  Which city later replaced Cochin as the Portuguese capital in India?
    A) Calicut
    B) Cannore
    C) Goa
    D) Bombay
    Answer: C) Goa

7.  Who was the first Portuguese governor in India?
    A) Albuquerque
    B) Vasco da Gama
    C) De Almeida
    D) Nuno da Cunha
    Answer: C) De Almeida

8.  What was the tenure of De Almeida as the first Portuguese governor in India?
    A) 1502-1505
    B) 1505-1509
    C) 1509-1515
    D) 1515-1520
    Answer: B) 1505-1509

9.  Who was appointed the second governor of Portuguese in India?
    A) De Almeida
    B) Vasco da Gama
    C) Albuquerque
    D) Cabral
    Answer: C) Albuquerque

10. In which year was Albuquerque appointed the second governor of Portuguese in India?
    A) 1505
    B) 1509
    C) 1510
    D) 1515
    Answer: B) 1509

11. Which Portuguese governor encouraged his countrymen to marry Indian women and persecuted the Muslims?
    A) De Almeida
    B) Albuquerque
    C) Vasco da Gama
    D) Cabral
    Answer: B) Albuquerque

12. When was the Dutch East India Company formed?
    A) March 1600
    B) March 1602
    C) March 1605
    D) March 1609
    Answer: B) March 1602

13. What was the name of the Dutch East India Company?
    A) British East India Company
    B) Danish East India Company
    C) Compagnie des Indes Orientales
    D) Uereenigde Oost Indische Companie
    Answer: D) ‘Uereenigde Oost Indische Companie’

14. What was the main centre of the Dutch in India until 1690?
    A) Nagapattam
    B) Cochin
    C) Pulicat
    D) Surat
    Answer: C) Pulicat

15. Which city replaced Pulicat as the main Dutch centre in India after 1690?
    A) Cochin
    B) Nagapattam
    C) Masulipatnam
    D) Chinsurah
    Answer: B) Nagapattam

16. In which battle were the Dutch finally defeated by the English, leading to their collapse in India?
    A) Battle of Plassey
    B) Battle of Buxar
    C) Battle of Wandiwash
    D) Battle of Badera
    Answer: D) Battle of Badera

17. In which year did the Battle of Badera take place?
    A) 1757
    B) 1759
    C) 1760
    D) 1763
    Answer: B) 1759

18. By which group of merchants was the English East India Company formed?
    A) The Merchant Traders
    B) The Merchant Adventurers
    C) The English Merchants
    D) The Royal Traders
    Answer: B) “The Merchant Adventurers”

19. In which year was the English East India Company formed?
    A) 1599
    B) 1600
    C) 1602
    D) 1609
    Answer: A) 1599

20. When did Captain Hawkins arrive at Jahangir's court?
    A) 1599
    B) 1600
    C) 1602
    D) 1609
    Answer: D) 1609

21. What mansab was given to Captain Hawkins at Jahangir's court?
    A) 100
    B) 200
    C) 300
    D) 400
    Answer: D) 400

22. Who was the ambassador of James-I to Jahangir’s court?
    A) Captain Hawkins
    B) Sir Thomas Roe
    C) John Mildenhall
    D) William Finch
    Answer: B) Sir Thomas Roe

23. In which year did the Portuguese give Bombay as a part of dowry to the British princess, on her marriage with Charles-II?
    A) 1600 A.D.
    B) 1611 A.D.
    C) 1661 A.D.
    D) 1717 A.D.
    Answer: C) 1661 A.D.

24. Who was the largest shareholder of the East India Company?
    A) King James I
    B) Charles II
    C) Queen Elizabeth
    D) Robert Clive
    Answer: C) Queen Elizabeth

25. What was the first English settlement on the East Coast of India?
    A) Surat
    B) Madras
    C) Masulipatnam
    D) Calcutta
    Answer: C) Masulipatnam

26. In which year was Masulipatnam established as the first English settlement on the East Coast?
    A) 1600
    B) 1609
    C) 1611
    D) 1616
    Answer: C) 1611

27. In which year did Farrukh Siyar confirm the privileges (farman) of 1691 and extend them to Gujarat and Deccan?
    A) 1691
    B) 1707
    C) 1717
    D) 1742
    Answer: C) 1717

28. When did the Danes form an East India Company?
    A) 1602
    B) 1616
    C) 1664
    D) 1668
    Answer: B) 1616

29. What was the headquarter of the Danes in India?
    A) Tranquebar
    B) Serampore
    C) Surat
    D) Pondicherry
    Answer: B) Serampore

30. Who established the first French factory at Surat?
    A) Marcara
    B) Dupleix
    C) Francois Caron
    D) Bellanger de Lespinay
    Answer: C) Francois Caron

31. In which year was the first French factory established at Surat?
    A) 1664
    B) 1668
    C) 1669
    D) 1673
    Answer: B) 1668

32. Who established a French factory at Masulipatnam in 1669?
    A) Francois Caron
    B) Marcara
    C) Dupleix
    D) Bellanger de Lespinay
    Answer: B) Marcara

33. In which year were the villages of Valikondapuram (Pondicherry) acquired by Francois and Bellanger de Lespinay?
    A) 1668
    B) 1669
    C) 1673
    D) 1742
    Answer: C) 1673

34. When did Dupleix, the French Governor, arrive in India, marking the beginning of the Anglo-French conflict?
    A) 1673
    B) 1742
    C) 1744
    D) 1760
    Answer: B) 1742

35. In which battle were the French finally defeated by the English in 1760?
    A) Battle of Plassey
    B) Battle of Buxar
    C) Battle of Wandiwash
    D) Battle of Badera
    Answer: C) Battle of Wandiwash

36. By which treaty did the French lose almost all Indian settlements to the English in 1763?
    A) Treaty of Aix-La-Chapelle
    B) Treaty of Pondicherry
    C) Treaty of Paris
    D) Treaty of Srirangapatnam
    Answer: C) Treaty of Paris

Establishment of First European Factories in India

37. What was the Year of Establishment for Estado da India (Portuguese Company)?
    A) 1498
    B) 1600
    C) 1602
    D) 1616
    Answer: A) 1498

38. What was the Year of Establishment for British East India Company?
    A) 1498
    B) 1600
    C) 1602
    D) 1616
    Answer: B) 1600

39. What was the Year of Establishment for Verrenigde Oost Indische Companie (Dutch)?
    A) 1498
    B) 1600
    C) 1602
    D) 1616
    Answer: C) 1602

40. What was the Year of Establishment for Danish East India Company?
    A) 1600
    B) 1602
    C) 1616
    D) 1664
    Answer: C) 1616

41. What was the Year of Establishment for Compagnie des Indes Orientales (French)?
    A) 1602
    B) 1616
    C) 1664
    D) 1668
    Answer: C) 1664

ENTRY OF EUROPEAN POWERS (Order)

42. What is the correct chronological order of the entry of European powers into India?
    A) Dutch → Portuguese → English → Danes → French
    B) Portuguese → Dutch → English → Danes → French
    C) English → Portuguese → Dutch → Danes → French
    D) Portuguese → English → Dutch → Danes → French
    Answer: B) Portuguese → Dutch → English → Danes → French

ESTABLISHMENT OF BRITISH RULES

43. Which of the following events did NOT contribute to the establishment of British Supremacy in India?
    A) The Carnatic Wars
    B) Battle of Plassey
    C) Anglo-Sikh wars
    D) Revolt of 1857
    Answer: D) Revolt of 1857

THE CARNATIC WARS

44. The Carnatic Wars were conflicts between which two European powers?
    A) Anglo-Dutch
    B) Anglo-Portuguese
    C) Anglo-French
    D) Anglo-Danish
    Answer: C) Anglo-French

45. For how many years did the Carnatic Wars continue?
    A) 10 years
    B) 15 years
    C) 20 years
    D) 25 years
    Answer: C) 20 years

46. What was the period of the First Carnatic War?
    A) 1742-1746
    B) 1744-1748
    C) 1749-1754
    D) 1758-1763
    Answer: B) 1744-1748

47. Which city did the French capture during the First Carnatic War?
    A) Pondicherry
    B) Madras
    C) Fort St. David
    D) Arcot
    Answer: B) Madras

48. Which treaty ended the First Carnatic War, also concluding the War of Austrian Succession?
    A) Treaty of Pondicherry
    B) Treaty of Paris
    C) Treaty of Aix-La-Chapelle
    D) Treaty of Madras
    Answer: C) Treaty of Aix-La-Chapelle

49. What was the period of the Second Carnatic War?
    A) 1744-1748
    B) 1749-1754
    C) 1758-1763
    D) 1766-1769
    Answer: B) 1749-1754

50. With whom did Dupleix align in the Second Carnatic War?
    A) Haider Ali and Tipu Sultan
    B) Muzaffar Jung (Hyderabad) and Chanda Sahib (Carnatic)
    C) Nizam and Marathas
    D) Siraj-ud-Daulah and Mir Qasim
    Answer: B) Muzaffar Jung (Hyderabad) and Chanda Sahib (Carnatic)

51. Who emerged victorious for the English after initial reverses in the Second Carnatic War?
    A) General Eyre Coote
    B) Major Munro
    C) Robert Clive
    D) Lord Cornwallis
    Answer: C) Robert Clive

52. Who signed the Treaty of Pondicherry in 1755 with the English?
    A) Dupleix
    B) Godeheau
    C) Count de Lally
    D) Francois Caron
    Answer: B) Godeheau

53. What was the period of the Third Carnatic War?
    A) 1749-1754
    B) 1755-1757
    C) 1758-1763
    D) 1766-1769
    Answer: C) 1758-1763

54. Which fort did the French, under Count de Lally, capture during the Third Carnatic War?
    A) Fort William
    B) Fort St. George
    C) Fort St. David
    D) Fort St. Louis
    Answer: C) ‘Fort St.David’

55. In which battle were the French defeated at Wandiwash in 1760 during the Third Carnatic War?
    A) Battle of Plassey
    B) Battle of Buxar
    C) Battle of Wandiwash
    D) Battle of Badera
    Answer: C) Wandiwash

56. Who led the English forces at the Battle of Wandiwash?
    A) Robert Clive
    B) Major Munro
    C) General Eyre Coote
    D) Lord Cornwallis
    Answer: C) General Eyre Coote

57. By which treaty was Pondicherry returned to the French after the Third Carnatic War?
    A) Treaty of Pondicherry
    B) Treaty of Paris
    C) Treaty of Aix-La-Chapelle
    D) Treaty of Srirangapatnam
    Answer: B) Treaty of Paris

CONQUEST OF BENGAL

58. Who was the Nawab of Bengal against the misuse of Dastaks?
    A) Mir Jaffar
    B) Mir Qasim
    C) Sirajuddaulah
    D) Nizam-ud-daulah
    Answer: C) Nawab Sirajuddaulah

59. Which English factory did Nawab Sirajuddaulah besiege?
    A) Surat
    B) Calcutta
    C) Qasim Bazaar
    D) Madras
    Answer: C) English factory at Qasim Bazaar.

60. On what date was Fort William (Calcutta) captured by the Nawab?
    A) June 23rd, 1757
    B) June 20th, 1756
    C) October 22nd, 1764
    D) August 29th, 1766
    Answer: B) On 20th June, 1756

61. Who was placed in charge of Calcutta by the Nawab after its capture?
    A) Mir Jaffar
    B) Manik chand
    C) Aminchand
    D) Jagat Seth
    Answer: B) Manik chand

62. The 'Black Hole Episode' refers to the mass death of English soldiers in a chamber at which city?
    A) Murshidabad
    B) Qasim Bazaar
    C) Calcutta
    D) Monghyr
    Answer: C) Calcutta

63. When was Calcutta recovered by Clive?
    A) Late 1756
    B) Early 1757
    C) Mid 1757
    D) Late 1757
    Answer: B) In early 1757

64. When was the Battle of Plassey fought?
    A) June 20th, 1756
    B) June 23rd, 1757
    C) October 22nd, 1764
    D) August 29th, 1766
    Answer: B) 23rd June, 1757

65. Who led the English East India Company’s forces at the Battle of Plassey?
    A) Major Munro
    B) General Eyre Coote
    C) Robert Clive
    D) Lord Wellesley
    Answer: C) Robert Clive

66. Which of the following did NOT betray Nawab Siraj-ud-Daulah at the Battle of Plassey?
    A) Mir Jaffar
    B) Manikchand
    C) Dupleix
    D) Jagat Seth
    Answer: C) Dupleix

67. Who was proclaimed Nawab of Bengal after the Battle of Plassey?
    A) Siraj-ud-Daulah
    B) Mir Jaffar
    C) Mir Qasim
    D) Nizam-ud-daulah
    Answer: B) Mir Jaffar

68. What did the company receive as a result of the Battle of Plassey?
    A) The Diwani of Bengal
    B) The Zamindari of 24 Parganas and free trading rights in Bengal, Bihar and Orissa
    C) Control over the entire Bengal army
    D) The right to collect Chauth
    Answer: B) The Company received the Zamindari of 24 Parganas and free trading rights in Bengal, Bihar and Orissa.

69. In which year was Mir Jaffar replaced by Mir Qasim?
    A) 1757 A.D.
    B) 1760 A.D.
    C) 1764 A.D.
    D) 1765 A.D.
    Answer: B) 1760 A.D.

70. Where did Mir Qasim shift his capital?
    A) Murshidabad
    B) Calcutta
    C) Monghyr
    D) Patna
    Answer: C) Monghyr

71. Which territories did Mir Qasim cede?
    A) Calcutta, Hooghly, Chandannagar
    B) Burdwan, Midnapore and Chittagong
    C) Dhaka, Murshidabad, Patna
    D) Bihar, Bengal, Orissa
    Answer: B) Burdwan, Midnapore and Chittagong

72. Who were part of the confederation formed by Mir Qasim after fleeing to Awadh?
    A) Haider Ali and Tipu Sultan
    B) Nizam and Marathas
    C) Shujauddaulah and Shah Alam
    D) Dupleix and Count de Lally
    Answer: C) Shujauddaulah and Shah Alam

73. When was the Battle of Buxar fought?
    A) June 23rd, 1757
    B) October 22nd, 1764
    C) August 29th, 1766
    D) 1772
    Answer: B) 22nd October, 1764

74. Who defeated Mir Qasim in the Battle of Buxar?
    A) Robert Clive
    B) General Eyre Coote
    C) Major Munro
    D) Lord Wellesley
    Answer: C) Major Munro

75. What was a significant outcome of the Battle of Buxar for the British?
    A) It placed Bengal under the company.
    B) It placed Awadh under the company.
    C) It placed Mysore under the company.
    D) It placed the Marathas under the company.
    Answer: B) The Battle of Buxar placed Awadh under the company.

76. Who was again placed on the throne of Bengal after the Battle of Buxar?
    A) Mir Qasim
    B) Siraj-ud-Daulah
    C) Mir Jaffar
    D) Nizam-ud-daulah
    Answer: C) Mir Jaffar

77. Who was placed on the throne after the death of Mir Jaffar?
    A) Mir Qasim
    B) Shah Alam II
    C) Nizamud-daulah
    D) Shujauddaulah
    Answer: C) Nizamud- daulah

78. In which year did Nizam-ud-daulah sign the Treaty of Allahabad with Mughal emperor Shah Alam II?
    A) 1764
    B) 1765
    C) 1772
    D) 1784
    Answer: B) 1765

79. What did the Company gain by the Treaty of Allahabad?
    A) Free trading rights in Bengal
    B) The Diwani of Bihar, Bengal and Orissa
    C) The Zamindari of 24 Parganas
    D) Control over Awadh
    Answer: B) The Company got the Diwani of Bihar, Bengal and Orissa.

80. Who granted the Diwani to the company?
    A) Nawab of Bengal
    B) Mughal Emperor
    C) British Parliament
    D) Peshwa
    Answer: B) Mughal Emperor

81. Who introduced the Dual System of government in Bengal?
    A) Warren Hastings
    B) Robert Clive
    C) Lord Cornwallis
    D) Lord Wellesley
    Answer: B) Clive

82. In which year did Warren Hastings end the Dual System?
    A) 1765
    B) 1772
    C) 1773
    D) 1784
    Answer: B) 1772

ANGLO MYSORE WARS

83. What was the period of the First Anglo-Mysore War?
    A) 1760-1763
    B) 1766-1769
    C) 1780-1784
    D) 1789-1792
    Answer: B) 1766-1769

84. Who defeated the British army in the First Anglo-Mysore War?
    A) Tipu Sultan
    B) Haider Ali
    C) Nizam
    D) Marathas
    Answer: B) Haider Ali

85. Which treaty was signed at the end of the First Anglo-Mysore War?
    A) Treaty of Mangalore
    B) Treaty of Srirangapatnam
    C) Treaty of Madras
    D) Treaty of Bassein
    Answer: C) The Treaty of Madras

86. What was the period of the Second Anglo-Mysore War?
    A) 1766-1769
    B) 1780-1784
    C) 1789-1792
    D) 1799
    Answer: B) 1780-1784

87. Which French port, in Haider Ali’s territory, was attacked by Warren Hastings, leading to the Second Anglo-Mysore War?
    A) Pondicherry
    B) Mahe
    C) Karaikal
    D) Yanam
    Answer: B) Mahe

88. Who led a joint front with Nizam and Marathas and captured Arcot during the Second Anglo-Mysore War?
    A) Tipu Sultan
    B) Haider Ali
    C) Lord Cornwallis
    D) Lord Wellesley
    Answer: B) Haider Ali

89. In which year was Haider Ali defeated at Porto Novo by Eyre Coote?
    A) 1780
    B) 1781
    C) 1782
    D) 1784
    Answer: B) 1781

90. Which treaty was signed by Tipu Sultan at the end of the Second Anglo-Mysore War?
    A) Treaty of Madras
    B) Treaty of Srirangapatnam
    C) Treaty of Mangalore
    D) Treaty of Bassein
    Answer: C) Treaty of Mangalore (1784)

91. What was the period of the Third Anglo-Mysore War?
    A) 1780-1784
    B) 1789-1792
    C) 1799
    D) 1803-1806
    Answer: B) 1789-1792

92. Who aided the British in the Third Anglo-Mysore War?
    A) French and Dutch
    B) Portuguese and Danes
    C) Marathas and Nizam
    D) Sikhs and Rajputs
    Answer: C) Marathas and Nizam

93. Who captured Bangalore during the Third Anglo-Mysore War?
    A) Tipu Sultan
    B) Haider Ali
    C) Cornwallis
    D) Lord Wellesley
    Answer: C) Cornwallis

94. By which treaty did Tipu cede half of his territories after the Third Anglo-Mysore War?
    A) Treaty of Mangalore
    B) Treaty of Srirangapatnam
    C) Treaty of Madras
    D) Treaty of Bassein
    Answer: B) Treaty of Srirangapatnam

95. What was the period of the Fourth Anglo-Mysore War?
    A) 1789-1792
    B) 1796
    C) 1799
    D) 1802
    Answer: C) 1799

96. As a member of which club did Tipu enlist himself, also planting a tree of liberty at Srirangapatnam?
    A) The East India Club
    B) The Royal Society
    C) The Jacobin Club
    D) The Patriotic Club
    Answer: C) The Jacobin Club

97. Who attacked Mysore in the Fourth Anglo-Mysore War, leading to Tipu's death?
    A) Robert Clive
    B) General Eyre Coote
    C) Lord Cornwallis
    D) Lord Wellesley
    Answer: D) Lord Wellesley

98. Who participated in the Fourth Anglo-Mysore War, being the brother of the Governor-General?
    A) Robert Clive
    B) Arthur Wellesley
    C) Major Munro
    D) Charles Metcalfe
    Answer: B) Arthur Wellesley

ANGLO-MARATHA WARS

99. When was the First Anglo-Maratha War fought?
    A) 1775-1782 A.D.
    B) 1782-1785 A.D.
    C) 1803-1806 A.D.
    D) 1817-1818 A.D.
    Answer: A) 1775-1782 A.D.

100. Which treaty did the Britishers sign with Raghunath Rao in 1775?
    A) Treaty of Purandhar
    B) Treaty of Salbai
    C) Treaty of Surat
    D) Treaty of Bassein
    Answer: C) Treaty of Surat

101. In which year was the Treaty of Purandhar made?
    A) 1775
    B) 1782
    C) 1802
    D) 1803
    Answer: A) 1775

102. Which treaty gave 20 years of peace after the First Anglo-Maratha War?
    A) Treaty of Surat
    B) Treaty of Purandhar
    C) Treaty of Salbai
    D) Treaty of Bassein
    Answer: C) The Treaty of Salbai (1782)

103. When was the Second Anglo-Maratha War fought?
    A) 1775-1782 A.D.
    B) 1802-1803 A.D.
    C) 1803-1806 A.D.
    D) 1817-1818 A.D.
    Answer: C) 1803-1806 A.D.

104. What was the primary cause of the Second Maratha War?
    A) Treaty of Salbai
    B) Treaty of Purandhar
    C) Treaty of Bassein (1802)
    D) Treaty of Rajpurghat
    Answer: C) The Treaty of Bassein (1802)

105. Which Maratha factions were defeated in the Second Anglo-Maratha War?
    A) The Peshwa and the Gaekwad
    B) The Holkar and the Bhonsle
    C) The Scindia and the Bhonsle
    D) The Peshwa and the Scindia
    Answer: C) The Scindia and the Bhonsle

106. When was the Third Anglo-Maratha War fought?
    A) 1803-1806
    B) 1813-1817
    C) 1817-1818
    D) 1823-1828
    Answer: C) 1817-1818

107. What was the outcome of the Third Anglo-Maratha War?
    A) The Maratha confederacy strengthened.
    B) The Maratha confederacy was completely destroyed.
    C) The Marathas gained territory.
    D) The British signed a humiliating treaty.
    Answer: B) The Maratha confederacy was completely destroyed.

108. Who was the Peshwa dethroned and pensioned off at Bithur near Kanpur?
    A) Baji Rao I
    B) Balaji Baji Rao
    C) Baji Rao-II
    D) Madhava Rao Ballal
    Answer: C) Peshwa Baji Rao-II

ANGLO-SIKH WARS

109. What prevailed after the death of Ranjit Singh, leading to the Anglo-Sikh Wars?
    A) Economic prosperity
    B) Political stability
    C) Political instability
    D) Religious revival
    Answer: C) Period of political instability

110. When was the First Anglo-Sikh War fought?
    A) 1845-1846
    B) 1848-1849
    C) 1857-1858
    D) 1803-1806
    Answer: A) 1845-1846

111. Which treaty ended the First Anglo-Sikh War?
    A) Treaty of Amritsar
    B) Treaty of Lahore (1846)
    C) Treaty of Purandhar
    D) Treaty of Salbai
    Answer: B) Treaty of Lahore (1846)

112. When was the Second Anglo-Sikh War fought?
    A) 1845-1846
    B) 1848-1849
    C) 1857-1858
    D) 1803-1806
    Answer: B) 1848-1849

113. Who annexed Punjab in 1849 after the defeat of the Sikhs?
    A) Lord William Bentinck
    B) Lord Dalhousie
    C) Lord Canning
    D) Lord Ripon
    Answer: B) Lord Dalhousie

1857 REVOLT

114. On what date did the sepoys at Barrackpore refuse to use the greased cartridge?
    A) March 29th, 1857
    B) May 10th, 1857
    C) May 12th, 1857
    D) June 4th, 1857
    Answer: A) On 29th March, 1857

115. Who attacked and fired on the Adjutant at Barrackpore?
    A) Tantia Tope
    B) Nana Saheb
    C) Mangal Pandey
    D) Kunwar Singh
    Answer: C) Mangal Pandey

116. Which Native Infantry was disbanded after the Barrackpore incident?
    A) 19th Native Infantry
    B) 34th Native Infantry
    C) 47th Native Infantry
    D) 74th Native Infantry
    Answer: B) The 34 Native Infantry

117. On what date was Delhi seized by the rebels during the 1857 Revolt?
    A) March 29th, 1857
    B) May 10th, 1857
    C) May 12th, 1857
    D) June 4th, 1857
    Answer: C) 12th May 1857

1857 REVOLTS : AT A GLANCE

118. Who were the leaders of the Revolt of 1857 in Delhi?
    A) Nana Saheb, Tatia Tope
    B) Hazrat Mahal, Birjis Qadir
    C) Bahadur Shah, Bakht Khan
    D) Rani Laxmibai, Tatia Tope
    Answer: C) Bahadur Shah, Bakht Khan

119. When did the revolt in Delhi begin?
    A) 4 June, 57
    B) 5 June, 57
    C) 11 May, 57
    D) 12 June, 57
    Answer: C) 11 May, 57

120. Who suppressed the revolt in Delhi?
    A) Colin Campbell
    B) Gen. Hugh Rose
    C) Nicholson and Hudson
    D) Major William Taylor
    Answer: C) Nicholson and Hudson

121. When was the revolt in Delhi suppressed?
    A) Dec., 57
    B) 31 Mar., 58
    C) 17 June, 1858
    D) 20 Sep., 57
    Answer: D) 20 Sep., 57

    B) Nana Saheb, Tatia Tope

VIEW OVER THE REVOLT 1857

149. Who stated that the Revolt of 1857 was "Neither first nor National nor War of Independence"?
    A) V.D. Savarkar
    B) Sir John Seeley
    C) R.C. Majumdar
    D) Joutram and W Taylor
    Answer: C) R.C. Majumdar

150. Who called the Revolt of 1857 the "First war of Indian Independence"?
    A) R.C. Majumdar
    B) V.D. Savarkar
    C) Sir John Seeley
    D) Hugh Rose
    Answer: B) V.D. Savarkar

151. Who described the Revolt of 1857 as "Wholly unpatriotic and selfish sepoy Mutiny"?
    A) R.C. Majumdar
    B) V.D. Savarkar
    C) Sir John Seeley
    D) T.R. Holmes
    Answer: C) Sir John Seeley

152. Who viewed the Revolt of 1857 as a "Hindu-Muslim Conspiracy"?
    A) Hugh Rose
    B) T.R. Holmes
    C) Joutram and W Taylor
    D) R.C. Majumdar
    Answer: C) Joutram and W Taylor

153. Who made the statement: "Here lay the woman (Laxmi Bai), who was the only man among the rebels"?
    A) Sir John Seeley
    B) Joutram and W Taylor
    C) Hugh Rose
    D) T.R. Holmes
    Answer: C) Hugh Rose

154. Who characterized the Revolt of 1857 as a "Conflict between civilisation and Barbarism"?
    A) Hugh Rose
    B) T.R. Holmes
    C) R.C. Majumdar
    D) V.D. Savarkar
    Answer: B) T.R. Holmes

CAUSES OF FAILURE OF 1857 REVOLT

155. Which of the following was NOT a cause for the failure of the 1857 Revolt mentioned in the text?
    A) The revolt remained confined to a small part of North India.
    B) Lack of resources compared to the British.
    C) Lack of support from the general populace.
    D) The rebel leaders lacked political leadership, military experience and strategic knowledge.
    Answer: C) Lack of support from the general populace. (While the text mentions limited social base, "lack of support from general populace" is a broader and distinct option not explicitly stated as a cause here, though implied by "limited social base").

156. Which groups supported the British Authorities during the 1857 Revolt?
    A) Peasants and craftsmen
    B) Sikh, Marathas, Rajputs and some other sections of society
    C) Zamindars and cultivators
    D) Religious leaders and intellectuals
    Answer: B) Sikh, Marathas, Rajputs and some other sections of society

GOVERNOR-GENERALS

Hastings (1772-1785)

157. When did Warren Hastings become Governor of Bengal?
    A) 1772
    B) 1773
    C) 1774
    D) 1776
    Answer: A) 1772

158. When did Warren Hastings become Governor-General through the Regulating Act?
    A) 1772
    B) 1773
    C) 1774
    D) 1776
    Answer: B) 1773

159. Which system of administration did Warren Hastings abolish?
    A) Permanent Settlement
    B) Ryotwari Settlement
    C) Subsidiary Alliance
    D) Dual system of administration
    Answer: D) Dual system of administration

160. Who wrote the introduction to the first English translation of the Gita by Charles Wilkins?
    A) William Jones
    B) Lord Cornwallis
    C) Warren Hastings
    D) Sir John Shore
    Answer: C) Warren Hastings

161. In which year did Warren Hastings found the Asiatic Society of Bengal with William Jones?
    A) 1773
    B) 1774
    C) 1782
    D) 1784
    Answer: D) 1784

162. When did the Rohilla War take place during Hastings' tenure?
    A) 1772
    B) 1774
    C) 1776
    D) 1780
    Answer: B) 1774

163. Which Anglo-Maratha War took place during Hastings' tenure?
    A) 1st Anglo-Maratha War (1776-1782)
    B) 2nd Anglo-Maratha War (1803-1805)
    C) 3rd Anglo-Maratha War (1817-1818)
    D) All of the above
    Answer: A) 1st Anglo-Maratha War (1776- 1782)

164. Which Anglo-Mysore War took place during Hastings' tenure?
    A) 1st Anglo-Mysore War (1766-1769)
    B) 2nd Anglo-Mysore War (1780-1784)
    C) 3rd Anglo-Mysore War (1789-1792)
    D) 4th Anglo-Mysore War (1799)
    Answer: B) 2nd Anglo-Mysore War (1780- 1784).

165. Which Act was passed in 1784 during Hastings' tenure?
    A) Regulating Act
    B) Charter Act of 1813
    C) Pitt’s India Act, 1784
    D) Government of India Act 1858
    Answer: C) Pitt’s India Act, 1784

Lord Cornwallis (1786-1793)

166. What administrative reform did Lord Cornwallis introduce regarding revenue and justice?
    A) Combined them
    B) Separated the revenue administration from the administration of justice
    C) Abolished both
    D) Centralized both under one authority
    Answer: B) Separated the revenue administration from the administration of justice.

167. Which new post did Lord Cornwallis create?
    A) Collector
    B) District Judge
    C) Superintendent of Police
    D) Commissioner
    Answer: B) Created post of district judge.

168. What land revenue system did Lord Cornwallis introduce in Bengal?
    A) Ryotwari Settlement
    B) Mahalwari Settlement
    C) Permanent Settlement
    D) Zamindari Settlement (excluding Permanent)
    Answer: C) Introduced Permanent Settlement in Bengal.

169. Who is called the father of civil service in India?
    A) Warren Hastings
    B) Lord Cornwallis
    C) Lord Wellesley
    D) Lord Dalhousie
    Answer: B) Cornwallis

170. Which Anglo-Mysore War did Lord Cornwallis fight?
    A) 1st Anglo-Mysore War
    B) 2nd Anglo-Mysore War
    C) 3rd Anglo-Mysore War
    D) 4th Anglo-Mysore War
    Answer: C) Fought 3rd Anglo-Mysore War.

Sir John Shore (1793-1798)

171. Which Charter Act did Sir John Shore introduce?
    A) 1st Charter Act
    B) Charter Act of 1813
    C) Charter Act of 1833
    D) Charter Act of 1853
    Answer: A) Introduced the 1st Charter Act.

172. Who planned the permanent settlement and later succeeded Cornwallis as Governor General?
    A) Warren Hastings
    B) Sir John Shore
    C) Lord Wellesley
    D) Lord Minto-I
    Answer: B) Sir John Shore

173. What was the Battle of Kharda fought between during Sir John Shore's tenure?
    A) British and Marathas
    B) Nizam and the Marathas
    C) British and Mysore
    D) Sikhs and British
    Answer: B) Battle of Kharda between Niazm and the Marathas (1795).

Lord Wellesley (1798-1805)

174. Which system did Lord Wellesley start to achieve British paramountcy in India?
    A) Permanent Settlement
    B) Ryotwari Settlement
    C) Subsidiary Alliance system
    D) Dual system of government
    Answer: C) Started Subsidiary Alliance system

175. With whom was the first Subsidiary Alliance formed?
    A) Nizam of Hyderabad
    B) Nawab of Awadh
    C) Peshwa
    D) Tipu Sultan
    Answer: A) First alliance with Nizam of Hyderabad.

176. Which Presidency was formed during Lord Wellesley's tenure?
    A) Bengal Presidency
    B) Bombay Presidency
    C) Madras Presidency
    D) Punjab Presidency
    Answer: C) Madras Presidency was formed during his tenure.

177. Which Anglo-Mysore War occurred during Lord Wellesley's tenure, leading to the defeat and death of Tipu Sultan?
    A) 1st Anglo-Mysore War
    B) 2nd Anglo-Mysore War
    C) 3rd Anglo-Mysore War
    D) 4th Anglo-Mysore War (1799)
    Answer: D) 4th Anglo-Mysore War (1799)– defeat and the death of Tipu Sultan.

178. Which Anglo-Maratha War took place during Lord Wellesley's tenure, leading to the defeat of the Scindhia, the Bhonsale and the Holkar?
    A) 1st Anglo-Maratha War
    B) 2nd Anglo-Maratha War (1803-1805)
    C) 3rd Anglo-Maratha War
    D) None of the above
    Answer: B) 2nd Anglo-Maratha War, (1803-1805) defeat of the Scindhia, the Bhonsale and the Holkar.

179. Which treaty was signed in 1802 during Lord Wellesley's tenure?
    A) Treaty of Salbai
    B) Treaty of Srirangapatnam
    C) Treaty of Bassein (1802)
    D) Treaty of Lahore
    Answer: C) Treaty of Bassein (1802).

George Marlow (1805-1807)

180. Which treaty ended the Subsidiary Alliance with Holkar during George Marlow's tenure?
    A) Treaty of Bassein
    B) Treaty of Salbai
    C) Treaty of Rajpurghat
    D) Treaty of Amritsar
    Answer: C) Subsidiary alliance with Holkar by Treaty of Rajpurghat.

181. Which mutiny occurred during George Marlow's tenure?
    A) Barrackpore Mutiny
    B) Vellore Mutiny
    C) Sepoy Mutiny (1857)
    D) Meerut Mutiny
    Answer: B) Vellore Mutiny.

182. Which Anglo-Maratha War ended during George Marlow's tenure?
    A) First Anglo-Maratha War
    B) Second Anglo-Maratha War
    C) Third Anglo-Maratha War
    D) None of the above
    Answer: B) Second Anglo-Maratha War ended.

Lord Minto-I (1807-1813)

183. Which treaty did Lord Minto-I conclude with Ranjit Singh in 1809?
    A) Treaty of Lahore
    B) Treaty of Srirangapatnam
    C) Treaty of Amritsar
    D) Treaty of Purandhar
    Answer: C) Concluded Treaty of Amritsar with Ranjit Singh (1809).

184. Which Charter Act was passed during Lord Minto-I's tenure?
    A) Charter Act of 1793
    B) Charter Act of 1813
    C) Charter Act of 1833
    D) Charter Act of 1853
    Answer: B) Charter Act of 1813 was passed.

Lord Hastings (1813-1823)

185. Which war took place during Lord Hastings' tenure, besides the strife with Pindaris?
    A) 1st Burmese War
    B) Anglo–Nepalese War (1813-1823)
    C) Anglo-Sikh War
    D) Anglo-Mysore War
    Answer: B) Anglo–Nepalese War (1813- 1823), strife with Pindaris (1817-1818)

186. Which Anglo-Maratha War occurred during Lord Hastings' tenure?
    A) 1st Anglo-Maratha War
    B) 2nd Anglo-Maratha War
    C) 3rd Anglo-Maratha War (1817-1818)
    D) None of the above
    Answer: C) 3rd Anglo-Maratha War (1817- 1818).

187. Which land revenue settlement was introduced in Madras by Thomas Munro, the Governor, during Lord Hastings' tenure?
    A) Permanent Settlement
    B) Mahalwari Settlement
    C) Ryotwari settlement
    D) Zamindari System
    Answer: C) Introduced the Ryotwari settlement in Madras by Thomas Munro, the Governor.

Lord Amherst (1823-1828)

188. Which major war took place during Lord Amherst's tenure?
    A) Anglo-Nepalese War
    B) 1st Burmese War (1824-26)
    C) Anglo-Sikh War
    D) Anglo-Afghan War
    Answer: B) 1st Burmese War (1824-26).

189. What territory was acquired during Lord Amherst's tenure?
    A) Punjab
    B) Awadh
    C) Malay Peninsula
    D) Nagpur
    Answer: C) Acquisition of territories in Malay Peninsula.

190. Which city was captured in 1826 during Lord Amherst's tenure?
    A) Delhi
    B) Calcutta
    C) Bharatpur
    D) Madras
    Answer: C) Capture of Bharatpur (1826).


GOVERNOR GENERAL OF INDIA

Lord William Bentick (1828-1834)

1.  Who is regarded as the Father of Modern Western Education in India?
    A) Lord Dalhousie
    B) Lord William Bentick
    C) Sir Charles Metcalfe
    D) Lord Hardinge-I
    Answer: B) Lord William Bentick

2.  Which Governor-General introduced English as the official language in India?
    A) Lord Dalhousie
    B) Lord William Bentick
    C) Lord Canning
    D) Lord Macaulay
    Answer: B) Lord William Bentick

3.  In which year did Lord William Bentick abolish Sati and other cruel rites?
    A) 1828
    B) 1829
    C) 1830
    D) 1831
    Answer: B) 1829

4.  Which practice did Lord William Bentick suppress in 1830?
    A) Female infanticide
    B) Sati
    C) Thuggee
    D) Child marriage
    Answer: C) Thuggee

5.  Who curbed Thuggee under Lord William Bentick?
    A) General Eyre Coote
    B) Robert Clive
    C) Colonel Sleeman
    D) Major Munro
    Answer: C) Colonel Sleeman

6.  Which of the following states was annexed by Lord William Bentick on the plea of misgovernment?
    A) Satara
    B) Nagpur
    C) Awadh
    D) Mysore
    Answer: D) Mysore

    A) 1829
    B) 1830
    C) 1831
    D) 1834
    Answer: C) 1831

    A) Satara and Jhansi
    B) Coorg and Central Cachar
    C) Berar and Awadh
    D) Punjab and Sindh
    Answer: B) Coorg (1834), Central Cachar (1834)

    A) Nizam of Hyderabad
    B) Scindia
    C) Ranjit Singh
    D) Tipu Sultan
    Answer: C) Ranjit Singh

Lord Dalhousie (1848-1856)

    Answer: B) Widow Remarriage Act (1856).

    A) Macaulay's Minute
    B) Wood’s Educational Despatch of 1854
    C) Hunter Commission

23. What policy did Lord Dalhousie introduce for annexing Indian states?
    A) Subsidiary Alliance
    B) Permanent Settlement
    C) Doctrine of Lapse

    C) First Afghan War

VICEROYS OF INDIA

Lord Canning (1858-1862)

41. Which Act did Lord Canning pass that ended the rule of the East India Company?
    A) Regulating Act of 1773
    B) Pitt's India Act of 1784
    C) Act of 1858
    D) Indian Councils Act of 1861
    Answer: C) Passed the Act of 1858

42. Which policy did Lord Canning withdraw?
    A) Subsidiary Alliance
    B) Permanent Settlement
    C) Doctrine of Lapse
    D) Ryotwari System
    Answer: C) Withdrew Doctrine of Lapse.

43. Which Act of 1861 proved to be a landmark in the constitutional history of India?
    A) Indian High Court Act
    B) Indian Penal Code
    C) Indian Councils Act of 1861
    D) Government of India Act of 1858
    Answer: C) The Indian Councils Act of 1861

44. When was the Indian Penal Code of Criminal Procedure passed?
    A) 1858
    B) 1859
    C) 1861
    D) 1862
    Answer: B) The Indian Penal Code of Criminal Procedure (1859)

45. When was the Indian High Court Act enacted?
    A) 1859
    B) 1860
    C) 1861
    D) 1862
    Answer: C) The Indian High Court Act (1861)

46. When was Income Tax introduced for the first time in India?
    A) 1857
    B) 1858
    C) 1859
    D) 1860
    Answer: B) Income Tax was introduced for the first time in 1858.

47. Which mutiny by European troops occurred in 1859?
    A) Sepoy Mutiny
    B) White Mutiny
    C) Vellore Mutiny
    D) Barrackpore Mutiny
    Answer: B) ‘White Mutiny’ by European troops in 1859.

Lord Elgin-I (1862-1863)

48. Which movement did Lord Elgin-I suppress?
    A) Santhal Uprising
    B) Kuka Rebellion
    C) Wahabi Movement
    D) Quit India Movement
    Answer: C) Suppressed Wahabi Movement.

Sir John Lawrence (1864-1868)

49. In which year were High Courts established at Calcutta, Bombay, and Madras?
    A) 1857
    B) 1861
    C) 1865
    D) 1868
    Answer: C) 1865

50. Which war took place in 1865 during Sir John Lawrence's tenure?
    A) First Afghan War
    B) Second Burmese War
    C) Bhutan War
    D) Third Burmese War
    Answer: C) Bhutan War (1865).

51. What type of railways did Sir John Lawrence advocate?
    A) Private-managed railways
    B) State-managed railways
    C) Joint-venture railways
    D) Foreign-funded railways
    Answer: B) Advocated State-managed railways.

52. What new department did Sir John Lawrence create?
    A) Department of Agriculture and Commerce
    B) Department of Public Works
    C) Indian Forests Department
    D) Department of Education
    Answer: C) Created the Indian Forests Department and reorganized the native judicial service.

Lord Mayo (1869-1872)

53. Which colleges did Lord Mayo establish for Indian princes?
    A) Hindu College and Elphinstone College
    B) Rajkot College at Kathiawar and Mayo College at Ajmer
    C) Fort William College and St. Xavier's College
    D) Presidency College and Scottish Church College
    Answer: B) He established the Rajkot College at Kathiawar and Mayo College at Ajmer for the Indian princes.

54. What survey did Lord Mayo organize?
    A) Archaeological Survey of India
    B) Geological Survey of India
    C) Statistical Survey of India
    D) Linguistic Survey of India
    Answer: C) He organised the Statistical Survey of India.

55. Which department did Lord Mayo establish?
    A) Department of Revenue
    B) Department of Home Affairs
    C) Department of Agriculture and Commerce
    D) Department of Education
    Answer: C) He established the Department of Agriculture and Commerce.

56. Who was the only Viceroy to be murdered in office in the Andamans?
    A) Lord Canning
    B) Lord Mayo
    C) Lord Lytton
    D) Lord Ripon
    Answer: B) He was the only Viceroy to be murdered in office by a convict in the Andamans in 1872.

Lord Northbrook (1872-1876)

    A) Santhal uprising
    B) Kuka rebellion
    C) Moplah rebellion
    D) Wahabi Movement
    Answer: B) Kuka rebellion in Punjab (1872)

    A) Earthquake
    B) Flood
    C) Famine
    D) Cyclone
    Answer: C) Famine in Bihar (1874)

    A) 1872
    B) 1874
    C) 1875
    D) 1876
    Answer: C) Visit of Prince of Wales in 1875.

60. Over what issue did Lord Northbrook resign?
    A) Vernacular Press Act
    B) Illbert Bill
    C) Afghanistan Question
    D) Famine in Bihar
    Answer: C) Resigned over Afghanistan Question.

Lord Lytton (1876-1880)

61. Who was considered the most unpopular Viceroy?
    A) Lord Mayo
    B) Lord Northbrook
    C) Lord Lytton
    D) Lord Ripon
    Answer: C) Lord Lytton

62. What economic policy did Lord Lytton pursue that accelerated the drain of India?
    A) Protectionism
    B) Free trade and abolished duties on 29 British manufactured goods
    C) Imposed high tariffs
    D) Encouraged local industries
    Answer: B) Pursued free trade and abolished duties on 29 British manufactured goods which accelerated drain of India.

63. Where did Lord Lytton arrange the Grand Darbar in 1877 when the country was suffering from a severe famine?
    A) Calcutta
    B) Bombay
    C) Delhi
    D) Madras
    Answer: C) Arranged the Grand Darbar in Delhi (in 1877)

64. Which Act was passed in 1876, declaring Queen Victoria as the Kaisera-Hind?
    A) Vernacular Press Act
    B) Arms Act
    C) Royal Title Act (1876)
    D) Indian Councils Act
    Answer: C) Passed the Royal Title Act (1876)

65. Which Act of 1878 made it mandatory for Indians to acquire a license for arms?
    A) Royal Title Act
    B) Vernacular Press Act
    C) Arms Act (1878)
    D) Factory Act
    Answer: C) Arms Act (1878)

66. Which infamous Act was passed by Lord Lytton in 1878?
    A) Arms Act
    B) Royal Title Act
    C) Vernacular Press Act (1878)
    D) Indian Councils Act
    Answer: C) Passed the infamous Vernacular Press Act (1878).

67. Which Afghan War took place during Lord Lytton's tenure?
    A) First Afghan War
    B) Second Afghan War (1878-80)
    C) Third Afghan War
    D) None of the above
    Answer: B) The second Afghan War (1878-80)

Lord Rippon (1880-1884)

    A) Arms Act
    B) Royal Title Act
    C) Vernacular Press Act (1882)
    D) Factory Act
    Answer: C) Repeal of the Vernacular Press Act, 1882.

    A) Mines Act
    B) Factory Act, 1881
    C) Trade Unions Act
    D) Labor Welfare Act
    Answer: B) The first Factory Act, 1881

    B) Resolution on Provincial Government
    C) Resolution on Local Self Government in 1882
    D) Resolution on Judicial Reforms
    Answer: C) Resolution on Local Self Government in 1882.

    A) Saddler Commission
    B) Hunter Commission (for education reform)
    C) Frazer Commission
    D) Lyall Commission
    Answer: B) Appointed Hunter commission (for education reform) in 1882.

72. Which controversy erupted during Lord Rippon's time?
    A) Arms Act controversy
    B) Vernacular Press Act controversy
    C) Illbert Bill controversy
    D) Partition of Bengal controversy
    Answer: C) The Illbert Bill controversy erupted during his time.

    D) Indian and British

    A) Indian Council Act of 1861
    B) Indian Council Act of 1892
    C) Morley-Minto Reforms
    D) Montagu-Chelmsford Reforms
    Answer: B) Indian Council Act of 1892 (Introduced elections which was indirect).

    A) Hunter Commission
    B) Saddler Commission
    C) Durand Commission
    D) Lyall Commission

    B) 1896
    C) 1897

Lord Curzon (1899-1905)

    B) Universities Commission
    C) Police Commission
    D) Civil Service Commission

    A) Indian Councils Act
    B) Indian Universities Act of 1904
    C) Indian Education Act
    D) Indian Civil Service Act
    Answer: B) accordingly the Indian Universities Act of 1904 was passed.

    B) Department of Commerce and Industry

    B) Calcutta Municipal Act

90. Which Act did Lord Curzon pass in 1899, putting India on a gold standard?
    A) Indian Currency Act
    B) Indian Coinage and Paper Currency Act (in 1899)
    C) Indian Gold Standard Act
    D) Indian Reserve Bank Act
    Answer: B) Passed the Indian Coinage and Paper Currency Act (in 1899) and put India on a gold standard.

    B) 1904
    C) 1905
    D) 1906

Lord Chelmsford (1916-1921)

102. When was the Lucknow Pact signed between Congress and Muslim League?
    A) 1915
    B) 1916
    C) 1917
    D) 1918
    Answer: B) Lucknow Pact in 1916 between Congress and Muslim League.

    A) Kheda Satyagraha
    B) Ahmedabad Mill Strike
    C) Champaran Satyagraha
    D) Dandi March
    Answer: C) Arrival of Gandhi, and Champaran Satyagraha in 1917.

104. In which year were the Home Rule Leagues formed by Annie Besant and Tilak?
    A) 1915
    B) 1916
    C) 1917
    D) 1918
    Answer: A) Formation of Home Rule Leagues by Annie Besant and Tilak (1916) (Note: The text states 1956 which is a typo, corrected to 1916 based on historical fact.)

105. When was Montague’s August Declaration made?
    A) 1916
    B) 1917
    C) 1918
    D) 1919
    Answer: B) Montague’s August Declaration (1917).

106. Which commission on Education was appointed in 1917?
    A) Hunter Commission
    B) Saddler Commission of Education in 1917
    C) Lyall Commission
    D) Frazer Commission
    Answer: B) Saddler Commission of Education in 1917.

107. Which Satyagrahas occurred in 1918?
    A) Champaran and Kheda
    B) Kheda Satyagraha and Satyagraha at Ahmedabad
    C) Dandi March and Quit India
    D) Non-Cooperation and Khilafat
    Answer: B) Kheda Satyagraha and Satyagraha at Ahmedabad in 1918.

108. Which Government of India Act was passed in 1919?
    A) Government of India Act (1909)
    B) Government of India Act (1919)
    C) Government of India Act (1935)
    D) Government of India Act (1858)
    Answer: B) Government of India Act (1919).

109. Which repressive Act was passed in 1919?
    A) Arms Act
    B) Vernacular Press Act
    C) Rowlatt Act (1919)
    D) Indian Councils Act
    Answer: C) Repressive Rowlatt Act (1919).

110. When did the Massacre at Jalianwala Bagh occur?
    A) 1917
    B) 1918
    C) 1919
    D) 1920
    Answer: C) Massacre at Jalianwala Bagh (1919).

111. Which two major national movements began during Lord Chelmsford's tenure?
    A) Swadeshi Movement and Home Rule Movement
    B) Khilafat Movement and Non-Cooperation Movement
    C) Civil Disobedience Movement and Quit India Movement
    D) Quit India Movement and Indian National Army movement
    Answer: B) Khilafat Movement. Non-Cooperation Movement.

112. Who was the first Indian to become a governor, appointed by Lord Chelmsford?
    A) Mahatma Gandhi
    B) Jawaharlal Nehru
    C) S.P. Sinha as governor of Bihar
    D) Sardar Vallabhbhai Patel
    Answer: C) Appointment of S.P. Sinha as governor of Bihar (the first Indian to become a governor).

Lord Reading (1921-1926)

113. Which Acts were repealed by Lord Reading?
    A) Arms Act and Royal Title Act
    B) Press Act (1910) and Rowlatt Act of 1919
    C) Factory Act and Indian Councils Act
    D) Vernacular Press Act and Income Tax Act
    Answer: B) Repeal of Press Act (1910) and Rowlatt Act of 1919.

114. Which incident occurred in 1922 during Lord Reading's tenure?
    A) Chauri-Chaura incident
    B) Jallianwala Bagh Massacre
    C) Kakori robbery
    D) Dandi March
    Answer: A) Chauri-Chaura incident.

115. Which violent rebellion occurred in Kerala in 1921?
    A) Santhal rebellion
    B) Kuka rebellion
    C) Moplah rebellion
    D) Wahabi movement
    Answer: C) Voilent Moplah rebellion in Kerala (1921)

116. In which year was CPI (Communist Party of India) founded?
    A) 1920
    B) 1921
    C) 1922
    D) 1923
    Answer: B) CPI founded in 1921.

    B) Only in Delhi

    Answer: C) Decision to hold simultaneous examinations for the ICS both in Delhi and London, with effect from 1923.

LAND REVENUE SYSTEM DURING BRITISH RULE

Zamindari System (Permanent Settlement)

161. Which land revenue system was introduced by Lord Cornwallis in 1793?
    A) Ryotwari System
    B) Mahalwari System
    C) Zamindari System (Permanent Settlement)
    D) Inam System
    Answer: C) This land revenue system was introduced by Lord Cornwallis in 1793

162. In which provinces was the Zamindari System introduced?
    A) Madras and Bombay
    B) Punjab and Central Province
    C) Bengal, Bihar, Orissa and Varanasi
    D) Assam and Coorgh
    Answer: C) It was introduced in provinces of Bengal, Bihar, Orissa and Varanasi.

163. Who were made the owners of the lands and given rights to collect rent from peasants under the Zamindari System?
    A) Peasants
    B) British Government
    C) Zamindars
    D) Village Committee
    Answer: C) Zamindars were made the owner of the lands and given them rights to collect the rent from the peasants.

164. What share of the realized amount belonged to the Zamindar under the Zamindari System?
    A) 1/10
    B) 1/11
    C) 10/11
    D) 50%
    Answer: B) 1/11 of the share belong to Zamindar

165. What share of the realized amount belonged to the East India Company under the Zamindari System?
    A) 1/11
    B) 10/11
    C) 1/2
    D) All of it
    Answer: B) 10/11 of the share belongs to East India Company.

Ryotwari System

166. Which land revenue system was introduced by Thomas Munro in 1820?
    A) Zamindari System
    B) Ryotwari System
    C) Mahalwari System
    D) Permanent Settlement
    Answer: B) This land revenue system was introduced by Thomas Munro in 1820.

167. Which of the following areas was not a major area of introduction for the Ryotwari System?
    A) Madras
    B) Bombay
    C) Parts of Assam
    D) Bengal
    Answer: D) Bengal

168. In the Ryotwari System, who were the ownership rights handed over to?
    A) Zamindars
    B) British Government
    C) Peasants
    D) Village Committee
    Answer: C) In Ryotwari System the ownership rights were handed over to the peasants.

169. Who collected taxes directly from the peasants under the Ryotwari System?
    A) Zamindars
    B) Village Committee
    C) British Government
    D) Local Kings
    Answer: C) British Government collected taxes directly from the peasants.

170. What were the revenue rates of the Ryotwari System for dry lands?
    A) 60%
    B) 50%
    C) 33%
    D) 25%
    Answer: B) 50% where the lands were dry

171. What were the revenue rates of the Ryotwari System for irrigated lands?
    A) 50%
    B) 60%
    C) 75%
    D) 80%
    Answer: B) 60% in irrigated land.

Mahalwari System

172. In which year was the Mahalwari System introduced?
    A) 1820
    B) 1829
    C) 1833
    D) 1834
    Answer: C) This land revenue system was introduced in 1833

173. During whose period was the Mahalwari System introduced?
    A) Lord Cornwallis
    B) Thomas Munro
    C) William Bentick
    D) Lord Dalhousie
    Answer: C) during the period of William Bentick.

174. Which of the following was a major area of introduction for the Mahalwari System?
    A) Madras
    B) Bombay
    C) Central Province
    D) Orissa
    Answer: C) It was introduced in central Province, North-West Frontier, Agra, Punjab, Gangetic Valley, etc of British India.

175. In the Mahalwari System, how was the land divided?
    A) Into villages
    B) Into Mahals
    C) Into individual plots
    D) Into districts
    Answer: B) the land was divided into Mahals.

176. Who was responsible for the collection of taxes under the Mahalwari System?
    A) Individual peasants
    B) Zamindars
    C) The Villages committee
    D) British officials
    Answer: C) The Villages committee was held responsible for collection of the taxes.

FOUNDATION OF INDIAN NATIONAL CONGRESS

211. Who was the architect of the Indian National Congress?
    A) Mahatma Gandhi
    B) Jawaharlal Nehru
    C) Allan Octavian Hume
    D) Dadabhai Naoroji
    Answer: C) Allan Octavian Hume

212. In which month and year was the Indian National Congress founded?
    A) January 1885
    B) December 1885
    C) May 1886
    D) October 1887
    Answer: B) December 1885

213. How many political workers joined A.O. Hume in founding the INC?
    A) Fifty-one
    B) Sixty-one
    C) Seventy-one
    D) Eighty-one
    Answer: C) seventy one

214. Where was the first meeting of the INC organized?
    A) Pune
    B) Calcutta
    C) Gokaldas Tejpal Sanskrit College on December 28th, 1885 in Bombay
    D) Madras
    Answer: C) Gokaldas Tejpal Sanskrit College on December 28th, 1885 in Bombay.

215. What was Hume’s main purpose in encouraging the foundation of the Congress, according to the text?
    A) To promote Indian self-rule
    B) To provide a ‘safety valve’ to the growing discontent among the educated Indians
    C) To establish a political party
    D) To gather intelligence for the British government
    Answer: B) to provide a ‘safety valve to the growing discontent among the educated Indians.


Swadeshi Movement

1.  When did Lord Curzon announce the scheme for the partition of Bengal?
    A) 1904
    B) 1905
    C) 1906
    D) 1903
    Answer: A) 1904

2.  What was the real motive behind the partition of Bengal, as described in the text?
    A) Administrative efficiency
    B) Curbing national feeling and dividing Hindus and Muslims
    C) Promoting economic development
    D) Establishing new provincial capitals
    Answer: B) Curbing national feeling and dividing Hindus and Muslims

3.  When was the partition of Bengal declared in Shimla?
    A) July 20, 1905
    B) July 7, 1905
    C) October 16, 1905
    D) December 7, 1905
    Answer: B) July 7, 1905

4.  When did the partition of Bengal formally take place?
    A) July 7, 1905
    B) July 20, 1905
    C) October 16, 1905
    D) January 1, 1906
    Answer: B) July 20, 1905

5.  Which movement culminated from the anti-partition movement?
    A) Non-Cooperation Movement
    B) Civil Disobedience Movement
    C) Swadeshi Movement
    D) Quit India Movement
    Answer: C) Swadeshi Movement

6.  In which year did Dadabhai Naoroji speak of ‘Swaraj’ at the Calcutta session?
    A) 1905
    B) 1906
    C) 1907
    D) 1904
    Answer: B) 1906

7.  What were some of the key programs involved in the Swadeshi Movement?
    A) Boycott of government service, promotion of swadeshi goods, promotion of national education
    B) Only promoting national education
    C) Only boycotting foreign goods
    D) Demanding separate electorates
    Answer: A) Boycott of government service, promotion of swadeshi goods, promotion of national education

8.  In which session did the Indian National Congress first take the Swadeshi call?
    A) Calcutta Session of 1906
    B) Surat Session of 1907
    C) Banaras Session of 1905
    D) Lucknow Session of 1916
    Answer: C) Banaras Session of 1905

9.  Who presided over the Banaras Session of the Indian National Congress in 1905?
    A) Dadabhai Naoroji
    B) G.K. Gokhale
    C) Lala Lajpat Rai
    D) Aurobindo Ghosh
    Answer: B) G.K. Gokhale

10. Who were the prominent figures (Lal, Bal, Pal) who played an important role in the Swadeshi Movement?
    A) Lala Lajpat Rai, Bal Gangadhar Tilak, Bipin Chandra Pal
    B) Lal Bahadur Shastri, Bal Gangadhar Tilak, Palwankar Vithal
    C) Lala Hardayal, Balwant Singh, Palanisamy
    D) Lakshmi Narayan, Balram Singh, Pankaj Das
    Answer: A) Lala Lajpat Rai, Bal Gangadhar Tilak, Bipin Chandra Pal

11. When was the day of partition formally enforced and observed as the day of mourning throughout Bengal?
    A) July 7, 1905
    B) July 20, 1905
    C) October 16, 1905
    D) August 1, 1905
    Answer: C) October 16, 1905

Revolutionary Leaders with Swadeshi Movement

12. Who was associated with the Swadeshi Movement in Mumbai & Pune?
    A) Ajit Singh
    B) B.G. Tilak
    C) Lala Lajpat Rai
    D) Sayyid Haider Raza
    Answer: B) B.G. Tilak

13. Which revolutionary leader was associated with the Swadeshi Movement in Punjab?
    A) B.G. Tilak
    B) Ajit Singh
    C) Sayyid Haider Raza
    D) Chidambaram Pillai
    Answer: B) Ajit Singh

14. Who was associated with the Swadeshi Movement in Uttar Pradesh?
    A) Ajit Singh
    B) Lala Lajpat Rai
    C) Sayyid Haider Raza
    D) Chidambaram Pillai
    Answer: B) Lala Lajpat Rai

15. Who was associated with the Swadeshi Movement in Delhi?
    A) Lala Lajpat Rai
    B) Ajit Singh
    C) Sayyid Haider Raza
    D) Chidambaram Pillai
    Answer: C) Sayyid Haider Raza

16. Who was associated with the Swadeshi Movement in Madras?
    A) Sayyid Haider Raza
    B) Ajit Singh
    C) Chidambaram Pillai
    D) B.G. Tilak
    Answer: C) Chidambaram Pillai

17. Who set up a ‘Swadesh Bandhava Committee’ to spread the message of the Swadeshi Movement?
    A) R.N. Tagore
    B) Aurobindo Ghosh
    C) Ashwani Kumar Datta
    D) B.G. Tilak
    Answer: C) Ashwani Kumar Datta

18. Who set up ‘Bengal National College’ for National education?
    A) Aurobindo Ghosh
    B) Ashwani Kumar Datta
    C) R.N. Tagore
    D) Bipin Chandra Pal
    Answer: C) R.N. Tagore

19. Who was the Principal of Bengal National College?
    A) R.N. Tagore
    B) Ashwani Kumar Datta
    C) Aurobindo Ghosh
    D) K. K. Mitra
    Answer: C) Aurobindo Ghosh

20. Which song, written by R.N. Tagore, later became the national song of Bangladesh?
    A) Vande Mataram
    B) Jana Gana Mana
    C) Amar Sonar Bangla
    D) Saare Jahan Se Accha
    Answer: C) Amar Sonar Bangla

Acts passed by Government to suppress the Movement

    A) Criminal Law Amendment Act
    B) Indian Newspaper Act
    C) Sedition Meeting Act
    D) Indian Press Act
    Answer: C) Sedition Meeting Act, 1907

22. Which Act was passed in 1908 to suppress the Swadeshi Movement?
    A) Sedition Meeting Act
    B) Criminal Law Amendment Act
    C) Indian Press Act
    D) Indian Newspaper Act (Note: Both B and D were passed in 1908 based on the text. However, Criminal Law Amendment Act is listed first).
    Answer: B) Criminal Law Amendment Act, 1908 (or D) Indian Newspaper Act, 1908

23. Which Act passed in 1908 dealt with explosives?
    A) Sedition Meeting Act
    B) Indian Newspaper Act
    C) Explosive Substances Act
    D) Criminal Law Amendment Act
    Answer: C) Explosive Substances Act, 1908

24. Which Act was passed in 1910 to suppress the Swadeshi Movement?
    A) Sedition Meeting Act
    B) Criminal Law Amendment Act
    C) Indian Press Act
    D) Explosive Substances Act
    Answer: C) Indian Press Act, 1910

The Surat Split (1907)

34. In which year did the Surat Split of the Indian National Congress occur?
    A) 1906
    B) 1907
    C) 1908
    D) 1905
    Answer: B) 1907

35. Under whose presidentship did the Surat session take place?
    A) Dadabhai Naoroji
    B) G.K. Gokhale
    C) Ras Bihari Ghosh
    D) Lala Lajpat Rai
    Answer: C) Ras Bihari Ghosh

36. Into which two groups did the Indian National Congress split during the Surat session?
    A) Revolutionaries and Moderates
    B) Extremists and Moderates
    C) Swarajists and No-Changers
    D) Liberals and Conservatives
    Answer: B) Extremists and Moderates

37. Who led the Extremists during the Surat split?
    A) G.K. Gokhale
    B) Lal, Bal, and Pal
    C) Dadabhai Naoroji
    D) Ras Bihari Ghosh
    Answer: B) Lal, Bal, and Pal

38. Who led the Moderates during the Surat split?
    A) Lal, Bal, and Pal
    B) G.K. Gokhale
    C) Ras Bihari Ghosh
    D) Lala Lajpat Rai
    Answer: B) G.K. Gokhale

39. Who did the Extremists want to be chosen as president of the INC Surat session?
    A) Ras Bihari Ghosh
    B) G.K. Gokhale
    C) Lala Lajpat Rai
    D) Bal Gangadhar Tilak
    Answer: C) Lala Lajpat Rai

Foundation of Muslim League (1906)

40. When was the Muslim League founded?
    A) 1905
    B) 1906
    C) 1907
    D) 1909
    Answer: B) 1906

41. Who were the key leaders involved in the establishment of the Muslim League in 1906?
    A) Aga Khan, Nawab Salimullah of Dhaka, and Nawab Mohsin-ul-mulk
    B) Muhammad Ali Jinnah, Liaquat Ali Khan, and Sir Syed Ahmed Khan
    C) Maulana Abul Kalam Azad, Hakim Ajmal Khan, and M.A. Ansari
    D) Vakar-ul-mulk, Sayyid Haider Raza, and Ajit Singh
    Answer: A) Aga Khan, Nawab Salimullah of Dhaka, and Nawab Mohsin-ul-mulk

42. Who was the first president of the Muslim League?
    A) Aga Khan
    B) Nawab Salimullah
    C) Nawab Mohsin-ul-mulk
    D) Vakar-ul-mulk
    Answer: D) Vakar-ul-mulk

43. What was one of the primary objectives of the Muslim League?
    A) To promote rebellion against the British Government
    B) To promote loyalty towards the British Government among Indian Muslims
    C) To demand immediate independence
    D) To merge with the Indian National Congress
    Answer: B) To promote loyalty towards the British Government among Indian Muslims

44. The formation of the Muslim League was considered a success of which British policy?
    A) Policy of reconciliation
    B) Policy of 'Divide & Rule'
    C) Policy of annexation
    D) Policy of economic development
    Answer: B) Policy of 'Divide & Rule'

Morley-Minto Reforms (1909)

45. Who became the Viceroy of India after Lord Curzon?
    A) Lord Minto
    B) Lord Morley
    C) Lord Chelmsford
    D) Lord Reading
    Answer: A) Lord Minto

46. What significant provision was envisaged for Muslims in the Morley-Minto Reforms?
    A) Joint electorates
    B) Reserved seats
    C) Separate electorate
    D) Universal suffrage
    Answer: C) Separate electorate

47. Who is referred to as the 'Father of Communal Electorate'?
    A) Lord Curzon
    B) Lord Minto
    C) Lord Morley
    D) Satyendra Prasad Sinha
    Answer: B) Lord Minto

48. What was a key aim of the Morley-Minto Reforms, besides introducing separate electorates?
    A) Uniting nationalist ranks
    B) Rallying moderates and Muslims to the government's side
    C) Promoting self-rule
    D) Expanding Indian representation without communal division
    Answer: B) Rallying moderates and Muslims to the government's side

49. Who was the first Indian to be appointed to the Viceroy's Executive Council?
    A) Muhammad Ali Jinnah
    B) G.K. Gokhale
    C) Satyendra Prasad Sinha
    D) Bal Gangadhar Tilak
    Answer: C) Satyendra Prasad Sinha

    A) Finance Member
    B) Law Member
    C) Home Member
    D) Defence Member
    Answer: B) Law member

The second stage of National Movement (Revolutionary Movement)

55. Which were the main centers of the revolutionary movement in India?
    A) Maharashtra, Bengal, Punjab
    B) Uttar Pradesh, Bihar, Delhi
    C) Madras, Gujarat, Rajasthan
    D) Assam, Orissa, Kerala
    Answer: A) Maharashtra, Bengal, Punjab

Maharashtra

56. When did the revolutionary movement start from Maharashtra?
    A) 1896
    B) 1897
    C) 1899
    D) 1909
    Answer: B) 1897

    A) Diwali and Holi
    B) Shivaji and Ganapati Festivals
    C) Dussehra and Navratri
    D) Pongal and Onam
    Answer: B) Shivaji and Ganapati Festivals

    A) Yugantar & Sanjivini
    B) Kesari & Maratha
    C) New India & Bande Matram
    D) Bharat Mata & Sandhya
    Answer: B) Kesari & Maratha

    A) Anushilan Committee
    B) Swadesh Bandhava Committee
    C) Acharya Bandhav Committee
    D) Mitra Mela
    Answer: C) Acharya Bandhav Committee

    A) V.D. Savarkar
    B) Madan Lal Dhingra
    C) Chapekar Brothers
    D) Anant Lakshman Kanhare
    Answer: C) Chapekar Brothers

    A) Mitra Mela
    B) Anushilan Committee
    C) Hindu Dharma Sangh
    D) Abhinav Bharat

    B) June 22, 1897
    C) June 22, 1899
    D) June 22, 1909
    Answer: B) June 22, 1897

    A) Chapekar Brothers
    B) V.D. Savarkar and his brother Ganesh
    C) Anant Lakshman Kanhare
    D) Madan Lal Dhingra
    Answer: B) V.D. Savarkar and his brother Ganesh

    B) Rand
    C) Amerst
    D) District Magistrate Jackson of Nasik

65. Who established Home Rule Society and India House in London in 1905 and brought out the journal 'The Sociologist'?

    B) Madan Lal Dhingra
    C) Shyamji Krishna Varma (Implied from the context, as the information is placed right after Savarkar's mention and before Dhingra)
    D) Madam Bhikaji Cama
    Answer: C) Shyamji Krishna Varma

    A) V.D. Savarkar
    B) Anant Lakshman Kanhare
    C) Madan Lal Dhingra
    D) Rash Bihari Bose
    Answer: C) Madan Lal Dhingra

67. Who operated from Paris and Geneva and brought out the journal 'Bande Matram'?
    A) Anne Besant
    B) Madam Bhikaji Cama

Bengal

    C) Praffula Chaki, Khudiram Bose, and Ullas Dutta

    B) Bhupendra Nath Dutta

    B) Praffula Chaki and Khudiram Bose
    C) Ullas Dutta and Hemchandra Das
    D) Rash Bihari Bose and Amirchandra

    B) He committed suicide.
    C) He was released due to lack of evidence.
    D) He fled to Japan.
