Ghadar Party (1913)

87. When was the Ghadar Party founded?
    A) 1907
    B) 1910
    C) 1913
    D) 1916
    Answer: C) 1913

88. What was the initial objective of the Ghadar Party?
    A) To promote Indian independence through armed struggle
    B) To protect and defend the rights of Indian settlers in the United States and Canada
    C) To establish a new political party in India
    D) To cooperate with the British Government
    Answer: B) To protect and defend the rights of Indian settlers in the United States and Canada

89. Who established the Hindustan Association in San Francisco in 1907 and edited 'Sarkular-i-Azadi' in Urdu?
    A) Lala Lajpat Rai
    B) Ajit Singh
    C) Ramnath Puri
    D) Lala Hardayal
    Answer: C) Ramnath Puri

90. Which session of the Indian National Congress brought the Moderates and Extremists together and led to an entente between Congress and the Muslim League?
    A) Calcutta Session of 1906
    B) Surat Session of 1907
    C) Lucknow Session of 1916
    D) Banaras Session of 1905
    Answer: C) Lucknow Session of 1916

91. When was the partition of Bengal annulled?
    A) October 16, 1905
    B) July 20, 1905
    C) October 2, 1911
    D) December 12, 1911
    Answer: C) October 2, 1911

92. Which events related to Turkey disappointed the Muslim community in India between 1911 and 1913?
    A) Turkish support for the British
    B) Tripoli and Balkan wars
    C) Economic prosperity in Turkey
    D) Alliance with Germany
    Answer: B) Tripoli and Balkan wars

93. Who presided over the Lucknow Session of Congress in 1916?
    A) Dadabhai Naoroji
    B) G.K. Gokhale
    C) Ambika Charan Majumdar
    D) Muhammad Ali Jinnah
    Answer: C) Ambika Charan Majumdar

94. Who were the architects of the Congress-League cooperation at the Lucknow Session?
    A) G.K. Gokhale and Dadabhai Naoroji
    B) Jinnah and Tilak
    C) Annie Besant and Bipin Chandra Pal
    D) Lala Lajpat Rai and Ajit Singh
    Answer: B) Jinnah & Tilak

95. Under whose leadership was the cooperation of the Muslim League welcomed by INC at the Karachi Session in 1913?
    A) Muhammad Ali Jinnah
    B) Nawab Sayyid Mohammad Bahadur
    C) Aga Khan
    D) Vakar-ul-mulk
    Answer: B) Nawab Sayyid Mohammad Bahadur

96. When did Tilak set up the Home Rule League in Belgaon?
    A) April 28, 1915
    B) April 28, 1916
    C) September 1916
    D) April 28, 1917
    Answer: B) April 28, 1916

97. Which regions were covered by Tilak’s Home Rule League?
    A) All of India
    B) Madras and rest of the country
    C) Maharashtra, Karnataka, Central provinces, and Berar
    D) Punjab, Bengal, and Uttar Pradesh
    Answer: C) Maharashtra, Karnataka, Central provinces, and Berar

98. Who set up a similar Home Rule organisation in Madras in September 1916?
    A) B.G. Tilak
    B) Annie Besant
    C) Mahatma Gandhi
    D) Muhammad Ali Jinnah
    Answer: B) Annie Besant

99. How many branches did Annie Besant’s Home Rule League have?
    A) Six branches
    B) 200 branches
    C) 50 branches
    D) 100 branches
    Answer: B) 200 branches

100. What did British authorities prohibit to suppress the Home Rule Movement?
    A) Public meetings
    B) Publication of journals
    C) School and college boys from participating
    D) Forming new organizations
    Answer: C) School and college boys from participating in the Home Rule Movement

Montague Declaration (1917)

101. What was the Montague Declaration also known as?
    A) The August Offer
    B) The August Declaration
    C) The December Declaration
    D) The September Declaration
    Answer: B) The August Declaration

102. What did Moderates call the Montague Declaration?
    A) A meaningless declaration
    B) The Magna Carta of India
    C) Morning Without the Sun
    D) A step towards full independence
    Answer: B) The Magna Carta of India

103. How did Tilak describe the Montague Declaration?
    A) The Magna Carta of India
    B) Morning Without the Sun
    C) A beacon of hope
    D) A significant reform
    Answer: B) Morning Without the Sun

The Montague-Chelmsford Reforms (1919) (The Indian Council Act 1919)

104. When did the Montague-Chelmsford Reforms come into force?
    A) 1919
    B) 1920
    C) 1921
    D) 1922
    Answer: C) 1921

105. What kind of legislature was established in place of the Imperial Legislative Council by the Montague-Chelmsford Reforms?
    A) Unicameral
    B) Bicameral (Legislative Assembly and Council of State)
    C) Tricameral
    D) Provisional
    Answer: B) Bicameral

106. What system was introduced in the provinces by the Montague-Chelmsford Reforms?
    A) Federalism
    B) Unitary government
    C) Dyarchy
    D) Autonomy
    Answer: C) Dyarchy

107. Into which two categories were the provincial subjects divided under Dyarchy?
    A) Central and Provincial
    B) Reserved and Transferred
    C) Executive and Legislative
    D) Financial and Administrative
    Answer: B) Reserved and Transferred

108. Which departments were considered 'Reserved' subjects under the Montague-Chelmsford Reforms?
    A) Education and Health
    B) Finance, Police, General Administration
    C) Agriculture and Local Self-Government
    D) Public Works and Industries
    Answer: B) Finance, Police, General Administration

109. Which principle did the Montague-Chelmsford Reforms extend by providing separate electorates for Sikhs, Indian Christians, Anglo-Indians, and Europeans?
    A) Universal suffrage
    B) Joint electorate
    C) Communal representation
    D) Adult franchise
    Answer: C) Communal representation

The Third Phase of National Movement (Gandhi Era - 1919-1947)

110. When did Gandhiji return from South Africa to India?
    A) January 9, 1910
    B) January 9, 1915
    C) January 9, 1919
    D) January 9, 1920
    Answer: B) January 9, 1915

111. Who did Gandhiji accept as his political Guru?
    A) Bal Gangadhar Tilak
    B) G.K. Gokhale
    C) Jawaharlal Nehru
    D) Sardar Patel
    Answer: B) G.K. Gokhale

112. In which district of Bihar province did Gandhiji first try the efficacy of his weapon (Satyagraha) in India?
    A) Patna
    B) Gaya
    C) Champaran
    D) Muzaffarpur
    Answer: C) Champaran

113. What was the 'tinkathia system' in Champaran?
    A) A system of forced labor
    B) A system where tenants were bound to plant indigo on 3/20th of their land
    C) A system of land revenue collection
    D) A system of debt bondage
    Answer: B) A system where tenants were bound to plant trees out of twenty parts of his land with Indigo

114. Who requested Gandhiji to look into the problems of the Indigo planters in Champaran?
    A) Sardar Patel
    B) Rajkumar Shukla
    C) Indulal Yagnik
    D) Mahadev Desai
    Answer: B) Rajkumar Shukla

Movement of Ahmedabad Mill workers (1918)

115. What was the dispute about between the mill owners and workers in Ahmedabad in 1918?
    A) Wage increase
    B) Working conditions
    C) 'Plague bonus' withdrawal
    D) Dismissal of workers
    Answer: C) The 'plague bonus' which the owner wanted to withdraw

116. What action did Gandhiji undertake to support the Ahmedabad mill workers' strike?
    A) Civil Disobedience
    B) Hunger strike
    C) Non-Cooperation
    D) Mass demonstration
    Answer: B) Hunger strike

117. What was the percentage increase in wages that the mill owners accepted after Gandhiji's intervention?
    A) 25%
    B) 30%
    C) 35%
    D) 40%
    Answer: C) 35%

Kheda Satyagraha (1918)

118. What was the main reason for the distress of peasants in Kheda district in 1918?
    A) High taxes and government policies
    B) Failure of crops and the government ignoring appeals for remission of land revenue
    C) Exploitation by landlords
    D) Lack of irrigation facilities
    Answer: B) Failure of crops and the government ignored their appeals for the remission of land revenue

119. Who took a leading role with Gandhiji in the Kheda Satyagraha?
    A) Rajkumar Shukla and Mahadev Desai
    B) Indulal Yagnik and Sardar Patel
    C) C.R. Das and Motilal Nehru
    D) Jawaharlal Nehru and Subhash Chandra Bose
    Answer: B) Indulal Yagnik and Sardar Patel

Rowlatt Act (1919)

120. Which committee, headed by Justice Rowlatt, led to the Rowlatt Act?
    A) Hunter Committee
    B) Sedition Committee
    C) Simon Commission
    D) Cabinet Mission
    Answer: B) Sedition Committee

121. What was a key provision of the Rowlatt Act?
    A) Right to appeal
    B) Trial by jury
    C) Detention without trial for a maximum of 2 years
    D) Reduced police powers
    Answer: C) Detention without trial for maximum 2 years

122. What was the Rowlatt Act criticized as?
    A) The Golden Act
    B) The Black Act
    C) The White Act
    D) The Green Act
    Answer: B) ‘Black Act.’

123. What was another popular description of the Rowlatt Act?
    A) 'Bina Apeel Bina Vakil'
    B) 'Bina Apeel Bina Daleel Bina Vakil'
    C) 'Swaraj Mera Janmasiddh Adhikar Hai'
    D) 'Do or Die'
    Answer: B) ‘Bina Apeel Bina Daleel Bina Vakil’.

Jallianwala Bagh Tragedy (13 April, 1919)

124. When did the Jallianwala Bagh Tragedy occur?
    A) March 13, 1919
    B) April 13, 1919
    C) May 13, 1919
    D) June 13, 1919
    Answer: B) April 13, 1919

125. The meeting at Jallianwala Bagh was called to oppose the detention of which two leaders?
    A) Mahatma Gandhi and Sardar Patel
    B) Dr Satyapal and Dr Kitchlew
    C) C.R. Das and Motilal Nehru
    D) Lala Lajpat Rai and Bal Gangadhar Tilak
    Answer: B) Dr Satyapal & Kitchlew

126. Who ordered the troops to fire at Jallianwala Bagh?
    A) General Dyer
    B) Lord Chelmsford
    C) Lord Minto
    D) General O'Dyer
    Answer: A) Dyer

127. What did R.N. Tagore return in protest against the Jallianwala Bagh massacre?
    A) His Nobel Prize
    B) His Knighthood
    C) His literary awards
    D) His property
    Answer: B) ‘Knighthood’

128. Who resigned from the executive council of the viceroy due to the Jallianwala Bagh massacre?
    A) M.A. Jinnah
    B) Shankaran Nair
    C) Tej Bahadur Sapru
    D) Satyendra Prasad Sinha
    Answer: B) Shankaran Nair

129. Under whose chairmanship did the government set up an enquiry commission for the Jallianwala Bagh massacre?
    A) Rowlatt
    B) Hunter
    C) Simon
    D) Montagu
    Answer: B) Hunter

130. When did Sardar Udham Singh kill O’Dyer in London?
    A) March 13, 1919
    B) April 13, 1919
    C) March 13, 1940
    D) April 13, 1940
    Answer: C) March 13, 1940

Khilafat Movement (1920)

131. What was the primary reason for the Khilafat Movement in India?
    A) British economic exploitation
    B) Deposition of the Sultan of Turkey (who was also the Khalif) and dismemberment of his empire
    C) Demand for Indian independence
    D) Protests against the Rowlatt Act
    Answer: B) Deposition of the Sultan of Turkey and dismemberment of his empire

132. Under whose chairmanship did an All India Khilafat Conference meet in Delhi?
    A) Muhammad Ali Jinnah
    B) Shaukat Ali
    C) Mahatma Gandhi
    D) Maulana Abul Kalam Azad
    Answer: C) Gandhiji

133. When was ‘Khilafat Day’ celebrated?
    A) October 17, 1919
    B) August 1, 1920
    C) February 5, 1922
    D) March 13, 1922
    Answer: A) October 17, 1919

134. Who suggested to the Khilafat committee to start the Non-Cooperation Movement?
    A) Shaukat Ali
    B) Maulana Abul Kalam Azad
    C) Mahatma Gandhi
    D) Muhammad Ali Jinnah
    Answer: C) Gandhiji

The Non-Cooperation Movement (1920-21)

135. When did Gandhiji launch the Non-Cooperation Movement?
    A) July 20, 1920
    B) August 1, 1920
    C) October 17, 1919
    D) February 12, 1922
    Answer: B) August 1, 1920

136. What were the three demands on which the Non-Cooperation Movement was based?
    A) Swaraj, complete independence, and economic reforms
    B) The Khilafat issue, justice for Jallianwala Bagh Massacre, and Self-Rule
    C) Boycott of foreign goods, promotion of Swadeshi, and national education
    D) Abolition of Rowlatt Act, Communal harmony, and constitutional reforms
    Answer: B) The Khilafat issue, Justice for Jallianwala Bagh Massacre, Issue of Self Rule

137. What fund was started to finance the Non-Cooperation Movement?
    A) Gandhi Fund
    B) Swaraj Fund
    C) Tilak Swaraj Fund
    D) National Fund
    Answer: C) Tilak Swaraj Fund

138. Which prominent leaders gave up their legal practices during the Non-Cooperation Movement?
    A) Sardar Patel and Indulal Yagnik
    B) C.R. Das and Motilal Nehru
    C) Jawaharlal Nehru and Subhash Chandra Bose
    D) Mahatma Gandhi and G.K. Gokhale
    Answer: B) C. R. Das and Moti Lal Nehru

139. Who resigned from the civil services during the Non-Cooperation Movement?
    A) Motilal Nehru
    B) C.R. Das
    C) Subhash Chandra Bose
    D) Jawaharlal Nehru
    Answer: C) Subhash Chandra Bose

Chauri-Chaura Incident (1922)

140. Where did the Chauri-Chaura Incident take place?
    A) Lucknow
    B) Allahabad
    C) Gorakhpur district of Uttar Pradesh
    D) Ahmedabad
    Answer: C) Chauri-Chaura in Gorakhpur district of Uttar Pradesh

141. When did the Chauri-Chaura Incident occur?
    A) February 5, 1920
    B) February 5, 1921
    C) February 5, 1922
    D) February 5, 1923
    Answer: C) February 5, 1922

142. How many policemen were burnt by the mob during the Chauri-Chaura Incident?
    A) 12
    B) 18
    C) 22
    D) 25
    Answer: C) 22

143. When did Gandhiji withdraw the Non-Cooperation Movement due to the Chauri-Chaura Incident?
    A) February 5, 1922
    B) February 12, 1922
    C) March 13, 1922
    D) January 1, 1923
    Answer: B) February 12, 1922

144. When was Mahatma Gandhi arrested after the withdrawal of the Non-Cooperation Movement?
    A) February 12, 1922
    B) March 13, 1922
    C) January 1, 1923
    D) February 5, 1922
    Answer: B) March 13, 1922

Swaraj Party (1923)

145. Who were the key leaders who demanded that nationalists should enter the legislative council and expose them?
    A) Rajendra Prasad and Rajgopalachari
    B) Motilal Nehru, C.R. Das, and N.C. Kelkar
    C) Sardar Patel and Indulal Yagnik
    D) Mahatma Gandhi and G.K. Gokhale
    Answer: B) Motilal Nehru, C.R. Das and N.C. Kelkar

146. When was the Swaraj Party formed?
    A) January 1, 1922
    B) January 1, 1923
    C) January 1, 1924
    D) January 1, 1925
    Answer: B) Jan 1, 1923

147. In the elections of 1923, where did the Das-Nehru group (Swaraj Party) emerge as the single largest party?
    A) Only in the Central Assembly
    B) Only in Bombay and Bengal Councils
    C) In the central Assembly, Bombay and Bengal Councils
    D) All over India
    Answer: C) In the central Assembly, Bombay and Bengal Councils

148. What was the strategy pursued by the Swarajists in the legislative assemblies?
    A) Cooperation with the government
    B) Complete non-participation
    C) Obstructionist strategy to defeat all legislative proposals
    D) Supporting all government measures
    Answer: C) Obstructionist strategy to defeat all proposals for legislative enactments.


The Simon Commission (1927-28)

1.  In which year did the British decide to appoint the Simon Commission?
    A) 1928
    B) 1927
    C) 1929
    D) 1930
    Answer: B) 1927

2.  Who was the chairman of the Simon Commission?
    A) Lord Irwin
    B) Lord Simon
    C) Lala Lajpat Rai
    D) Jawaharlal Nehru
    Answer: B) Simon

3.  Who was the Viceroy of India during the time of the Simon Commission?
    A) Lord Curzon
    B) Lord Minto
    C) Lord Irwin
    D) Lord Linlithgow
    Answer: C) Lord Irwin

4.  Why did the Congress oppose the Simon Commission?
    A) It recommended complete independence.
    B) All its members were British.
    C) It demanded Dominion Status.
    D) It was appointed too early.
    Answer: B) All the members of this commission were the British

5.  What did Congress call the Simon Commission?
    A) Black Commission
    B) White Commission
    C) Indian Commission
    D) Constitutional Commission
    Answer: B) white commission

6.  Who succumbed to injuries on October 30, 1929, in protest against the Simon Commission?
    A) Mahatma Gandhi
    B) Jawaharlal Nehru
    C) Lala Lajpat Rai
    D) Bhagat Singh
    Answer: C) Lala Lajpat Rai

Nehru Report (1928)

7.  In which year was the Nehru Report drafted?
    A) 1927
    B) 1928
    C) 1929
    D) 1930
    Answer: B) 1928

8.  What was a key demand of the Nehru Report regarding the form of government?
    A) Complete independence
    B) Responsible government in the provinces only
    C) Dominion Status, not complete independence
    D) Absolute monarchy
    Answer: C) Dominion Status and not complete independence

9.  Which of the following was a recommendation of the Nehru Report?
    A) Separate electorates for all minorities
    B) Dissociation of the state from religion
    C) No rights for women
    D) Restricted freedom to form unions
    Answer: B) dissociation of the state from religion

10. Who was a member of the committee that comprised the Nehru Report?
    A) Jawaharlal Nehru
    B) Motilal Nehru
    C) Subhash Chandra Bose
    D) Mahatma Gandhi
    Answer: C) Subhash Chandra Bose (and others like Tej Bahadur Sapru, Ali Imam, etc.)

The Lahore Session of the Congress (1929)

11. When did the Indian National Congress declare 'Purna Swaraj'?
    A) December 1928
    B) December 1929
    C) January 1930
    D) March 1930
    Answer: B) December, 1929

12. Who was the chairman of the Lahore Session of the Congress in 1929?
    A) Mahatma Gandhi
    B) Motilal Nehru
    C) J.L. Nehru
    D) Sardar Patel
    Answer: C) J.L. Nehru

13. Which decision was taken at the Lahore Session regarding the Round Table Conference?
    A) To attend the conference
    B) To boycott the conference
    C) To send observers
    D) To postpone a decision
    Answer: B) Round table conference to be boycotted.

14. What was declared as the first independence day to be celebrated every year, fixed at the Lahore Session?
    A) December 31, 1929
    B) January 26, 1930
    C) March 12, 1930
    D) April 6, 1930
    Answer: B) January 26, 1930

Civil Disobedience Movement

15. When did the Civil Disobedience Movement begin with the Dandi March?
    A) April 6, 1930
    B) March 12, 1930
    C) May 5, 1930
    D) January 26, 1930
    Answer: B) 12 March, 1930

16. What act formally inaugurated the Civil Disobedience Movement?
    A) Purna Swaraj Declaration
    B) Salt law breaking
    C) Dandi March
    D) Boycott of Round Table Conference
    Answer: B) The breaking of salt laws on 6 April, 1930

17. Who led the Civil Disobedience Movement in the North-Western Frontier Province?
    A) Mahatma Gandhi
    B) Jawaharlal Nehru
    C) Khan Abdul Ghaffar Khan
    D) Surya Sen
    Answer: C) Khan Abdul Ghaffar Khan

18. Which group carried out a raid on two armouries and declared a provisional government?
    A) Rani Gaidinliu's followers
    B) Chittagong Revolt Group led by Surya Sen
    C) Sholapur textile workers
    D) Bihar peasants
    Answer: B) Surya Sen’s Chittagong Revolt Group

19. In which city did textile workers go on strike and declare the establishment of a parallel government during the Civil Disobedience Movement?
    A) Ahmedabad
    B) Bombay
    C) Sholapur
    D) Calcutta
    Answer: C) Sholapur (Maharashtra)

20. What type of tax was refused in Bihar during the Civil Disobedience Movement?
    A) Land revenue
    B) Income tax
    C) Chowkidari tax
    D) Salt tax
    Answer: C) chowkidari tax

21. Who raised the banner of revolt against foreign rule in Manipur during the Civil Disobedience Movement?
    A) Rani Lakshmibai
    B) Rani Gaidinliu
    C) Sarojini Naidu
    D) Aruna Asaf Ali
    Answer: B) Rani Gaidinliu

Round Table Conferences

22. When was the First Round Table Conference held in London?
    A) October 30, 1929
    B) November 12, 1930
    C) March 5, 1931
    D) August 1932
    Answer: B) Nov. 12, 1930

23. Which major Indian political party was kept away from the First Round Table Conference?
    A) Muslim League
    B) Hindu Mahasabha
    C) Indian National Congress
    D) All India Liberal Federation
    Answer: C) Congress

Gandhi-Irwin Pact (1931)

24. Who initiated efforts to break the ice between Gandhiji and the Government leading to the Gandhi-Irwin Pact?
    A) Lord Irwin
    B) Ramsay Macdonald
    C) Tej Bahadur Sapru, M.R. Jayakar, and Srinivas Shashtri
    D) Jawaharlal Nehru
    Answer: C) Tej Bahadur Sapru, M.R. Jayakar and Srinivas Shashtri

25. When was the Gandhi-Irwin Pact signed?
    A) March 12, 1930
    B) March 5, 1931
    C) November 12, 1930
    D) August 1932
    Answer: B) March 5, 1931

26. What did Gandhiji agree to do as part of the Gandhi-Irwin Pact?
    A) Launch a new movement
    B) Suspend the Civil Disobedience Movement and join the 2nd Round Table Conference
    C) Demand complete independence
    D) Boycott all future negotiations
    Answer: B) suspend Disobedience movement and agreed to join the 2nd round table conference.

27. What did the government concede as part of the Gandhi-Irwin Pact?
    A) Granting immediate dominion status
    B) Releasing all political prisoners and the right to make salt for consumption for villages along the coast
    C) Withdrawal of all taxes
    D) Complete autonomy to provinces
    Answer: B) released the political prisoners and conceded the right to make salt for consumption for villages along the coast

2nd Round Table Conference (1931)

28. Who was the Prime Minister of Britain during the 2nd Round Table Conference?
    A) Winston Churchill
    B) Clement Attlee
    C) Ramsay Macdonald
    D) Neville Chamberlain
    Answer: C) Ramsay Macdonald

29. What did Gandhiji demand control over during the 2nd Round Table Conference?
    A) Finance and Police
    B) Education and Health
    C) Defence and Foreign Affairs
    D) Agriculture and Industry
    Answer: C) defence and foreign affairs.

30. Who opposed Ambedkar's demand for separate electorates for Dalits at the 2nd Round Table Conference?
    A) Hindu Mahasabha
    B) Muslim League
    C) Gandhiji
    D) The Princess
    Answer: C) Gandhiji

Communal Award and Poona Pact (1932)

31. When did Ramsay Macdonald announce the 'Communal Award'?
    A) August 1931
    B) August 1932
    C) September 1932
    D) November 1932
    Answer: B) August the 1932

32. What was one of the recommendations of the Communal Award regarding provincial legislatures?
    A) To reduce the existing seats
    B) To abolish provincial legislatures
    C) To double the existing seats
    D) To introduce a single electorate
    Answer: C) To double the existing seats in provincial legislatures.

33. The Communal Award declared which group as minorities, entitling them to separate electorates?
    A) Sikhs
    B) Indian Christians
    C) Depressed classes
    D) Anglo-Indians
    Answer: C) depressed classes

34. Where was Gandhiji when he started a fast unto death against the Communal Award?
    A) Dandi
    B) Ahmedabad
    C) Yervada jail
    D) London
    Answer: C) Yervada jail

Poona Pact (Sep 25, 1932)

35. When was the Poona Pact signed?
    A) August 1932
    B) September 25, 1932
    C) November 1932
    D) September 1931
    Answer: B) Sep 25, 1932

36. How many seats were allotted to the depressed classes in the Provincial legislatures by the Poona Pact, as against 71 promised by the Communal Award?
    A) 71
    B) 100
    C) 148
    D) 18%
    Answer: C) 148 seats

37. What percentage of the total seats in the central legislature were allotted to the depressed classes by the Poona Pact?
    A) 71%
    B) 148%
    C) 18%
    D) 25%
    Answer: C) 18%

38. What principle did Ambedkar accept under the Poona Pact?
    A) Separate electorates
    B) Joint electorate
    C) Reserved seats without joint electorate
    D) No representation
    Answer: B) the principle of joint electorate.

39. Who became active to settle the issue leading to the Poona Pact?
    A) Gandhiji and Ambedkar alone
    B) Madan Mohan Malaviya, M.C. Rajah, and B.R. Ambedkar
    C) Jawaharlal Nehru and Motilal Nehru
    D) Lord Irwin and Ramsay Macdonald
    Answer: B) Madan Mohan Malaviya, M.C. Rajah and B.R. Ambedkar

40. When was an All-India anti-untouchability league started?
    A) August 1932
    B) September 1932
    C) November 1932
    D) January 1933
    Answer: B) September, 1932

Third Round Table Conference (November 1932)

41. When did the Third Round Table Conference meet in London?
    A) September 1932
    B) October 1932
    C) November 1932
    D) December 1932
    Answer: C) November 1932

42. Which major Indian political party boycotted the Third Round Table Conference?
    A) Muslim League
    B) Hindu Mahasabha
    C) Congress
    D) Princely States
    Answer: C) congress

Resignation of the Congress Ministries

43. Who declared India to be at war without the prior assent of the central legislature?
    A) Lord Mountbatten
    B) Lord Wavell
    C) Lord Linlithgow
    D) Lord Irwin
    Answer: C) Lord Linlithgow

44. What did the Muslim League celebrate on the day of the resignation of the Congress ministries?
    A) Pakistan Day
    B) Deliverance Day
    C) Freedom Day
    D) Loyalty Day
    Answer: B) ‘Deliverance Day’

August Declaration (August 8, 1940)

45. When was the August Declaration put forward?
    A) September 1939
    B) August 8, 1940
    C) October 1940
    D) March 1942
    Answer: B) August 8, 1940

46. Who put forward the proposals of the August Declaration?
    A) Lord Irwin
    B) Lord Wavell
    C) Lord Linlithgow
    D) Lord Mountbatten
    Answer: C) Lord Linlithgow

47. What did the August Declaration offer regarding India's future status?
    A) Immediate complete independence
    B) Dominion status in the unspecified future
    C) Full provincial autonomy
    D) A separate Muslim state
    Answer: B) Dominion status in the unspecified future.

48. Which group was given indirect veto power regarding the adoption of a future constitution under the August Declaration?
    A) Congress
    B) Minorities (implicitly Muslim League)
    C) Princely states
    D) Depressed classes
    Answer: B) minorities (indirectly veto power given to Muslim League)

The Demand for Pakistan (1940)

49. In which year did Iqbal suggest the idea of a Muslim state within the federation including frontier province, Baluchistan, Sindh and Kashmir?
    A) 1930
    B) 1933
    C) 1940
    D) 1947
    Answer: A) 1930

50. Who gave the term 'Pakistan' in 1933?
    A) Muhammad Ali Jinnah
    B) Allama Iqbal
    C) Choudhary Rehmat Ali
    D) Liaquat Ali Khan
    Answer: C) Choudhary Rehmat Ali

51. In which session did the Muslim League first pass the proposal of a separate Pakistan?
    A) Lahore session in 1940
    B) Calcutta session in 1906
    C) Lucknow session in 1916
    D) Karachi session in 1913
    Answer: A) its Lahore session in 1940.

Individual Satyagraha (October 1940)

52. When did the Congress working committee meet in Wardha to discuss Individual Civil Disobedience?
    A) August 8, 1940
    B) October 13, 1940
    C) March 1942
    D) August 8, 1942
    Answer: B) On 13th October

53. Who was selected by Gandhiji to inaugurate the Individual Satyagraha movement?
    A) Jawaharlal Nehru
    B) Sardar Patel
    C) Vinoba Bhave
    D) J.B. Kripalani
    Answer: C) Vinoba Bhave

Cripps’ Proposals (March, 1942)

54. When did the Cripps Mission come to India?
    A) October 1940
    B) March 1942
    C) August 1942
    D) June 1945
    Answer: B) March, 1942

55. What was one of the key constitutional proposals of the Cripps Mission?
    A) Immediate complete independence
    B) Dominion status to be granted after the war
    C) Immediate transfer of defense to Indian hands
    D) Formation of an interim government immediately
    Answer: B) Dominion status to be granted after the war.

56. How would members of the constitution-making body for India be elected/nominated according to Cripps' Proposals?
    A) Directly elected by the people
    B) Elected by provincial assemblies and nominated by rulers in princely states
    C) Appointed by the British government
    D) Only nominated by the Viceroy
    Answer: B) elected by the provincial assemblies and nominated by the rulers in case of the princely states.

57. How did Gandhiji describe Cripps' Proposals?
    A) A great step forward
    B) "post-dated cheque on a crashing bank"
    C) A satisfactory offer
    D) A clear path to independence
    Answer: B) “post-dated cheque on a crashing bank”.

Quit India Movement (1942)

58. When was the Quit India resolution passed?
    A) March 1942
    B) August 8, 1942
    C) October 1940
    D) June 1945
    Answer: B) August 8, 1942

59. Where was the Quit India resolution passed?
    A) Delhi
    B) Calcutta
    C) Gowalia Tank, Bombay
    D) Wardha
    Answer: C) Gowalia Tank, Bombay

60. What was Gandhiji's message during the Quit India Movement?
    A) Non-Violence
    B) Satyagraha
    C) 'Do or Die'
    D) Swaraj
    Answer: C) ‘Do or Die.’

61. Which prominent leader emerged during the Quit India Movement, when it was leaderless?
    A) Mahatma Gandhi
    B) Jawaharlal Nehru
    C) Ram Manohar Lohia
    D) Sardar Patel
    Answer: C) Ram Manohar Lohia (also Aruna Asaf Ali, Achyut Patwardhan, Jayaprakash Narayan)

62. Who started an underground radio during the Quit India Movement?
    A) Aruna Asaf Ali
    B) Jayaprakash Narayan
    C) Usha Mehta
    D) Ram Manohar Lohia
    Answer: C) Usha Mehta

The Parallel Governments

63. Where was a parallel government established under Nana Patil?
    A) Tamluk
    B) Balia
    C) Satara
    D) Katak
    Answer: C) Satara

64. Where was 'Tamluk Jatiya Sarkar' established under Satish Samant?
    A) Satara
    B) Midnapur, West Bengal
    C) Balia
    D) Katak
    Answer: B) Tamluk Jatiya Sarkar Midhapur, West Bengal

65. Where was a parallel government established under Chittu Pandey?
    A) Midnapur
    B) Satara
    C) Balia
    D) Katak
    Answer: C) Balia

66. Where was a parallel government established under Lakshman Nayak?
    A) Satara
    B) Balia
    C) Katak
    D) Midnapur
    Answer: C) Katak

The Indian National Army

67. When did Subhash Chandra Bose escape from his home in Calcutta and reach Berlin?
    A) January 14, 1941
    B) March 28, 1941
    C) December 1943
    D) October 1940
    Answer: A) 14 Jan, 1941 (reached Berlin on March 28, 1941)

68. Who originally conceived the idea of the Indian National Army?
    A) Subhash Chandra Bose
    B) Mohan Singh
    C) Rasbihari Bose
    D) Lakshmi Swaminathan
    Answer: C) Rasbihari Bose

69. Who helped Rasbihari Bose in forming the INA, being an Indian officer of the British India Army in Malaya?
    A) S.C. Bose
    B) Mohan Singh
    C) Abid Husain
    D) Lakshmi Swaminathan
    Answer: B) Mohan Singh

70. Who handed over the chairmanship of INA to S.C. Bose?
    A) Mohan Singh
    B) Rasbihari Bose
    C) Lakshmi Swaminathan
    D) Abid Husain
    Answer: B) Rasbihari Bose

71. Which regiment was established by Lakshmi Swaminathan within the INA?
    A) Gandhi Brigade
    B) Nehru Brigade
    C) Rani Lakshmi Bai Regiment
    D) Azad Brigade
    Answer: C) Rani Lakshmi Bai Regimenl

72. Which of the following was an exclusive women's force in the INA?
    A) Gandhi brigade
    B) Nehru brigade
    C) Azad brigade
    D) Rani Jhansi brigade
    Answer: D) Rani Jhansi brigade

73. Where did S.C. Bose give the call, "You give me blood and I will give you freedom"?
    A) Delhi
    B) Calcutta
    C) Rangoon
    D) Singapore
    Answer: C) Jubilee place of Rangoon.

74. When did INA win Andaman and Nicobar Island?
    A) December 1942
    B) December 1943
    C) December 1944
    D) December 1945
    Answer: B) December 1943

75. What names were given to Andaman and Nicobar Islands by INA after their capture?
    A) Freedom and Unity
    B) Shahid and Swaraj
    C) Bose and Gandhi
    D) Azad and Hind
    Answer: B) ‘Shahid’ and ‘Swaraj’ respectively.

76. Who escaped Gandhi Brigade from the British Army and was given the title 'Sardar-A-Jung' by S.C. Bose?
    A) Mohan Singh
    B) Lakshmi Swaminathan
    C) Abid Husain
    D) P. K. Sehgal
    Answer: C) Abid Husain

INA Trials

77. Where were the INA trials held?
    A) Red Fort, Delhi
    B) Victoria Memorial, Calcutta
    C) Gateway of India, Bombay
    D) India Gate, Delhi
    Answer: A) Red Fort, Delhi.

78. Who were put on trial at the Red Fort in the INA trials?
    A) Subhash Chandra Bose, Mohan Singh, and Rasbihari Bose
    B) P. K. Sehgal, Shah Nawaj, and Gurbaksh Singh Dhillon
    C) Abid Husain, Lakshmi Swaminathan, and Azad
    D) Jawaharlal Nehru, Sardar Patel, and Bhulabhai Desai
    Answer: B) P. K. Sehgal, Shah Nawaj and Gurbaksh Singh Dhillon

79. Who appeared for the defence in the INA trials?
    A) Jinnah and Liaquat Ali Khan
    B) Bhulabhai Desai, Tejbahadur Sapru, J.L. Nehru, K.N. Katju, and Arun Asaf Ali
    C) P. K. Sehgal, Shah Nawaj, and Gurbaksh Singh Dhillon
    D) Wavell and Mountbatten
    Answer: B) Bhula bai Desai, Tejbahadur Sapru, J.L. Nehru, K.N. Katju and Arun Asaf Ali

80. Who used veto power to set the INA prisoners free?
    A) Lord Mountbatten
    B) Lord Linlithgow
    C) Lord Wavell
    D) General Dyer
    Answer: C) Wavell

81. When was 'INA Day' celebrated?
    A) December 1943
    B) November 12, 1945
    C) June 4, 1945
    D) February 18, 1946
    Answer: B) November 12, 1945

Wavell Plan (4 June, 1945)

82. When was the Wavell Plan proposed?
    A) March 1942
    B) August 1942
    C) June 4, 1945
    D) February 1946
    Answer: C) 4 June, 1945

83. What was a key provision of the Wavell Plan regarding the Executive Council?
    A) All members were to be British.
    B) All members were to be Indians.
    C) With the exception of the governor-general and the commander-in-chief, all members were to be Indians.
    D) Only British members were to be on the council.
    Answer: C) with the exception of the governor-general and the commander-in-chief, all members of the executive council were to be Indians.

84. Which communities were given equal representation in the assembly under the Wavell Plan?
    A) Sikhs and Indian Christians
    B) Caste Hindus and Muslims
    C) Depressed classes and Anglo-Indians
    D) Princely states and Provinces
    Answer: B) Caste Hindus and Muslims

85. Why was the Simla conference convened in regard to the Wavell Plan rejected?
    A) Congress rejected it.
    B) Jinnah rejected it.
    C) Viceroy Wavell withdrew it.
    D) British Parliament rejected it.
    Answer: B) rejected by Jinnah.

Revolt of the Royal Indian Navy (1946)

86. When did the Revolt of the Royal Indian Navy begin in Bombay?
    A) June 4, 1945
    B) February 18, 1946
    C) March 24, 1946
    D) May 16, 1946
    Answer: B) 18 February, 1946

87. What was one of the reasons for the naval mutiny?
    A) Demand for immediate independence
    B) Flagrant racial discrimination, unpalatable food, and abuse by superior officers
    C) Support for the INA trials
    D) Boycott of elections
    Answer: B) flagrant racial discrimination unpalatable food and abuse by superior officers.

88. What was scrawled on the ship by B. C. Dutt that led to his arrest and sparked the mutiny?
    A) 'Jai Hind'
    B) 'Quit India'
    C) 'Inquilab Zindabad'
    D) 'Vande Mataram'
    Answer: B) ‘quit India’

Cabinet Mission (1946)

89. When did the Cabinet Mission reach Delhi?
    A) February 18, 1946
    B) March 24, 1946
    C) May 16, 1946
    D) September 2, 1946
    Answer: B) 24 March, 1946

90. Who were the three members of the Cabinet Mission?
    A) Lord Wavell, Lord Mountbatten, and Winston Churchill
    B) Pethick Lawrence, Stafford Cripps, and A.V. Alexander
    C) J.L. Nehru, Sardar Patel, and Jinnah
    D) Ramsay Macdonald, Lord Irwin, and Lord Linlithgow
    Answer: B) Pethick Lawrence, Stafford Cripps, and A.V. Alexander

91. When did the Cabinet Mission put forward its proposals?
    A) March 24, 1946
    B) May 16, 1946
    C) July 1946
    D) September 2, 1946
    Answer: B) On May 16, 1946

92. What was the Cabinet Mission's stance on the demand for a full-fledged Pakistan?
    A) It fully supported it.
    B) It rejected the demand.
    C) It left the decision to a referendum.
    D) It recommended a truncated Pakistan.
    Answer: B) Rejection of the demand for a full fledged Pakistan.

93. Into how many sections were the members of the Constituent Assembly divided according to the Cabinet Mission Plan?
    A) Two
    B) Three
    C) Four
    D) Five
    Answer: B) three sections.

94. Which section of the Constituent Assembly under the Cabinet Mission Plan comprised Muslim Majority provinces in the north-east?
    A) Section - A
    B) Section - B
    C) Section - C
    D) Section - D
    Answer: C) Section - C

95. What was the status of princely states under the Cabinet Mission Plan?
    A) They would automatically join India.
    B) They would automatically join Pakistan.
    C) They were no longer to be under the paramountcy of the British Government.
    D) They would remain British protectorates.
    Answer: C) Princely states were no longer to be under paramountcy of British Government.

96. Which political parties accepted the Cabinet Mission Plan?
    A) Only Congress
    B) Only Muslim League
    C) Both Congress and Muslim League
    D) Neither Congress nor Muslim League
    Answer: C) Both congress and Muslim league accepted it.

97. When were the elections for the Constituent Assembly held?
    A) May 1946
    B) July 1946
    C) September 1946
    D) October 1946
    Answer: B) July 1946

98. How many seats did Congress get out of the total 273 seats in the Constituent Assembly elections?
    A) 100
    B) 150
    C) 209
    D) 273
    Answer: C) 209

99. When was an interim government consisting of Congress nominees formed based on the Cabinet Mission Plan?
    A) July 1946
    B) September 2, 1946
    C) October 26, 1946
    D) July 29, 1946
    Answer: B) 2 September, 1946

100. Who was the vice-president of the interim government formed on September 2, 1946?
    A) Lord Wavell
    B) J.L. Nehru
    C) Sardar Patel
    D) Muhammad Ali Jinnah
    Answer: B) J.L. Nehru

101. When did the Muslim League finally join the interim government?
    A) September 2, 1946
    B) October 26, 1946
    C) July 29, 1946
    D) June 29, 1946
    Answer: B) on 26 October, 1946.

102. Why did the Muslim League withdraw its acceptance of the Cabinet Mission Plan?
    A) Congress refused to join the interim government.
    B) Jinnah was alarmed at the results of the elections, fearing the Muslim League would be eclipsed.
    C) The British government changed the plan.
    D) They achieved full Pakistan.
    Answer: B) Jinnah was alarmed at the results of the elections because the muslim league was in danger of being totally eclipsed in the constituent Assembly.

103. When did the Cabinet Mission return?
    A) May 16, 1946
    B) July 29, 1946
    C) June 29, 1946
    D) September 2, 1946
    Answer: C) 29 June, 1946

104. What resolution did the Muslim League pass after withdrawing its acceptance of the Cabinet Mission Plan?
    A) Quit India Resolution
    B) Purna Swaraj Resolution
    C) Direct Action Resolution
    D) Demand for Interim Government
    Answer: C) ‘Direct action’ resolution.

105. When did Jinnah celebrate ‘Pakistan Day’?
    A) March 24, 1946
    B) May 16, 1946
    C) May 27, 1947
    D) June 3, 1947
    Answer: C) May 27, 1947

Lord Attlee’s Declaration (1947)

106. When did Mr. Attlee announce in the House of Commons that the British Government decided to quit India by a fixed date?
    A) January 30, 1948
    B) February 20, 1947
    C) June 3, 1947
    D) July 18, 1947
    Answer: B) February 20, 1947

107. By what date did the British Government decide to quit India, as announced by Attlee?
    A) March 30, 1947
    B) June 30, 1948
    C) August 15, 1947
    D) December 31, 1947
    Answer: B) 30 June, 1948

Mountbatten Plan (3 June, 1947)

108. When was Lord Mountbatten sworn in as Governor General?
    A) February 20, 1947
    B) March 24, 1947
    C) June 3, 1947
    D) August 15, 1947
    Answer: B) 24, March, 1947

109. When was the Mountbatten Plan announced?
    A) February 20, 1947
    B) March 24, 1947
    C) June 3, 1947
    D) July 18, 1947
    Answer: C) 3 June, 1947

110. What was a key outline of the Mountbatten Plan regarding India?
    A) India would remain a single, undivided nation.
    B) India to be divided into India and Pakistan.
    C) India would become a British dominion indefinitely.
    D) Princely states would become independent.
    Answer: B) India to be divided into India and Pakistan.

111. Which provinces would be partitioned according to the Mountbatten Plan?
    A) Punjab and Sindh
    B) Bengal and Bihar
    C) Bengal and Punjab
    D) Assam and NWFP
    Answer: C) Bengal and Punjab

112. Which date was fixed for the handover of power to India and Pakistan?
    A) June 3, 1947
    B) July 18, 1947
    C) August 15, 1947
    D) June 30, 1948
    Answer: C) August 15, 1947

Indian Independence Act (1947)

113. When did the British Parliament pass the 'Independence of India Act, 1947'?
    A) June 3, 1947
    B) July 18, 1947
    C) August 15, 1947
    D) August 18, 1947
    Answer: B) On 18 July

114. When was the Indian Independence Act, 1947, implemented?
    A) July 18, 1947
    B) August 15, 1947
    C) June 3, 1947
    D) June 30, 1948
    Answer: B) August 15, 1947

115. What was abolished by the Indian Independence Act, 1947?
    A) The office of the Governor-General
    B) The sovereignty of the British Parliament
    C) The Princely States
    D) The Indian National Congress
    Answer: B) Sovereignty of British parliament was abolished.

116. How many princely states were there in India at the time of independence?
    A) 500
    B) 562
    C) 600
    D) 650
    Answer: B) 562

117. Which princely state signed the Instrument of Accession with Pakistan?
    A) Kashmir
    B) Hyderabad
    C) Junagarh
    D) Bahawalpur
    Answer: D) Bhawalpur

118. Which areas were under the control of the Portuguese at the time of India's independence?
    A) Pondicherry
    B) Goa, Daman and Diu, and Dadra and Nagar Haveli
    C) Chandannagar
    D) Mahe and Karaikal
    Answer: B) Goa, Daman and Diu and Dadra and Nagar Haveli

119. Which area was under French domination at the time of India's independence?
    A) Goa
    B) Daman
    C) Pondicherry
    D) Diu
    Answer: C) Pondicherry


Slogans and Leaders

1.  The slogan "Delhi Chalo" was given by:
    a) Mahatma Gandhi
    b) Jawaharlal Nehru
    c) Subhash Chandra Bose
    d) Bhagat Singh
    Answer: c) Subhash Chandra Bose

2.  "Jai Hind" was a slogan popularized by:
    a) Mahatma Gandhi
    b) Subhash Chandra Bose
    c) Bal Gangadhar Tilak
    d) Jawaharlal Nehru
    Answer: b) Subhash Chandra Bose

3.  Who famously said, "Tum Mujhe Khun Do, Mai Tumhe Azadi Dunga"?
    a) Bhagat Singh
    b) Mahatma Gandhi
    c) Subhash Chandra Bose
    d) Sardar Vallabhbhai Patel
    Answer: c) Subhash Chandra Bose

4.  The slogan "Bharat Chodo" is associated with:
    a) Subhash Chandra Bose
    b) Mahatma Gandhi
    c) Bhagat Singh
    d) Jawaharlal Nehru
    Answer: b) Mahatma Gandhi

5.  "Do or Die" was the inspiring call given by:
    a) Bhagat Singh
    b) Subhash Chandra Bose
    c) Mahatma Gandhi
    d) Jawaharlal Nehru
    Answer: c) Mahatma Gandhi

6.  Who coined the slogan "Aaram Haram Hai"?
    a) Mahatma Gandhi
    b) Jawaharlal Nehru
    c) Bhagat Singh
    d) Subhash Chandra Bose
    Answer: b) Jawaharlal Nehru

7.  "Inklab Zindabad" was a revolutionary slogan given by:
    a) Subhash Chandra Bose
    b) Mahatma Gandhi
    c) Bhagat Singh
    d) Bal Gangadhar Tilak
    Answer: c) Bhagat Singh

8.  The slogan "Back to the Vedas" was given by:
    a) Swami Vivekananda
    b) Raja Ram Mohan Roy
    c) Dayanand Saraswati
    d) Ishwar Chandra Vidyasagar
    Answer: c) Dayanand Saraswati

9.  "Swaraj is my birth right and I shall have it" was a powerful declaration by:
    a) Mahatma Gandhi
    b) Jawaharlal Nehru
    c) Bal Gangadhar Tilak
    d) Bhagat Singh
    Answer: c) Bal Gangadhar Tilak

Newspapers of British India

10. India's first newspaper was:
    a) Madras Courier
    b) Bombay Herald
    c) Bengal Gazette
    d) Samachar Darshan
    Answer: c) Bengal Gazette

11. The Bengal Gazette was founded in which year?
    a) 1784
    b) 1780
    c) 1789
    d) 1818
    Answer: b) 1780

12. Who was the founder/editor of the Bengal Gazette?
    a) Kaire, Marshman
    b) James Silk Buckingham
    c) James Augustus Hickey
    d) Raja Ram Mohan Roy
    Answer: c) James Augustus Hickey

13. The oldest newspaper in an Indian language was:
    a) Sambad Kaumudi
    b) Bombay Samachar
    c) Samachar Darshan
    d) Mirat-ul-Akhbar
    Answer: c) Samachar Darshan

14. Samachar Darshan (Bangla) was founded in:
    a) 1821
    b) 1822
    c) 1818
    d) 1826
    Answer: c) 1818

15. Who was the founder/editor of Samachar Darshan?
    a) Raja Ram Mohan Roy
    b) Fardoorejee Majban
    c) Kaire, Marshman
    d) Jugul Kishore
    Answer: c) Kaire, Marshman

16. Sambad Kaumudi (Bangla) was founded by:
    a) Ishwar Chandra Gupta
    b) Raja Ram Mohan Roy
    c) Fardoorejee Majban
    d) P.M. Motiwala
    Answer: b) Raja Ram Mohan Roy

17. In which year was Sambad Kaumudi founded?
    a) 1822
    b) 1821
    c) 1830
    d) 1831
    Answer: b) 1821

18. Bombay Samachar (Gujarati) was founded in 1822 by:
    a) Raja Ram Mohan Roy
    b) Fardoorejee Majban
    c) Jugul Kishore
    d) P.M. Motiwala
    Answer: b) Fardoorejee Majban

19. Mirat-ul-Akhbar (Farsi) was founded by Raja Ram Mohan Roy in:
    a) 1821
    b) 1822
    c) 1826
    d) 1830
    Answer: b) 1822

20. The first newspaper of Hindi, Udant Martand, was founded in 1826 by:
    a) Ishwar Chandra Gupta
    b) Jugul Kishore
    c) P.M. Motiwala
    d) Bennet Colloman & Co.
    Answer: b) Jugul Kishore

21. Who founded Bombay Times (English) in 1838?
    a) Bennet Colloman & Co.
    b) Harishchandra Mukherjee
    c) Ishwar Chandra Vidyasagar
    d) Dada Bhai Nauroji
    Answer: a) Bennet Colloman & Co.

22. Hindu Patriot was founded by Harishchandra Mukherjee in:
    a) 1858
    b) 1853
    c) 1861
    d) 1865
    Answer: b) 1853

23. Som Prakash was founded by Ishwar Chandra Vidyasagar in which year?
    a) 1853
    b) 1858
    c) 1861
    d) 1868
    Answer: b) 1858

24. Rast Goftar was founded by Dada Bhai Nauroji in:
    a) 1861
    b) 1865
    c) 1868
    d) 1875
    Answer: a) 1861

25. Amrit Bazar Patrika was founded in 1868 by:
    a) S.K. Ghosh, Motilal Ghosh, Tusarkanti Ghosh
    b) Robert Knight, Sunanda Dutta Roy
    c) Sir Dayal Singh Mazithia
    d) Vir Raghvachari, G.S. Iyer, N Subba Rao Pantulu
    Answer: a) S.K. Ghosh, Motilal Ghosh, Tusarkanti Ghosh

26. The first evening newspaper, Madras Mail, was founded in:
    a) 1865
    b) 1868
    c) 1875
    d) 1877
    Answer: b) 1868

27. Who founded Statesman in 1875?
    a) S.K. Ghosh
    b) Robert Knight, Sunanda Dutta Roy
    c) Sir Dayal Singh Mazithia
    d) Vir Raghvachari
    Answer: b) Robert Knight, Sunanda Dutta Roy

28. The Tribune (Chandigarh, Punjab) was founded in 1877 by:
    a) Robert Knight
    b) Sir Dayal Singh Mazithia
    c) Vir Raghvachari
    d) Bipin Chandra Pal
    Answer: b) Sir Dayal Singh Mazithia

29. Hindu (English-Madras) was founded in 1878 by:
    a) Vir Raghvachari, G.S. Iyer. N Subba Rao Pantulu
    b) Bipin Chandra Pal, Aurobindo Ghosh
    c) Bal Gangadhar Tilak
    d) Gopal Ganesh Agarkar
    Answer: a) Vir Raghvachari, G.S. Iyer. N Subba Rao Pantulu

30. Paridashak was founded in 1880 by:
    a) Bal Gangadhar Tilak
    b) Gopal Ganesh Agarkar
    c) Bipin Chandra Pal, Aurobindo Ghosh
    d) Maulana Abul Kalam Azad
    Answer: c) Bipin Chandra Pal, Aurobindo Ghosh

31. Kesari (Marathi) and Maratha (English) were both founded in 1881 by:
    a) Gopal Ganesh Agarkar
    b) Bipin Chandra Pal
    c) Bal Gangadhar Tilak
    d) Annie Besant
    Answer: c) Bal Gangadhar Tilak

32. Sudharak (Marathi) was founded in 1888 by:
    a) Bal Gangadhar Tilak
    b) Gopal Ganesh Agarkar
    c) Bipin Chandra Pal
    d) Arvind Ghosh
    Answer: b) Gopal Ganesh Agarkar

33. Bande Matram was founded in 1906 by:
    a) Maulana Abul Kalam Azad
    b) Feroz Shah Mehta
    c) Annie Besant
    d) Bipin Chandra Pal, Arvind Ghosh
    Answer: d) Bipin Chandra Pal, Arvind Ghosh

34. Al-Hilal was founded in 1912 by:
    a) Annie Besant
    b) Feroz Shah Mehta
    c) Maulana Abul Kalam Azad
    d) Bal Gangadhar Tilak
    Answer: c) Maulana Abul Kalam Azad

35. Bombay Chronicle was founded in 1913 by:
    a) Annie Besant
    b) Feroz Shah Mehta
    c) Maulana Abul Kalam Azad
    d) Bipin Chandra Pal
    Answer: b) Feroz Shah Mehta

36. Madras Standard was founded by Annie Besant in:
    a) 1906
    b) 1912
    c) 1913
    d) 1914
    Answer: d) 1914

Titles Given to National Freedom Fighters

37. The title "Mahatma" was given to M.K. Gandhi by:
    a) Subhash Chandra Bose
    b) Maharaja Khetadi
    c) Rabindranath Tagore
    d) Women of Bardoli
    Answer: c) Rabindranath Tagore

38. M.K. Gandhi was given the title "Father of the Nation" by:
    a) Rabindranath Tagore
    b) Subhash Chandra Bose
    c) Women of Bardoli
    d) Akbar II
    Answer: b) Subhash Chandra Bose

39. Who was known by the title "Lokahitwadi"?
    a) Gopal Hari Deshmukh
    b) Vallabhbhai Patel
    c) Ram Mohan Roy
    d) Swami Vivekanand
    Answer: a) Gopal Hari Deshmukh

40. The title "Sardar" was bestowed upon Vallabhbhai Patel by:
    a) Mahatma Gandhi
    b) Women of Bardoli
    c) Akbar II
    d) Rabindranath Tagore
    Answer: b) Women of Bardoli

41. Raja Ram Mohan Roy was given the title "Raja" by:
    a) Rabindranath Tagore
    b) Akbar II
    c) Subhash Chandra Bose
    d) Maharaja Khetadi
    Answer: b) Akbar II

42. Who was famously known as "Netaji"?
    a) Mahatma Gandhi
    b) Jawaharlal Nehru
    c) Subhash Chandra Bose
    d) Bhagat Singh
    Answer: c) Subhash Chandra Bose

43. The title "Deshnayak" was given to Subhash Chandra Bose by:
    a) Mahatma Gandhi
    b) Rabindranath Tagore
    c) Jawaharlal Nehru
    d) Sardar Vallabhbhai Patel
    Answer: b) Ravindranath Tagore

44. Swami Vivekanand's title "Vivekanand" was given by:
    a) Rabindranath Tagore
    b) Maharaja Khetadi
    c) M.K. Gandhi
    d) Ram Mohan Roy
    Answer: b) Maharaja Khetadi

Annual Sessions of the Indian National Congress

45. The first session of the Indian National Congress was held in 1885 at:
    a) Calcutta
    b) Madras
    c) Bombay
    d) Allahabad
    Answer: c) Bombay

46. Who was the president of the first Indian National Congress session in 1885?
    a) Dadabhai Nauroji
    b) Badruddin Tayyabji
    c) George Yule
    d) W. C. Banerjee
    Answer: d) W. C. Banerjee

47. The 1885 INC session was attended by how many delegates?
    a) 436
    b) 72
    c) 617
    d) 200
    Answer: b) 72

48. Who was the first Muslim President of the Indian National Congress?
    a) Dadabhai Nauroji
    b) Badruddin Tayyabji
    c) M. A. Sayani
    d) Maulana Abul Kalam Azad
    Answer: b) Badruddin Tayyabji

49. Badruddin Tayyabji presided over the INC session in:
    a) 1886
    b) 1887
    c) 1888
    d) 1890
    Answer: b) 1887

50. The first English President of the Indian National Congress was:
    a) William Wedderburn
    b) Henry Cotton
    c) George Yule
    d) Alfred Webb
    Answer: c) George Yule

51. George Yule presided over the INC session held in:
    a) Bombay (1889)
    b) Allahabad (1888)
    c) Calcutta (1890)
    d) Nagpur (1891)
    Answer: b) Allahabad (1888)

52. In which year was the decision taken to organize a session of Congress in London?
    a) 1889
    b) 1890
    c) 1891
    d) 1892
    Answer: b) 1890

53. Who was the president of the INC session in Calcutta in 1890?
    a) William Wedderburn
    b) F. S. Mehta
    c) P. Anand Charlu
    d) W. C. Banerjee
    Answer: b) F. S. Mehta

54. The demand for a representative body only for the educated class was made during the INC session in:
    a) 1893 (Lahore)
    b) 1894 (Madras)
    c) 1895 (Poona)
    d) 1896 (Calcutta)
    Answer: c) 1895 (Poona)

55. Who was the president of the Poona session of INC in 1895?
    a) Dadabhai Nauroji
    b) Mr. Alfred Webb
    c) S. N. Banerjee
    d) M. A. Sayani
    Answer: c) S. N. Banerjee

56. In which INC session was social reform set as the main goal?
    a) 1896 (Calcutta)
    b) 1897 (Amravati)
    c) 1898 (Madras)
    d) 1899 (Lucknow)
    Answer: c) 1898 (Madras)

57. Who was the president of the Madras session of INC in 1898?
    a) M.C. Sankaran
    b) A. M. Bose
    c) Ramesh C. Dutt
    d) H. G. Chandavarkar
    Answer: b) A. M. Bose

58. The resentment against the partition of Bengal was expressed during the INC session of:
    a) 1903 (Madras)
    b) 1904 (Bombay)
    c) 1905 (Varanasi)
    d) 1906 (Calcutta)
    Answer: c) 1905 (Varanasi)

59. Who was the president of the Varanasi session of INC in 1905?
    a) Henry Cotton
    b) Gopal Krishna Gokhale
    c) Dadabhai Nauroji
    d) Ras Bihari Ghosh
    Answer: b) Gopal Krishna Gokhale

60. The word "Swaraj" was first used from the Congress platform in the session of:
    a) 1905 (Varanasi)
    b) 1906 (Calcutta)
    c) 1907 (Surat)
    d) 1908 (Madras)
    Answer: b) 1906 (Calcutta)

61. Who was the president of the Calcutta session of INC in 1906?
    a) Gopal Krishna Gokhale
    b) Dadabhai Nauroji
    c) Ras Bihari Ghosh
    d) Annie Besant
    Answer: b) Dadabhai Nauroji

62. The Indian National Congress split in the session of:
    a) 1906 (Calcutta)
    b) 1907 (Surat)
    c) 1908 (Madras)
    d) 1916 (Lucknow)
    Answer: b) 1907 (Surat)

63. Who was the president of the Surat session of INC in 1907?
    a) Dadabhai Nauroji
    b) Ras Bihari Ghosh
    c) Ambika Charan Majumdar
    d) Annie Besant
    Answer: b) Ras Bihari Ghosh

64. A constitution for the Congress was adopted in the session of:
    a) 1907 (Surat)
    b) 1908 (Madras)
    c) 1916 (Lucknow)
    d) 1917 (Calcutta)
    Answer: b) 1908 (Madras)

65. The Congress merger and pact with the Muslim League happened during the session in:
    a) 1908 (Madras)
    b) 1916 (Lucknow)
    c) 1917 (Calcutta)
    d) 1920 (Calcutta)
    Answer: b) 1916 (Lucknow)

66. Gandhi attended the INC session in:
    a) 1905 (Varanasi)
    b) 1906 (Calcutta)
    c) 1916 (Lucknow)
    d) 1917 (Calcutta)
    Answer: c) 1916 (Lucknow)

67. Who was the president of the Lucknow session of INC in 1916?
    a) Ras Bihari Ghosh
    b) Ambika Charan Majumdar
    c) Annie Besant
    d) Lala Lajpat Rai
    Answer: b) Ambika Charan Majumdar

68. Who was the first woman President of the Indian National Congress?
    a) Sarojini Naidu
    b) Annie Besant
    c) Kadambini Ganguly
    d) Nellie Sengupta
    Answer: b) Annie Besant

69. Annie Besant became the first woman President of INC in the session of:
    a) 1916 (Lucknow)
    b) 1917 (Calcutta)
    c) 1920 (Calcutta)
    d) 1925 (Kanpur)
    Answer: b) 1917 (Calcutta)

70. The Gandhian programme was adopted and a change in Congress Constitution occurred in the INC session of:
    a) 1917 (Calcutta)
    b) 1920 (Calcutta - suspended)
    c) 1920 (Nagpur - Annual)
    d) 1921 (Ahmedabad)
    Answer: b) 1920 (Calcutta - suspended)

71. Who was the president of the suspended Calcutta session of INC in 1920?
    a) C. Vijayraghava Chariar
    b) Lala Lajpat Rai
    c) C. R. Das
    d) Hakim Ajaml Khan
    Answer: b) Lala Lajpat Rai

72. Hasrat Mohani demanded for complete independence during the INC session in:
    a) 1920 (Calcutta - suspended)
    b) 1920 (Nagpur - Annual)
    c) 1921 (Ahmedabad)
    d) 1924 (Belgaum)
    Answer: b) 1920 (Nagpur - Annual)

73. The formation of the Swaraj Party took place during the INC session in:
    a) 1920 (Nagpur)
    b) 1921 (Ahmedabad)
    c) 1924 (Belgaum)
    d) 1925 (Kanpur)
    Answer: b) 1921 (Ahmedabad)

74. Who was the acting president of the Ahmedabad session of INC in 1921, as C.R. Das was in prison?
    a) Lala Lajpat Rai
    b) Hakim Ajaml Khan
    c) Mahatma Gandhi
    d) Sarojini Naidu
    Answer: b) Hakim Ajaml Khan

75. The only session presided over by Mahatma Gandhi was held in:
    a) 1921 (Ahmedabad)
    b) 1924 (Belgaum)
    c) 1925 (Kanpur)
    d) 1927 (Madras)
    Answer: b) 1924 (Belgaum)

76. Who was the first Indian woman President of the Indian National Congress?
    a) Annie Besant
    b) Sarojini Naidu
    c) Nellie Sengupta
    d) Kadambini Ganguly
    Answer: b) Sarojini Naidu

77. Sarojini Naidu became the first Indian woman President of INC in the session of:
    a) 1924 (Belgaum)
    b) 1925 (Kanpur)
    c) 1927 (Madras)
    d) 1928 (Calcutta)
    Answer: b) 1925 (Kanpur)

78. Nehru and S.C. Bose moved a resolution for independence, and it was passed for the first time, in the INC session of:
    a) 1925 (Kanpur)
    b) 1927 (Madras)
    c) 1928 (Calcutta)
    d) 1929 (Lahore)
    Answer: b) 1927 (Madras)

79. Who was the president of the Madras session of INC in 1927?
    a) Sarojini Naidu
    b) M. A. Ansari
    c) Motilal Nehru
    d) Jawaharlal Nehru
    Answer: b) M. A. Ansari

80. The First All India Youth Congress was held during the INC session in:
    a) 1927 (Madras)
    b) 1928 (Calcutta)
    c) 1929 (Lahore)
    d) 1931 (Karachi)
    Answer: b) 1928 (Calcutta)

81. Who was the president of the Calcutta session of INC in 1928?
    a) M. A. Ansari
    b) Motilal Nehru
    c) Jawaharlal Nehru
    d) Vallabbhai Patel
    Answer: b) Motilal Nehru

82. The Poorna Swaraj Resolution and pledge for Independence Day on 26 January 1930 were adopted in the INC session of:
    a) 1928 (Calcutta)
    b) 1929 (Lahore)
    c) 1931 (Karachi)
    d) 1934 (Bombay)
    Answer: b) 1929 (Lahore)

83. Who was the president of the Lahore session of INC in 1929?
    a) Motilal Nehru
    b) Jawaharlal Nehru
    c) Vallabbhai Patel
    d) Rajendra Prasad
    Answer: b) Jawaharlal Nehru

84. The Resolution for Fundamental Rights and National Economic Policy was passed in the INC session of:
    a) 1929 (Lahore)
    b) 1931 (Karachi)
    c) 1934 (Bombay)
    d) 1936 (Lucknow)
    Answer: b) 1931 (Karachi)

85. Who was the president of the Karachi session of INC in 1931?
    a) Jawaharlal Nehru
    b) Vallabbhai Patel
    c) Rajendra Prasad
    d) Subhash Chandra Bose
    Answer: b) Vallabbhai Patel

86. The formation of the Congress Socialist Party occurred during the INC session in:
    a) 1931 (Karachi)
    b) 1934 (Bombay)
    c) 1936 (Lucknow)
    d) 1937 (Faizpur)
    Answer: b) 1934 (Bombay)

87. Who was the president of the Bombay session of INC in 1934?
    a) Vallabbhai Patel
    b) Rajendra Prasad
    c) Jawaharlal Nehru
    d) Subhash Chandra Bose
    Answer: b) Rajendra Prasad

88. Support for socialism through democracy was a key outcome of the INC session in:
    a) 1934 (Bombay)
    b) 1936 (Lucknow)
    c) 1937 (Faizpur)
    d) 1938 (Haripura)
    Answer: b) 1936 (Lucknow)

89. Who was the president of the Lucknow session of INC in 1936?
    a) Rajendra Prasad
    b) Jawaharlal Nehru
    c) Subhash Chandra Bose
    d) Pattabhi Sitarammayya
    Answer: b) Jawaharlal Nehru

90. The demand for a Constituent Assembly was made during the INC session in:
    a) 1936 (Lucknow)
    b) 1937 (Faizpur)
    c) 1938 (Haripura)
    d) 1939 (Tripuri)
    Answer: b) 1937 (Faizpur)

91. Who was the president of the Faizpur session of INC in 1937?
    a) Rajendra Prasad
    b) Jawaharlal Nehru
    c) Subhash Chandra Bose
    d) Pattabhi Sitarammayya
    Answer: b) Jawaharlal Nehru

92. Poorna Swaraj was declared to cover also princely states during the INC session in:
    a) 1937 (Faizpur)
    b) 1938 (Haripura)
    c) 1939 (Tripuri)
    d) 1948 (Jaipur)
    Answer: b) 1938 (Haripura)

93. Who was the president of the Haripura session of INC in 1938?
    a) Jawaharlal Nehru
    b) Subhash Chandra Bose
    c) Rajendra Prasad
    d) Pattabhi Sitarammayya
    Answer: b) Subhash Chandra Bose

94. Subhash Chandra Bose resigned due to differences with Gandhi in the INC session of:
    a) 1937 (Faizpur)
    b) 1938 (Haripura)
    c) 1939 (Tripuri)
    d) 1948 (Jaipur)
    Answer: c) 1939 (Tripuri)

95. Who became president after Subhash Chandra Bose resigned from the Tripuri session of INC in 1939?
    a) Jawaharlal Nehru
    b) Vallabbhai Patel
    c) Rajendra Prasad
    d) Pattabhi Sitarammayya
    Answer: c) Rajendra Prasad

96. Who was the president of the Jaipur session of INC in 1948?
    a) Rajendra Prasad
    b) Subhash Chandra Bose
    c) Pattabhi Sitarammayya
    d) Jawaharlal Nehru
    Answer: c) Pattabhi Sitarammayya
